# Changelog

Journal des changements notables d'`OpenAether-apps` (manifests communs + wiring Flux).

Format inspiré de [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/).
Les changements d'infrastructure (OpenTofu, Talos, providers) vivent dans le
`CHANGELOG.md` d'`OpenAether-infra` — beaucoup de chantiers touchent les deux
dépôts, les entrées se répondent alors.

Ce fichier démarre au 2026-07-27 : les 200 commits antérieurs ne sont pas
rétro-documentés, l'historique git faisant foi. Les décisions structurantes de
cette période étaient consignées dans `OpenAether-infra/docs/backlog.md`, retiré
depuis (#86 côté infra) : ses tâches sont des issues GitHub, ses pièges sont dans
`docs/traps.md`.

---

## [Unreleased]

## [0.4.5] — 2026-11-11

0.4.5 is a **hotfix release** of 0.4.4 on one theme, the supply chain (no feature of its own), paired with **OpenAether-infra 0.4.5**, which re-pins this tag and carries no code change. It puts the two image sets that the weekly scan never saw (the Barman Cloud plugin sidecar, and the Flux copy pushed to CAPI children) into the scan, moves them and Longhorn to builds that a local scan of 2026-11-09 and 2026-11-10 found without CRITICAL (**CVE-2026-123158, published on 2026-11-05 and read by the scanner from 2026-11-10 at the latest, with no fixed image yet, keeps three images of the matrix red: `forgejo`, `restic` and the Flux copy's `kustomize-controller`; "Known open", first item**), deletes one waiver and renews the other to a dated limit, declares Flux's `Alert` and `Provider` at `v1`, and makes the children's Cilium restart one agent at a time. **The price is paid at the move: the move from 0.4.4 restarts every PostgreSQL pod once, and the two platform Clusters roll together** ("Upgrade from 0.4.4"). Durations are minutes:seconds. **Rung words:** OFFLINE = mocked or offline tests and checkers green on a copy of the head (rendered manifests, fixtures, stubs, the repository's own scripts); LOCAL SCAN = Trivy 0.69.3 (the CI version and flags) on a workstation, CRITICAL and HIGH, database of 2026-11-09 and 2026-11-10 (the full replay of the 59 images and the controller reads of 2026-11-12: database of 2026-11-11 19:04 UTC): not a cluster read and not the Actions scan; STATIC = a reading of sources, nothing run; LAB = a read on a cluster of one of the four runs of "Cloud proof": Scaleway, ONE run per figure (n=1), on a MOVING branch ref, never a tag (except the installs of the released 0.4.4 tags, the baselines). Durations are NOMINAL unless marked INCIDENT (INCIDENT-absorbed: the known load-balancer ACL trap, retried inside ONE `cluster-up`; INCIDENT-by-design: the documented convergence of a CAPI child, or a fault injected on purpose). WORSE = at least 10 s AND at least 10 % worse than a baseline re-read from an earlier run, against every earlier run of the same shape (for counts, the 10 % line alone); BETTER only without INCIDENT time, with the whole window observed and a like-for-like baseline, or, said where the word is used, for a measured count or a property of the design (two rows). Not a compliance claim. What this release is:

- **Security: what the scan sees, and what it finds (apps #219, #215, #217; LOCAL SCAN).** The scan matrix goes from 51 to **59** images (24 without `--helm`): the Barman Cloud plugin sidecar, which sits in every PostgreSQL pod and was outside the matrix, and the seven controller images of the Flux copy that CAPI children receive. Sidecar `v0.15.1` read **2 CRITICAL** (certifi, GHSA-6q48-6c7w-9gw2) and `v0.16.0` reads **0**; the children's `kustomize-controller:v1.9.6` read **1 CRITICAL** (brotli-libs CVE-2026-111497, in the CISA KEV catalog since 2026-10-27) and the 2.10.0 set reads **0**; Longhorn's `csi-provisioner:v5.3.0` (the image of the 0.4.4 `[vuln]` issue) read **2 CRITICAL** and its replacement `v5.3.0-20261028` reads **0**. **BETTER, as a measured count at the LOCAL SCAN rung only.** **The matrix is nevertheless NOT green:** a CVE published on 2026-11-05 that the scanner read from 2026-11-10 at the latest, after those reads, CVE-2026-123158 (OpenSSH, CRITICAL with a fix in the Alpine package: 10.3_p1-r2 on Alpine 3.24 for `restic` and the controller, 10.2_p1-r1 on Alpine 3.23 for `forgejo`; no fixed image upstream on 2026-11-12), reads 3 CRITICAL rows each on `forgejo:15.0.11-rootless`, `restic/restic:0.19.1` and the children's `kustomize-controller:v1.10.1`; a full local replay of the 59 images on 2026-11-12 reads those three and the other 56 clean ("Known open", first item). The Actions scan of the 59 images has not been seen (it runs at the tag push); reachability of the removed findings was never assessed.
- **Install and upgrade: every PostgreSQL pod restarts once (LAB, n=1).** The plugin's sidecar image changes, the sidecar is an init container of every instance pod, so CloudNativePG rolls all six pods of the two platform Clusters: replicas first, ONE planned switchover per Cluster, the old primary last, **both Clusters together**. No acknowledged commit was lost (127,604 ids checked on three stages, 0 missing) and no run saw two primaries; write interruption per Cluster was 14.73 and 14.42 s in a quiet restart and **28.15 s (plus a second gap of 3.09 s, 32.29 s combined) and 22.06 s** in the move, where the Longhorn upgrade ran on the same nodes, against the 35 s target (2.71 s of margin on the combined span; WORSE against the previous release's 14 to 16 s, W2). Said plainly here and in the notes.
- **Waivers (apps #199; OFFLINE, LOCAL SCAN).** The Longhorn Trivy ignore entry (libexpat) is deleted, the file stays empty; the restic VEX statement is re-read and **renewed to 2027-01-09, not fixed** (no fixed restic image exists). `check-vex.py` exits 0 on 2026-12-05, 2027-01-08 and 2027-01-09 and 1 on 2027-01-10.
- **Changed (LAB, n=1).** Longhorn 1.12.1 to 1.12.2 with its CSI sidecars (the upstream fix for acknowledged writes lost during a replica rebuild lives in the ENGINE image: a volume has it only after its engine image is upgraded); `Alert` and `Provider` at `notification.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v1`; the children's Cilium agent and envoy DaemonSets at `maxUnavailable: 1` (H7): the Helm upgrade of a live child restarted no pod, and a later rollout takes about twice as long (11.3 s to 22.4 s for the agents) with at most one agent out at a time (BETTER by design only, W7).
- **Evidence, and its limit.** Heads **apps b70c90a61b175618d920e7b6a98ee65120e6ecbd** (the first five commits, independently reviewed) and **0064d2e00cb639f7a18973159e3a94df23a105a2** (the final head: 11 signed commits on the 0.4.4 tag, 38 files, 1,214 insertions, 214 deletions), each plus one lab-only commit that adds a child cluster definition (3 files, not part of the release), on MOVING branch refs, never on a tag. The infrastructure code ran at the 0.4.4 tag (`ba685ea578ee0a8860652777dae6c046c5aeb971`) in every run: **the infrastructure 0.4.5 cut was never run on a cluster**. **What is released is the proven final head plus the cut:** the apps tag differs from 0064d2e by this entry, `README.md`, `README.fr.md`, the three documentation pages of the cut commit ("Documented", last bullet) and the comment lines above the H7 values in the four child cluster files (the parsed YAML of each file is identical to the proven head; no manifest value, script, image, workflow or `.vex` file changed; the diff is stated again in the release notes, and this entry is corrected if it differs). The date of this heading, 2026-11-11, is the day of the cut, not of a tag: it is provisional until the tag is pushed. **A move or an install pinned to the tag 0.4.5 is NOT proven.** Nine WORSE groups are listed under "Cloud proof"; the owner accepted them on 2026-11-11, each carried as "cause not established". None is a data-safety regression, none is claimed as a product regression, none is hidden.

### Cloud proof of the 0.4.5 heads (four runs on 2026-11-10 and 2026-11-11; Scaleway fr-par, 3 CP + 6 W POP2-4C-16G + bastion; n=1)

**Shape.** Management: Talos v1.14.4, Kubernetes v1.37.1, forge OFF (the two platform PostgreSQL Clusters only: `zitadel-db` PostgreSQL 18, `grafana-db` PostgreSQL 16), CloudNativePG operator 1.30.1, Flux 2.10.0 (seven controllers equal to `flux-image-digests.lock`). Child: 1 control plane + 1 worker DEV1-L, Talos v1.13.9, Kubernetes v1.35.3, minimal profile, ONE template, Cilium chart 1.19.9. Each cluster was torn down after its run. Baselines: the earlier proof runs of the 0.4.x line (the numbers are quoted where they are compared) and the installs and the move with no product change that the same runs made at the released 0.4.4 tags. **Heads:** the move, the Longhorn hop, the plugin roll, the PostgreSQL restart with writers, the quiet restart, the backup path, `Alert` / `Provider` v1, the fresh install of the candidate and the fresh child ran on b70c90a plus the lab-only commit; the in-place child move, H7 and a second PostgreSQL restart (no writers) ran on 0064d2e plus the same commit. The two heads render an identical `cnpg` brick (`kubectl kustomize apps/base/cnpg`, empty diff), differ in comments only under `apps/base` and `apps/components`, and the H7 values exist only on the final head.

- **The move (b70c90a; NOMINAL by label, host load mean 6.55, n=1).** A cluster installed at the released 0.4.4 tags, moved once to a lab branch with no product change (the baseline move), then to the candidate in ONE `cluster-up`, under writers (an acknowledged-commit ledger per Cluster, pinned writers, a split-brain reader), probes and two Longhorn canary volumes. `PLAN_ONLY` 2:03, `0 to add, 3 to change, 0 to destroy`; `cluster-up` **12:29** (WORSE, W1); own verify 30 / 0 / 0 in 80 s; 42 of 45 Kustomizations Ready 4:15 after the ref read-back (the three child Kustomizations of the lab-only commit make up the 45). Longhorn HelmRelease Progressing to `UpgradeSucceeded` **1:18**. Plugin Deployment rolled in **15 s** (the Secret has a hashed name, so a new image is a new Secret and a new ReplicaSet; at 1 s polling the Endpoints held the old pod only, then the new pods only, so no second version served beside the first). PostgreSQL: all six pods restarted (replicas first, one switchover per Cluster, both Clusters together); roll `zitadel-db` 209.9 s, `grafana-db` 204.0 s (W3); write gap `zitadel-db` 28.15 s + 3.09 s, `grafana-db` 22.06 s (W2); `cnpg` Ready 20 s after Progressing, at 09:01:42; the closing verify (30 / 0 / 0, 09:00:39 to 09:01:59) ended with `cluster-up`, which returned at 09:01:59: **both before the databases were whole** (healthy 09:05:07 and 09:05:13, 3:08 to 3:14 after rc 0; only the last 16 s of the verify overlapped the first replica recreations at 09:01:43, and the Clusters ran 2 of 3 instances for 2:30 and 2:37 from the demotions at 09:02:37 and 09:02:41, after the verify had ended). **47,032 acknowledged ids, 0 missing** (the last about 2 s of the direct writers' acknowledgements were not closed in this stage; the two stages below closed theirs); **0 s with two primaries at 1 s sampling (about 4 s unobserved per reader)**; pinned writers 0 acknowledgements after the promotion on 2 of 2; no primary for 11 and 12 s (a near-miss, listed with the WORSE rows). Probes at 1 Hz: echo 0 of 1,869 failed, API 0 of 1,871, Grafana `/api/health` 17 failures (all HTTP 503, 17 s, at the `grafana-db` switchover); the Zitadel login probe was NOT RUN (no SSO in these runs). Alerts over 35 minutes: only Watchdog and the five lab-induced `FluxObjectNotReady` fired; `CnpgInstanceDown`, `CnpgInstanceMissing`, `CnpgReplicaNotStreaming`, `PdbBlocksDisruption`, `PodPendingTooLong` and others went PENDING only. 9 of 9 node boot IDs identical (no node rolled).
- **Longhorn on the move cluster (b70c90a; n=1).** With the forge OFF the platform has **no Longhorn volume** (all 16 PVCs are `local-path` or `local-path-retain`), so the hop was read on two lab volumes of two replicas: `lh-canary`, attached, with two loops writing a short `fsync`ed line (id acknowledged outside the cluster, about 9 acknowledgements a second: the small-write pattern of the upstream fix), and `lh-idle`, detached. The manager DaemonSet was replaced on all nodes together; six CSI sidecars changed image (Ready 09:04:23 to 09:04:57; their pull durations are not archived); engine image `ei-493e04e7` (v1.12.1) to `ei-5830480e` (v1.12.2) deployed; support-bundle-kit v1.0.5. **The attached canary stayed `healthy` throughout, no rebuild, no attach error, max write gap 0.27 s, 20,470 and 20,473 acknowledged, 0 missing.** Three idle workers had their v1.12.1 instance-manager pod deleted at 09:02:50 to 09:02:56 and the v1.12.2 ones Running at 09:04:20 to 09:05:00: **no instance manager there for 1:25 to 2:10** (no effect, nothing ran there; an attach on such a node in that window is impossible). **Engine upgrade by hand (what delivers the fix):** live on the attached `lh-canary` **9.3 s**, healthy on every poll, no rebuild, ledger 0 missing, max gap 0.25 s; offline on the detached `lh-idle` **2.4 s**, checksum identical after the re-attach. One replica deleted on purpose under the new engine (INCIDENT-by-design): healthy again 35.1 s later, 0 missing: **a pass of ONE rebuild, not a test of the upstream race.** The old engine image was removed by Longhorn itself 1 h 00 min 26 s after its last reference; **the canary's engine process stayed in the OLD instance-manager pod 68 minutes after the live upgrade** (the old pod stays while an attached volume's engine process runs in it; detaching frees it: not run). 17 manager `error` lines, all between 09:02:24 and 09:09:41, none about the attached volume; none from 09:10:00 to 10:15:40.
- **The same restart, quiet (b70c90a; NOMINAL, host load 1.9, no image to pull: 23 of 23 `Pulled` were "already present"; n=1).** `kubectl cnpg restart` of each Cluster in turn: replicas 12.0 and 12.1 s each; request to promotion **5.90 s** (`zitadel-db`) and **5.12 s** (`grafana-db`) against 11.75 and 12.87 s in the move; roll **58.56 s** and **63.16 s**; **write gap 14.73 s and 14.42 s** (margin to the 35 s target 20.3 and 20.6 s; about 9 s of the `zitadel-db` gap come after the promotion, a 0.5.0 item): SAME as the previous release's 14.1 to 15.5 s. 14,422 acknowledged ids, 0 missing, tail closed; 0 s with two primaries; pinned writers 0 acknowledgements after the promotion on 2 of 2; probes: echo 0 of 742, API 0 of 744, Grafana 9 failures. **ContinuousArchiving read False for 1.26 s (`zitadel-db`) and 1.30 s (`grafana-db`) at each promotion** (the new primary's log: `failed to run wal-archive command: switchover in progress, refusing archiving`; `00000003.history` archived about 2 s later; `LastBackupSucceeded` True; no segment missing): expected, a documentation line; the same sampling on the 0.4.4 plugin was NOT RUN. A `kubectl cnpg restart` leaves a `restartedAt` annotation that Flux removes within minutes without a second roll.
- **The backup path on the new sidecar (b70c90a; NOMINAL; n=1; writers running, then stopped).** Manual `kubectl cnpg backup --method plugin`: **13 s** (`zitadel-db`) and **18 s** (`grafana-db`), `pluginMetadata.version` 0.16.0 (manual Backups on the 0.4.4 plugin: 22 and 19 s in the baseline install, 17 and 14 s in an earlier run: SAME or lower). `latest` restore by hand of the new base backups: 70 and 68 s (primary store), 69 and 72 s (replica store); **205,778 id checks over four restores, 0 missing** (checks, not distinct ids). **The product's restore checkers, template unchanged: PITR PASS x4** (both Clusters, both stores, PostgreSQL 18 and 16) in **83 / 72 / 78 / 67 s**, replaying backups written by v0.15.1 with the v0.16.0 restore side (closes the 0.4.4 item "PITR on PostgreSQL 16 and on the replica stores not proven" at this rung); `latest` PASS x4 in 78 / 78 / 72 / 72 s, then twice more with writers stopped, PASS x8 (WORSE, W5); **PITR over a base backup made after the move PASS x4** in 67 / 72 / 72 / 67 s (target on timeline 2, before the two timeline-3 forks: no timeline switch is exercised). WAL archive MISSING 0 on all four stores on every timeline at every read. 66,150 acknowledged ids in that stage, 0 missing, tail closed. **The first SCHEDULED Backup on v0.16.0 was NOT run** (the ScheduledBackups fire daily at 03:10 and 03:25 UTC; only manual Backups ran). Not product claims: the sidecar logs an error-level `While terminating server` (empty `error`) at scratch-pod and old-primary stops; `kubectl cnpg status` could not read the instances ("unable to handle the request"); every restore ran on one worker.
- **`Alert` / `Provider` at v1 (b70c90a; NOMINAL; n=1).** The CRDs read `storedVersions ["v1"]` before and after the move (a fresh 0.4.4 install already stores v1); both objects read back at v1; **neither kind has a `status` or a Ready condition**, so Ready is read on the owning Kustomization `flux-egress` (True; its inventory `v1beta3` before, `v1` after). **One real error event reached Alertmanager in 5 s** (a throwaway Kustomization on a missing path): **not rated BETTER**, because dispatch to `startsAt` is 0 to 1 s either way and the 121 s read before the move was the object's first error event, not the platform path.
- **The install of the candidate and a fresh child (b70c90a; INCIDENT-absorbed for the install; INCIDENT-by-design for the child; n=1).** Install: T0 to rc 0 **13:08** (the ACL trap 0:50 absorbed in ONE invocation; image build 43 s); own verify 24 / 0 / 0, standalone verify 30 / 0 / 0; day-1 2:19; databases 3 of 3 at rc 0 + 7:30 (`grafana-db`) and 7:36 (`zitadel-db`); 42 of 42 Kustomizations at rc 0 + 11:07 (INCIDENT: the known `observability-vm-customresources` fault, 2:03, inside); restarts 24 main + 0 init (W6, W9). **Child** (the two provider Secrets were created by the owner in the owner's own terminal): `Cluster` created 3:55 after the Secrets (the first attempts waited for the providers, `retryInterval` 2 m); child API 1:52 after creation; Cluster Available 3:44 after creation; the child's own Flux and its five profile Kustomizations Ready **9:04 after the Secrets**; **seven Flux controllers on 2.10.0, spec image AND imageID equal to the lock, 0 restarts**; 15 `fluxcd.io` CRDs Established, `alerts` and `providers` stored at `v1`; **890 `/readyz` samples at 1 Hz over 15 minutes, 0 failed, longest gap 1.03 s**; the child's kube-system restarts: 4 (the two Talos static pods, exit 1 at start-up, before the nodes were Ready). Deleted through CAPI: machines gone in 2:33; no server, load balancer, flexible IP, volume, gateway or private network left. **This meets the apps #215 criterion "a child built from it runs Flux 2.10" at this rung.** Scope: ONE Scaleway template, Talos v1.13.9 and Kubernetes v1.35.3 against the management's v1.14.4 and v1.37.1 (the child's Talos image is not built by the install: it pre-existed in the account), minimal profile, moving ref; not OVH, not Outscale, not a larger child.
- **The first IN-PLACE move of an existing child, 2.9.6 to 2.10.0 (0064d2e; NOMINAL; n=1; a 2-node child).** The child was built at the 0.4.4 arm (all five profile Kustomizations Ready 12:14 after the Secrets: W8; Flux 2.9.6 set, `alerts` and `providers` stored and served `v1beta3` only), then the management ref moved to the final head. The management's Kustomization that applies the Flux copy to the child reconciled in **3.25 s** (11 CRDs, ClusterRole, 4 Services, 7 Deployments configured); **7 of 7 controllers on the 2.10.0 set 23 s after the ref read-back**, spec image and imageID equal to the lock, **0 restarts**, **no CRD update refused** (15 Established); the child's GitRepository Unknown for 6 s; the five profile Kustomizations stayed Ready. No error-level controller log line (read once, 7 min 13 s after the apply). **FINDING: `alerts` and `providers` carry `storedVersions [v1beta3, v1]` after the move** (a fresh child has `[v1]`): the API server will REFUSE a later Flux release that stops serving `v1beta3` until the stored objects are migrated (re-apply or a storage-version migration, then patch `storedVersions`); the migration was NOT run. The child's own Flux stayed on the arm branch (`CHILD_REF`), so the candidate's v1 declaration through a child's own Flux, and delivery to an Alertmanager, were NOT RUN. No baseline (the first in-place move).
- **H7, the children's Cilium one agent at a time (0064d2e; NOMINAL; n=1; ONE 2-node child).** **The strategy-only Helm upgrade reached a LIVE child and restarted no pod:** HelmRelease Progressing 21:47:00, `UpgradeSucceeded` 21:47:06 (the 6 s is the 3 s sampling grid; the status gives about 2 s), Helm revision 1 to 2, `maxUnavailable` 2 to 1 on `ds/cilium` and `ds/cilium-envoy`, the same four pods with the same start times, 0 restarts; over the whole window the child API answered 839 of 839 samples (longest gap 1.07 s) and in-child 854 of 854. The 2026-07-26 degradation recorded in the infrastructure `docs/admin-access.md` did not materialise for a strategy-only change. **Before and after on the SAME child** (restart at the default `maxUnavailable 2`, then after the move): agents unavailable together **2 (10 s) to 1 (21 s)**, envoy 2 (6 s) to 1 (13 s); rollout complete **11.3 s to 22.4 s** (+11.1 s, +98 %, W7), envoy 9.1 s to 17.9 s; **three continuity probes (API 2,337 samples, in-child DNS, in-child TCP) 0 failures in every rung**. **BETTER by design only:** the count of agents out together is `maxUnavailable` working, confirmed per node by a sampler (each agent out 10 to 11 s in both rungs) but NOT like for like (host load 1.1 before against 3.8 to 4.0 after for the agent rung), and the raw pod-level count still reached 2 for 1 s (an old terminating pod and its new pod on the same node); the price is the doubled rollout. **NOT tested:** a values change that alters the pod template or the ConfigMap (it would now roll one agent at a time), a larger child, another provider; the baseline restart had left `restartedAt` on the DaemonSets before the upgrade (the variant without it was not run).
- **The move on the final head, no writers (0064d2e; NOMINAL by label, host load 5.47; n=1).** `PLAN_ONLY` 2:06, `0 to add, 3 to change, 0 to destroy`; `cluster-up` **10:45**, verify 30 / 0 / 0 in 67 s (W1); both PostgreSQL Clusters rolled together again, 184.6 s and 178.5 s (W3); the roll began 4 s after the closing verify ended, and `cnpg` and 45 of 45 were Ready 1:15 BEFORE the roll ended; **write interruption NOT MEASURED (no writer ran)**. Plugin Deployment: new pods Running 4 s after the scale-up, the last old pod gone 14 s after it. The Longhorn HelmRelease deployed (no volume in this run).
- **Static gates (OFFLINE, on a copy of 0064d2e).** `yamllint -c .yamllint apps/ scripts/ .github/workflows/` 0 errors, 2 warnings (`ci.yml` comment spacing); `check-language.sh` clean; `check-vex.py`, `check-eol.py` (37 components, 0 warnings), `check-child-clusters.py` (7 child files) and `check-image-pins.py` OK; gitleaks 8.30.1: 705 commits of history and the 11 commits of the range, no leaks; kubeconform **475 valid, 0 invalid at b70c90a** (472 before the CRD catalog pin moved; not re-run at 0064d2e, where the `apps/base` changes are comments only); the repository's offline suite, 88 scripts: 85 exit 0, and the other three (`test-backup-kyverno.sh`, `test-kyverno-policies.sh`, `test-grafana-auth.sh`) need the Kyverno CLI or `jmespath`, which that host lacked (environment only); the full suite on the cut head is the release gate's, not restated here.
- **The installs at the released 0.4.4 tags and the move with no product change (the baselines; they describe 0.4.4, need no decision of this release, and are listed because nothing is hidden).** Installs at the tags: T0 to rc 0 12:48, 11:30 and 11:22, each INCIDENT-absorbed (the ACL trap, 0:48 to 0:53) and each WORSE against the oldest runs (+11 % to +29 %, the carry of new buckets explaining part); day-1 2:56, 2:11 and 2:54 (each WORSE against at least one earlier figure of 1:22 to 2:38); `cnpg` Progressing at rc 0 + 2:10, 1:36 and 1:46 (WORSE against earlier runs); `cnpg` Ready at rc 0 + 8:11 in the first (WORSE, +51 s, +11.6 %); databases 3 of 3 at rc 0 + 8:05 and 8:12, then 7:29, then 7:40 and 8:05, against 6:53 and 7:19 in one earlier run (WORSE in the first and the third install, +46 to +72 s, +10.5 % to +17.4 %, the last one at the edge; SAME in the second); the `cnpg` gate 6m10s in the third (WORSE against 5m31s and 5m24s, +40 s, +12.0 % and +46 s, +14 %, an INCIDENT window inside the second comparator), SAME in the first two; 42 of 42 at rc 0 + 11:26, 10:45 and 11:26, INCIDENT (the same 2:02 fault); the HTTP probe window 70 in 1:10, 76 in 1:16 and **148 in 2:28** (the start not observed; cause not isolated); restarts 21, 22 and 10 (the same code spread 10 to 22). The move with no product change took 6:54 (SAME against 6:41 and 7:06) and 7:50 (WORSE by +10.3 % to +17.2 % against 6:41, 6:54 and 7:06); its 42 original Kustomizations were on target 4:05 to 4:10 after the ref switch (WORSE, +25 to +36 s, +11 % to +17 % against 3:34 to 3:40). `observability-vm-customresources` failed for about 2 minutes in all four installs of these runs, the candidate's included (apps #193: four more sightings).
- **WORSE rows (accepted by the owner on 2026-11-11, each carried as "cause not established"; none is a data-safety regression).**
  1. **W1. The move wall and its segments (LOAD-CONFOUNDED; NOMINAL by label).** Host load mean 6.55 in the move and 5.47 on the final head, against 2.06 to 2.42 in the moves with no product change. Move: `cluster-up` 12:29 (749 s) against nine earlier runs of the same shape (6:41 to 13:05): WORSE against eight (6:41 to 11:00; +13.5 % to +87 %), SAME against the ninth (13:05); launch to ref read-back 9:44 (+171 to +270 s against six); ref read-back to rc 0 2:45 (+44 to +78 s against eight); verify 80 s against 45 s (+35 s, +78 %); `PLAN_ONLY` 2:03 against 1:31 (+32 s, +35.2 %) and against 1:41, 1:45, 1:50, 1:47 (+22, +18, +13, +16 s); 42 of 45 on target 4:15 after the read-back against 3:34 to 3:40 (+35 to +41 s) and 3:49 (+26 s). The sidecar is `ghcr.io/cloudnative-pg/plugin-barman-cloud-sidecar:v0.16.0`. Plugin PR 1105 (every replica serves) is in this release; the leader-election flag stays OFF on purpose. **LOCAL SCAN:** the sidecar `v0.15.1` read 2 CRITICAL (both GHSA-6q48-6c7w-9gw2, `certifi` 2026.7.22 and 2026.6.17, fixed in 2026.10.18) and 56 HIGH (23 with a fix); `v0.16.0` reads **0 CRITICAL** and 38 HIGH (5 with a fix, 33 without): BETTER as a count, reachability not established. **OFFLINE:** `test-cnpg-barman-plugin.py` on the render (the `--leader-elect` removal still lands; the rotation-policy guard reads the render; mutation-checked), `test-sbom.py`, `test-check-vendored-lock.sh`. **LAB (n=1):** the rollout, the restart of every PostgreSQL pod and the backup path are in "Cloud proof" and "Upgrade from 0.4.4"; the operator logged only info lines (no error-level line from 09:01:00 to 09:06:30 on the move) and the plugin certificates were not re-issued. **Limits:** the first scheduled Backup is NOT run; the Actions scan that lists the sidecar is read at the tag push.
- **The Flux copy pushed to CAPI children goes from 2.9.6 to 2.10.0 (apps #215).** `apps/base/cluster-api-clusters/flux-gotk` is re-vendored (`Flux Version: v2.10.0`; the 2.10 minimums: Kubernetes 1.35 from 1.35.2, 1.36 from 1.36.1, 1.37 from 1.37.0) and the lock, the EOL entry (pin 2.10.0) and the Renovate path follow. **LOCAL SCAN:** `kustomize-controller:v1.9.6` read **1 CRITICAL** (brotli-libs 1.2.0-r0, CVE-2026-111497, fixed in 1.2.1-r0, CISA KEV since 2026-10-27) and 25 HIGH; the seven 2.10.0 images read **0 CRITICAL** on the database of 2026-11-09 and 2026-11-10 (HIGH: `kustomize-controller:v1.10.1` 8, four of them libexpat 2.9.0-r0, the others 1 each): BETTER as a count; **on the database of 2026-11-11 `kustomize-controller:v1.10.1` reads 3 CRITICAL rows of CVE-2026-123158 (4 rows, 2 CVEs, for `v1.9.6`), the other six controllers 0** ("Known open", first item). **LAB (n=1):** a fresh child runs it (7 of 7 equal to the lock, 0 restarts) and an existing child moved in place (7 of 7 in 23 s, 0 restarts, no CRD refused): "Cloud proof". **Limits:** the apps copy pins TAGS, not digests (the run compared the running image IDs with the infrastructure lock); one template, one provider, 1 CP + 1 W; the `storedVersions` finding.

The PyJWT statement left in 0.4.4. No cluster was used. The next renewal is due before 2027-01-09.

### Changed (0.4.5; rung: OFFLINE, rendered and tested, plus LAB reads (n=1, moving branch refs) where a bullet says so)

- **Longhorn 1.12.1 to 1.12.2 (apps #217, #199).** Chart pin; the CSI sidecars to the `-20261028` builds (attacher v4.13.0, provisioner v5.3.0, resizer v2.3.0, snapshotter v8.7.0, registrar v2.18.0, livenessprobe v2.20.0) and support-bundle-kit 0.0.92 to 1.0.5; a patch hop within the minor. The release fixes data loss during replica rebuilds with concurrent unaligned writes (upstream Longhorn issues 14451 and 14447: concurrent writes under 4 KiB to one sector). **The fix lives in the engine image, so a volume has it only after its engine image is upgraded** (`docs/patching.md`, step 6; the automatic engine upgrade is OFF by default here). OFFLINE: `test-longhorn-values.py`. **LAB (n=1, two lab volumes, "Cloud proof"):** an attached volume kept serving through the hop (max write gap 0.27 s); idle nodes had no instance manager for 1:25 to 2:10. **Delivered, not shown:** the engine fix was not tested against its race (no baseline on the old engine; one rebuild passed). With the forge OFF the platform has no Longhorn volume, so the hop matters with the forge or an opt-in component.
- **`Alert` and `Provider` at `notification.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v1` (apps #214).** `apps/base/flux/notification-alertmanager.yaml` from `v1beta3` to `v1`; no `notification.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v1beta3` is left in the tree outside the vendored copy (a grep, 0 hits). It needs Flux 2.10 (infrastructure 0.4.3 or later); a cluster still on Flux 2.9.6 cannot apply `v1` (expected, not run). A management cluster moved in place to Flux 2.10 stores `[v1beta3, v1]`, as a moved child does ("Upgrade from 0.4.4"). OFFLINE: `test-flux-notifications.py`; kubeconform against the 2.10 CRDs 475 valid, 0 invalid. LAB (n=1): "Cloud proof". The Flux `Provider` still posts to ONE Alertmanager pod (apps #218, unchanged).
- **The children's Cilium restarts one agent at a time (H7; owner decision of 2026-11-11).** `updateStrategy.rollingUpdate.maxUnavailable: 1` on the agent DaemonSet and, under `envoy.updateStrategy`, on the envoy DaemonSet, in the `<child>-cilium` values of `apps/clusters/edge-1.yaml`, `edge-2.yaml`, `edge-3.yaml` and `example-scaleway.yaml.example` (the chart default is 2; `helm template` before and after differs by exactly the two `maxUnavailable` lines, the pod templates unchanged; the three `edge-N` entries are commented out of `apps/clusters/kustomization.yaml`, so they render into nothing until a child is enabled). `scripts/check-child-clusters.py` asserts both keys in every child file and `test-check-child-clusters.sh` has a mutation for each: a child file of your own that lacks either key fails the checker, and so the "Child clusters" step of `ci.yml` ("Upgrade from 0.4.4"). OFFLINE. **LAB (n=1, one 2-node child, NOMINAL):** the Helm upgrade restarted no pod; a later rollout is one agent at a time and about twice as long ("Cloud proof", W7); BETTER by design only. **Limits:** a values change that alters the pod template was not tested; one provider, one child shape.

### Upgrade from 0.4.4 (rung: LAB for the roll, the move and the reads below (n=1, NOMINAL by label, 3 CP + 6 W, moving branch refs, not a tag); OFFLINE or STATIC where a bullet says so)

**Path.** Supported before 1.0.0: the previous minor only, **0.4.4 to 0.4.5**, paired with **OpenAether-infra 0.4.5**. Apps is tagged first and the move is the apps-ref move of the infrastructure repository (`task cluster-up` with `git_ref = "refs/tags/0.4.5"`; the steps are in the OpenAether-infra 0.4.5 notes and CHANGELOG entry). The Flux controllers of the management do not move (Flux 2.10.0, unchanged since infrastructure 0.4.3). **What the runs ran:** the real move from the released 0.4.4 tags (plus the lab-only commit) to the candidate in ONE `cluster-up` on a MOVING branch ref, twice (on b70c90a with writers, on 0064d2e without); both plans read `0 to add, 3 to change, 0 to destroy` (the three control planes' machine configurations in place, because the Talos inline manifest carries `git_ref`) and the 9 of 9 node boot IDs were identical afterwards (no node rolled). **A move pinned to `refs/tags/0.4.5` was NOT run.** What follows is what the first sync does to a running cluster, in the order of the risk it carries.

- **Every PostgreSQL pod restarts once, and the two platform Clusters roll together.** The Barman Cloud plugin's sidecar image changes (v0.15.1 to v0.16.0); the sidecar is an init container of every instance pod, so CloudNativePG rolls every Cluster that archives through the plugin (only the two platform Clusters existed in the runs; the forge-ON shape, with `forgejo-db`, was NOT run). Order, per Cluster: replicas first, then ONE planned switchover, the old primary last; the operator has one rollout slot per process and no delay between Clusters, so **both Clusters roll at the same time**. Nothing to run or approve. **What it costs (LAB, n=1):**

  | | Move on b70c90a (writers, Longhorn upgrade running, load 6.55) | Quiet restart (nothing to pull, load 1.9) | Move on 0064d2e (no writer, load 5.47) | The previous release's roll (earlier runs) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `zitadel-db` (PG18, 3): roll; write gap | 209.9 s; 28.15 s + 3.09 s (32.29 s combined) | 58.56 s; 14.73 s | 184.6 s; not measured | 92.8 / 91.6 s; 14.91 / 14.97 s |
  | `grafana-db` (PG16, 3): roll; write gap | 204.0 s; 22.06 s | 63.16 s; 14.42 s | 178.5 s; not measured | 89.7 / 96.2 s; 15.48 / 14.13 s |

  **In words: about 15 s of write interruption per Cluster in a quiet restart; 28.15 s (32.29 s with its second gap) and 22.06 s measured once in the move, while Longhorn replaced its images on the same nodes, against the 35 s target (2.71 s of margin on the combined span; one run, no tighter bound is claimed).** Each roll lasts 1:00 quiet and about 3:00 to 3:30 in the move, most likely because the 107 MB sidecar image waits in the kubelet's pull queue behind the Longhorn images (the sidecar pull events read 1:54 to 2:03 "including waiting" on the old primaries' nodes, against 6 to 9 s for the replicas in the same move; the Longhorn pull durations are not archived; cause not established). 0 acknowledged ids missing (127,604 checked on three stages; in the move stage the last about 2 s of the direct writers' acknowledgements were not closed, in the two others they were), two primaries never seen (1 s sampling). ContinuousArchiving reads False for about 1.3 s at each promotion (normal: CloudNativePG refuses the history-file archive during the switchover; no segment missing). Grafana's health probe saw 17 s of HTTP 503 at the `grafana-db` switchover in the move (9 s quiet); Zitadel was not probed. **Applications must reconnect at the switchover** (`docs/database-failover-clients.md`).
- **A Flux Ready is not a finished roll, and `cluster-up` returning is not the end of the move.** On both moves `cnpg` was Ready and `cluster-up` returned BEFORE the databases were whole. First move: `cnpg` was Ready 17 s before rc 0, the roll began 16 s before it, the closing `cluster-verify` (30 / 0 / 0) ended with `cluster-up` (only its last 16 s overlapped the first replica recreations), 42 of 45 Kustomizations were on target 1:30 after rc 0, and both Clusters were healthy 3:08 and 3:14 after rc 0. Second move: the verify ended, the roll began 4 s later (3 s after `cluster-up` returned), `cnpg` and 45 of 45 Kustomizations were Ready 1:15 before the roll ended, and the Clusters were healthy 3:02 and 3:08 after rc 0. **Read the Clusters, not Flux and not `cluster-up`: `kubectl get clusters.postgresql.cnpg.io -A` (phase "Cluster in healthy state") tells that the databases are whole.**
- **Longhorn 1.12.1 to 1.12.2 (a patch hop).** The manager DaemonSet is replaced on all nodes together (`maxUnavailable` 100 %); all six CSI sidecars change image; the instance managers are replaced. **Do not restart a Longhorn workload or drain a node in the first minutes: idle nodes had NO instance manager for 1:25 to 2:10** (an attach there is impossible then); an attached volume kept serving (max write gap 0.27 s, no rebuild). **The engine fix needs one more step by hand:** upgrade each volume's engine image (`docs/patching.md` step 6; live on an attached healthy volume 9.3 s, offline on a detached one 2.4 s, n=1). An old instance-manager pod stays while an attached volume's engine process runs in it (the sixth of six stayed 68 minutes); detach the volume to free it (not run). The old engine image goes by itself about an hour after its last reference. **With the forge OFF the platform has no Longhorn volume:** the hop matters with the forge or an opt-in component.
- **Children (the optional CAPI module).** The Flux copy moves in place: 7 of 7 controllers on 2.10.0 23 s after the management's ref read-back, 0 restarts, no CRD update refused, the profile Kustomizations stayed Ready, the child's GitRepository was Unknown for 6 s (one 2-node child, n=1). **The stored versions of `alerts` and `providers` stay `[v1beta3, v1]`** on a moved child (a fresh child has `[v1]`): migrate the stored objects (re-apply or a storage-version migration, then patch `storedVersions`) before a Flux release that stops serving `v1beta3`; not run. The child's own Flux follows its `CHILD_REF` (`refs/heads/main` when it is unset, as in the checked-in children): with a pinned ref its `flux-egress` declares `v1beta3` until that ref moves (INFO deprecation lines meanwhile), and bumping `CHILD_REF` is the operator's next step, NOT run; a child that leaves it unset reads the 0.4.5 tree as soon as `main` does, and a child still on Flux 2.9.6 cannot apply `v1` (expected, not run), so **move the management first**. The children's Cilium DaemonSets get `maxUnavailable: 1` only where a child's file carries it (the checked-in `edge-N` files and the example do; **a child file of your own must carry `updateStrategy.rollingUpdate.maxUnavailable: 1`, also under `envoy.`, or `scripts/check-child-clusters.py`, the "Child clusters" step of `ci.yml`, fails**; adding them is the strategy-only Helm change that ran on one live 2-node child): the Helm upgrade restarted no pod; **a later agent rollout takes about twice as long** (11.3 s to 22.4 s for the agents, 9.1 s to 17.9 s for envoy) and goes one agent at a time; 0 failures on three probes; tested only for a strategy-only change on one 2-node child (a change that alters the pod template is unproven: read the infrastructure `docs/admin-access.md` warning about a Helm upgrade of a live child's Cilium first). The child's Talos and Kubernetes versions are not changed by this release (apps #222).
- **Alert and Provider are declared at `notification.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v1`.** It needs Flux 2.10 (infrastructure 0.4.3 or later); a cluster on 2.9.6 cannot apply v1 (expected, not run). A fresh 0.4.4 install already stores v1 (`storedVersions ["v1"]` before and after the move); a management cluster moved in place to Flux 2.10 (the infrastructure 0.4.2 to 0.4.3 move) and not reinstalled since has `[v1beta3, v1]` (infrastructure `docs/upgrade.md`), and the same migration applies before a Flux release that stops serving `v1beta3`. **Neither kind has a status or a Ready condition: read the Kustomization `flux-egress` (Ready) and the version read back.**
- **The weekly scan lists 59 images instead of 51** and a local replay of 2026-11-12 (Trivy 0.69.3, the CI flags, the VEX file) reads **56 images clean and 3 red on ONE CVE, CVE-2026-123158**: the Actions scan of the tag is expected RED on `forgejo:15.0.11-rootless`, `restic/restic:0.19.1` and the children's `kustomize-controller:v1.10.1` (the scheduled scan of 2026-11-10 was already red on the first two, and on `csi-provisioner:v5.3.0` that the Longhorn bump replaces), and it is read after the push. The Longhorn Trivy ignore entry is gone (the file stays, empty); the one VEX statement (restic, OpenSSH CVE-2026-60002) is renewed to **2027-01-09** and `check-vex.py` is red on every pull request from 2027-01-10: renew it again or replace the image before then.
- **Backup path.** Backups, restores and PITR on the new sidecar passed ("Cloud proof"), but **the first scheduled Backup on v0.16.0 runs the night after the move (03:10 and 03:25 UTC): read `kubectl get backups.postgresql.cnpg.io -A` the next morning.**
- **Not changed.** Talos v1.14.4, Kubernetes v1.37.1, Flux 2.10.0 on the management, the management's Cilium, the CloudNativePG operator 1.30.1 and the PostgreSQL images (`18.6-202611030838-system-bookworm`, `16.15-202611020810-system-bookworm`), no collation change.

### Known open (0.4.5; limited to the defects this release touches; one line each)

- **CVE-2026-123158 (OpenSSH, password authentication bypass): the weekly scan is RED on three images and this release does not change it (LOCAL SCAN, full replay of the 59 images on 2026-11-12).** `forgejo:15.0.11-rootless`, `restic/restic:0.19.1` (its VEX statement covers CVE-2026-60002 only) and `kustomize-controller:v1.10.1` of the children's Flux copy (the infrastructure's Flux pin is the same image), 3 CRITICAL rows each (`openssh-client-common`, `openssh-client-default`, `openssh-keygen`; fixed in the Alpine package, 10.3_p1-r2 on Alpine 3.24 for `restic` and the controller, 10.2_p1-r1 on Alpine 3.23 for `forgejo`), 56 images clean. Published on 2026-11-05 and read by the scanner from 2026-11-10 at the latest (the tag run of 2026-11-09 did not see it), after the local reads above; the children's `kustomize-controller:v1.9.6` (the only 2.9.6 image rescanned) reads 4 rows on the database of 2026-11-11 19:04 UTC (this CVE and brotli CVE-2026-111497), so the move to 2.10.0 is still BETTER. The scheduled scan of 2026-11-10 opened apps #220 (`forgejo`) and #221 (`restic`), both open; the tag run opens one for the controller; the infrastructure side is dis-bzh/OpenAether-infra#455. **No fixed image exists** and none of the three was rebuilt. **Exposure read on 2026-11-12 (local image reads with `docker run --network none`, the manifests and the infrastructure code; no live cluster): the exploitable `sshd` is not in any of the three images.** The bug is exploitable through the server side of OpenSSH (`sshd`, the password check on Alpine without PAM; OpenSSH puts the root cause outside `sshd`, in musl's `crypt()`, and works around it in `sshd(8)`; Alpine's advisory lists "Docker and other container users that do not run `sshd` in the container" as not affected), and Trivy flags the three client packages only because Alpine's database is keyed on the source package `openssh`. Each image holds only `openssh-client-common`, `openssh-client-default` and `openssh-keygen`: no `sshd`, no `sshd_config`, no host keys, no PAM, no `openssh-server` in the package database or the image history; none of the 24 client binaries imports `crypt` or a shadow-file function; no account has a `$5$` or `$6$` hash. Nothing starts or reaches a server: `restic` (9 workloads, no `containerPort`, no Service, `s3:` repositories only), the controller (ports 8080 and 9440, no Service or route), `forgejo` (the forge is off by default; its own SSH server is a Go program and is switched off, `DISABLE_SSH` true and `START_SSH_SERVER` false; the chart's `forgejo-ssh` Service is ClusterIP only and the network policy admits port 3000 only). The only OpenSSH daemons OpenAether provisions are the bastions, Ubuntu (glibc with PAM, listed "not affected" by Ubuntu), key-only with password authentication off, and Talos nodes run none. **Limits:** no source says outright that the client packages are unaffected (the sources say `sshd` and are silent on the client; the measurements above are the support); no live cluster was read; the Proxmox host that doubles as the default bastion is operator-managed and its `sshd` configuration is not in the repository (Proxmox VE is Debian-based, glibc: outside the advisory's musl condition, but Debian's tracker lists bookworm and trixie `openssh` as "vulnerable" without a musl or PAM note, and the host's `UsePAM` was not read); the CVE is in the CISA KEV catalog (added 2026-11-09), so it is treated as exploited, and key-only bastions must never be relaxed. No statement was written for it here: a dated OpenVEX `not_affected` / `vulnerable_code_not_in_execute_path` statement would make a local Trivy 0.69.3 run read 0 CRITICAL on the three images (tested on a copy), and is a waiver the owner decides (dis-bzh/OpenAether-infra#455, option 2). A red scan is a finding, not a waiver. The infrastructure CHANGELOG carries the same item for its own scan. `docs/forgejo.md` ("0 CRITICAL", database of 2026-11-04) and `docs/supply-chain.md` (the restic statement, CVE-2026-60002) keep their dated figures and are not edited by this release.
- **apps #217, #219, #215, #214, #199: delivered and read once (n=1, LOCAL SCAN and LAB), open until a second reader, the tag-run scan and the owner's issue round close them.** #217: see "Security" (the issue is titled for the old reference). #219: first scheduled Backup NOT run, Actions scan listing the sidecar not seen, certifi reachability not read, checker durations WORSE (W5). #215: one template, one 2-node child, tags not digests, the `storedVersions` finding. #214: "Provider and Alert Ready" is read on `flux-egress`; a cluster on Flux 2.9.6 and the child's own Flux not run. #199: renewed to 2027-01-09, not fixed.
- **apps #213 (partly): the H7 comment is brought up to the proof.** The four child cluster files (`edge-1.yaml`, `edge-2.yaml`, `edge-3.yaml`, `example-scaleway.yaml.example`) said "NOT PROVEN ON A CLUSTER YET" above the H7 values, written before the child run that proved them; the comment now says what the run showed (comment lines only: the parsed YAML of every file is identical). Also open there: `configure.sh` line 164 (a ConfigMap hash: an edit recreates both vault Jobs), item F (`monitoring.enablePodMonitor` on `zitadel-db`), the last comment block of `authorizationpolicy-deny-metrics.yaml`.
- **apps #222 stays open (tracker, 0.5.0):** the CAPI child life cycle beyond create, delete and the in-place Flux move (Talos and Kubernetes version bumps one minor at a time, scale, node replacement, restart order on a 3-control-plane child) was never run; its item 4, the in-place move of the Flux copy, ran once.
- **Sightings of open defects (nothing new filed; one issue per theme per release):** apps #193 (`observability-vm-customresources` failed about 2 minutes in all four installs), apps #206 (Grafana restarted 3 times at first start in the install of the candidate), apps #180 and #128 (install convergence time, W6), `dis-bzh/OpenAether-infra#409` (the child ran Talos v1.13.9 and Kubernetes v1.35.3 against the management's v1.14.4 and v1.37.1). apps #208 (a scan that did not run is retried by nobody) and apps #218 (the Flux `Provider` posts to ONE Alertmanager pod) are untouched.
- **`dis-bzh/OpenAether-infra#450` (`flux_git_verify` across the Flux move) and the unsigned-push drill: NOT RUN in any run** (riders accepted for the runs on 2026-11-10, each "with a cloud proof"); no `SourceVerified` or `sourceVerificationFingerprint` was read across the Flux move. Moved to the next run that has a cluster up.
- **Run findings kept as drafts, not issues (one theme per release, inflow rule):** the restart of every PostgreSQL pod at a plugin bump (the upgrade item above); the pull queue behind the Longhorn wave that doubled the roll (a pre-pull of the sidecar, or an order of the Longhorn HelmRelease after the `cnpg` roll: the 0.5.0 milestone "service interruption and waiting delays"); the write gap of 22 to 28 s in the move against 14.4 and 14.7 s quiet; the live engine upgrade leaving the engine in the old instance-manager pod; the old engine image expiring after about an hour; `cnpg` Ready before the roll ends; ContinuousArchiving False about 1.3 s at each promotion; the restore-checker duration (an owner decision: re-decide the target, run the checker once on a 0.4.4 cluster, or accept); `kubectl cnpg restart` leaving an annotation that Flux removes; every restore on one worker; the PITR age rule (a new Backup makes the checker skip for 2 hours); the idle-time of the child HelmReleases' backoff; the child's Talos image not built by the install; the bastion SSH wait of 1:31 seen once (1 s in two other installs); the HTTP probe window of 148 failures after day-1 at one 0.4.4 install; the host-load sensitivity of the move wall. **An operator delay between the two PostgreSQL Clusters was built and removed before any run: by design it orders the Clusters against each other without shortening any one service's interruption (a design reading, never run); the work on interruption per service is planned for 0.5.0.**

### Not run (0.4.5; the honest list, one line each)

- **The first SCHEDULED Backup on the v0.16.0 sidecar** (only manual Backups ran; the first scheduled one runs the night after a move).
- **`flux_git_verify` drills and the unsigned-push drill** (`dis-bzh/OpenAether-infra#450`, riders).
- **The write interruption of the move on the final head** (measured with writers once, on b70c90a; on 0064d2e no writer ran). The `cnpg` render is identical at both heads. The cause of the move's excess over the quiet restart is not isolated.
- **Other providers** (OVH, Outscale), **larger children** (every child was 1 CP + 1 W; a 3-control-plane child is the shape for a zero-gap API), a child with the `cnpg` or `storage` profile, a child built on a clean account, **the child life cycle beyond create, delete and the in-place Flux move** (apps #222), an event delivery from a child, **the child's own Flux moved to the candidate ref**, a Flux 2.9.6 cluster applying v1, the Zitadel login probe (no SSO in any run).
- **A values change that alters the pod template or the ConfigMap of a live child's Cilium**, a larger child, another provider, and the variant without a prior manual restart annotation.

- **Longhorn:** a native volume on the platform (none with the forge OFF), the forge's Longhorn volume, the opt-in `longhorn-restore-test` component, the engine fix against its race, freeing the old instance manager by a workload restart, whether the fix needs the engine process in the new instance manager, a Longhorn backup.
- **Comparisons with no 0.4.4 baseline:** ContinuousArchiving at the promotion on the 0.4.4 plugin, the restore checker on a 0.4.4 cluster of the same day, a quiet restart at the 0.4.4 tags, `kubectl cnpg status`.
- **The forge-ON shape** (three PostgreSQL Clusters, `forgejo-db` and its health gate, a forge volume on Longhorn) was not run: the 0.4.4 finding that the `forgejo-db` gate does not hold its own roll is neither confirmed nor refuted here. The Pooler fixtures and the 540-probe exposure sweep did not run either (the metrics probe did: 2,420 samples on the move, all 403 with 19 bytes).
- **A tag-pinned move or install of 0.4.5** (moving branch refs only); **the infrastructure 0.4.5 cut** (the pin bump, the upgrade section, the docs) never ran on a cluster.
- **The Actions scan of the 59-image matrix and CI on the tag**, read after the push (expected RED on `forgejo`, `restic` and `kustomize-controller:v1.10.1`, green on the other 56 per the local replay of 2026-11-12: "Known open", first item; predicted from the LOCAL SCAN, not seen).
- **A cause for any WORSE row** (W1 to W9): one run per figure, host-load-confounded timings, no BETTER on a move.

## [0.4.4] — 2026-11-08

0.4.4 is a **debt release** of 0.4.3 (no feature), paired with **OpenAether-infra 0.4.4**. It moves the PostgreSQL image off the frozen Debian 11 tags whose certifi finding kept the weekly scan red in 0.4.3, repairs the alert delivery chain, tightens the scan and waiver process, and corrects documentation that over- or under-stated what was measured. Durations are minutes:seconds. **Rung words:** OFFLINE = mocked or offline tests green on the candidate (rendered manifests, fixtures, stubs, `vmalert-tool`, local Docker and Trivy); STATIC = a reading of sources, nothing run; LAB = a read on a cluster of lab 35 (2026-11-07) or lab 36 (2026-11-08): Scaleway, 3 control planes + 6 workers POP2-4C-16G + bastion, Talos v1.14.4, Kubernetes v1.37.1, ONE run per figure (n=1), the candidate on a MOVING BRANCH ref, never a tag. Durations are NOMINAL unless marked INCIDENT. WORSE = at least 10 s AND at least 10 % worse than a baseline re-read from an earlier lab; BETTER only without INCIDENT time and with the whole window observed (no row is claimed BETTER). Not a compliance claim. What this release is:

- **Security, PostgreSQL images (apps #162, #163).** The 16 image lines of the CloudNativePG PostgreSQL image move from the plain `18.6` and `16.15` tags (the last Debian 11 build) to the dated Debian 12 system builds `18.6-202611030838-system-bookworm` and `16.15-202611020810-system-bookworm`, same minors. OFFLINE: a local Trivy (CI version and flags) exits 0 on both. LAB (n=1, moving ref): the rolling update lost 0 acknowledged ids, was never seen with two primaries and cut writes for 9.71 to 16.33 s per planned switchover against the 35 s target, in lab 35 as a second move and in lab 36 inside ONE move from the released 0.4.3 tags. **Not shown:** point-in-time recovery on PostgreSQL 16 and the replica stores, archive continuity on timeline 3 of the primary store, the Pooler during a transaction, a move pinned to the tag, the Actions scan. The first restore-test run on the new image was slower (WORSE, row 3).
- **Other security and alerting (each read once at most, n=1).** Deny-metrics ports (apps #192); one token for the OpenBao snapshot pod (apps #74); vmalert to each Alertmanager pod (apps #207); the alert hosts as inputs (apps #146, half); `ForgejoScrapeDown` (apps #203, never seen firing); a WAL inhibit rule (apps #188).
- **Install and upgrade.** `forgejo-db` waits for its Cluster on a fresh install (apps #204) but did NOT hold its own image roll in either lab: **a Flux Ready is not a finished PostgreSQL roll, read the Clusters.** The Pooler init limit goes from 64Mi to 128Mi (apps #205: mitigated, stays open).
- **Scan and waivers.** `check-vex.py` fails a Trivy ignore entry on its last day (apps #200); `vuln-issue.sh` retries its `gh` calls (apps #209); the scan matrix goes from 40 to 51 images (apps #210); Renovate leaves the PostgreSQL image alone. **A local Trivy scan of the 13 added references (2026-11-07) found 12 clean and `docker.io/longhornio/csi-provisioner:v5.3.0` with 2 CRITICAL with a fix: after the release the weekly scan is expected to go red on it and `vuln-issue.sh` to open a `[vuln]` issue (predicted from the local read; the Actions scan has not run; the owner accepted it as a known open item, see "Known open").**
- **Evidence, and its limit.** Heads **apps 77e54104bc1c94f51d928fe989a8d62c4c7ce2fb** (lab 35 move 1 ran its parent dd301f1e57fc0ec259bf7d43d41649a71dd124ab, all but the image) and **infra e387f06f9bc1eb39c045153343e0621ea8ef291f**, on MOVING branch refs, never on a tag. **What is released is the proven head plus the cut:** the apps tag differs from 77e5410 by this file, `README.md`, `README.fr.md`, `docs/alert-delivery.md`, `docs/runbooks.md`, `docs/kyverno-enforcement.md`, `apps/flux/README.md` and one step of `.github/workflows/ci.yml` only (documentation: the version sections, this entry, and the rung words of four pages that the labs made stale, see "Documented"; the workflow step is the kubeconform input, see "Fixed"; no manifest, script, image or `.vex` file; the diff is stated again in the release notes, and this entry is corrected if it differs). The date of this heading, 2026-11-08, is the day of the cut, not of a tag: it is provisional until the tag is pushed. **A move or an install pinned to the tag 0.4.4 is NOT proven.** Six WORSE groups are listed under "Cloud proof"; the owner accepted them on 2026-11-08, each carried as "cause not established". None is a data-safety regression, none is claimed as a product regression, none is hidden.

### Cloud proof of the 0.4.4 heads (lab 35, 2026-11-07; lab 36, 2026-11-08; Scaleway, 3 CP + 6 W POP2-4C-16G + bastion, n=1)

Lab 35 cluster A ran the **arm** (the released 0.4.3 tags), **move 1** (to apps dd301f1 / infra e387f06, all but the PostgreSQL image) and **move 2** (to apps 77e5410, the image roll); lab 35 cluster B was a **fresh install** of the candidate, forge on; **lab 36** installed the released 0.4.3 tags and moved them to the candidate in ONE `cluster-up`, the move this release documents. Baselines: labs 29 to 32 and lab 34 (the 0.4.3 proof); for "everything ready" lab 32's 12:27, for a waiting `cnpg` gate lab 34 run 2 (9:45) and cluster B (9:52), not lab 32's 9:43 (its gate passed in 41 s on empty Clusters).

- **Lab 35 arm (describes 0.4.3).** T0 to rc 0 13:07 INCIDENT (load-balancer ACL trap), 41 of 41 Ready 11:26 after rc 0 INCIDENT (the known `observability-vm-customresources` fault) and day-1 2:38 were WORSE against older labs (+16 to +93 %). Also WORSE: the first invocation 4:38 INCIDENT against lab 30's 3:28 (+1:10, +33.7 %); zitadel-db 3 of 3 at 6:15 after `cnpg` Progressing against lab 34 cluster B's 5:39 (+36 s, +10.6 %, at the edge); the HTTP probe window 1:23 against that cluster's 6 s; `cluster-verify` 1:42 against its 54 s (a different measurement, not a gate row). The 2-instance fixture `lab27-standard` switched over on the OLD image with a **31.03 s** write gap (+87 % against the 3-instance class; a FINDING, 4 s under the 35 s target), not reproduced (8.51 to 10.88 s in four later switchovers).
- **Lab 35 moves.** Move 1: `PLAN_ONLY` 1:53, `0 to add, 3 to change, 0 to destroy`; the ONE `cluster-up` **11:00**, own verify 36 / 0 / 0 (SAME against lab 32, WORSE against labs 31 and 29: row 2); 44 of 44 Ready on the target 4:07 to 4:10 after the ref switch (row 1); 1 Hz probes (API, Zitadel, forge, echo) 0 failed of 1,990 to 3,968 each; 82,798 acknowledged ids and 1,255 forge pushes, 0 missing; 9 of 9 boot IDs identical. Move 2: `cluster-up` **8:49** (its closing verify was taken BEFORE the roll, which began 1:14 after it returned); 44 of 44 on the target 4:39 (row 1). `CiliumPolicyDeniedBurst` fired at 06:00 UTC and cleared 4 minutes after the lab's pinned writers were removed (a probable lab cause).
- **Lab 35 cluster B.** T0 to rc 0 **9:56 in ONE invocation, INCIDENT-absorbed** (the ACL trap hit and was retried inside): SAME against labs 30 and 29 once about 44 s of bucket creation is carried (-2.1 %, +4.4 %); 17.5 to 20.5 % lower, bucket creation carried, than the arm, lab 32 and lab 34 run 1, and 13.0 % lower, raw, than lab 34 run 2, none called BETTER (INCIDENT time inside both sides); n=1, dis-bzh/OpenAether-infra#361 stays open. The `cnpg` gate WAITED (6m5s): an OBSERVATION, not proof that lab 32's race is fixed. `forgejo-db` Ready 5 s after its Cluster (apps #204, one order). 44 of 44 Ready 11:16 after rc 0 INCIDENT: WORSE against lab 34 cluster B and run 2 (+14.2 %, +15.6 %), SAME against lab 32's 12:27. Other WORSE items: row 4.
- **Lab 36, the install at the released 0.4.3 tags (describes 0.4.3).** T0 to rc 0 12:21 INCIDENT (the ACL trap again); with 27 s of bucket creation carried from a failed first attempt, 12:48: SAME against lab 35's arm and lab 32, WORSE against labs 30 and 29 (+17.4 %, +25.3 %). `cnpg` Progressing at rc 0 + 2:22 against 1:53 (+29 s, +26 %): WORSE. Day-1 2:41 against lab 34 run 1's 2:08 and lab 34 cluster B's 1:22 (+25.8 %, +96.3 %): WORSE. Install-time restarts 22 main + 0 init against 9, 10, 15 and 18-19 in earlier labs: WORSE (16 of the 22 are Longhorn's; none added by the move). **INCIDENT, external: github.com answered 504 on release-asset downloads from 09:41:01 until the last chart was fetched at 10:13:21 (about 32 minutes).** Five charts that are github.com release assets could not be fetched; 43 of 43 Kustomizations were first Ready at 10:05:01 (25:06 after rc 0) and Grafana only at 10:15, so 43 of 43 was not a complete stack. The databases were 3 of 3 at rc 0 + 7:44 / 7:50 against 8:08 / 8:14 (-24 s, -4.9 %: SAME); the -53 to -57 s after `cnpg` Progressing lie inside the INCIDENT and are NOT rated BETTER.
- **Lab 36, the ONE move (NOMINAL, launched at 10:16:56 after the 504 episode).** `PLAN_ONLY` 1:57, `0 to add, 3 to change, 0 to destroy`; ONE `cluster-up` **8:54** (ref switched 6:53 after launch; own verify 33 / 0 / 0, taken BEFORE the roll; SAME as lab 35 move 2's 8:49); the first "Recreating instance pod" 0:36 after rc 0; 44 of 44 on the target 5:38 after the ref switch (row 6). 9 of 9 boot IDs identical; restarts 22 + 0 before and after; alerts at 10:35 Watchdog only (four earlier `KyvernoPolicyViolation` gone, unexplained); only the PostgreSQL pods changed image. Host load was about 1.9 times lab 35 move 2's (mean 6.62 against 3.44): lab-side, it may touch the wall and Kustomization times.
  - **Flux ordering, the difference from lab 35.** `cnpg` went Progressing at 10:26:21 and Ready at 10:28:05 (1:44): its gate HELD the roll this time. The `forgejo-db` Kustomization, behind `cnpg`, was Ready at 10:28:28, 1 s BEFORE its Cluster's first "Recreating": it did not hold its own roll (2 of 2 labs), and `forgejo-db` rolled in a second wave 2:00 after the other two (platform window 180.8 s against 111.6 s, row 6). In lab 35 move 2 `cnpg` was Ready 5 s after Progressing, before any Cluster flipped. Why the gate held here and not there is not established: a race between Flux's health check and the operator is the hypothesis, not isolated.
  - **Not run in the one move:** the `lab27` fixtures (Pooler, `standard`, `light`), point-in-time recovery, `oa_backup_verify_ok`, a restore of a new-image Backup, the CronJob templates in declared-against-running, the rollback and bad-tag drills (lab 35 only). **Not run in any lab: a move pinned to `refs/tags/0.4.4`.**
- **Sweeps.** Eight sweeps of 540 probes (lab 35: the arm, move 1 twice, move 2, cluster B; lab 36: the tag twice, after the move): no Prometheus body on any first-hop response and one byte-identical table (310 x 403, 114 x 301, 22 x 302, 22 x 303, 1 x 307, 10 x 400, 39 x 404, 22 x 200), except the first lab 36 sweep (Grafana not yet installed). 54 spellings x 5 hosts x 2 listeners; redirects not followed; the 22 x 200 are the lab echo app. Not a proof that every path is closed.
- **WORSE rows (accepted by the owner at the release gate on 2026-11-08, each carried as "cause not established"; none is a data-safety regression).**
  1. **Convergence after a ref switch (NOMINAL).** 44 of 44 Ready on the target 4:07 to 4:10 (lab 35 move 1) against lab 34 run 2's corrected 3:34 to 3:40 (+27 to +36 s, +12.3 to +16.8 %); `cnpg` DependencyNotReady 3:00 against 2:05 (+44 %); SAME against labs 32, 31, 29. Move 2's 4:39 (computed after the lab) is WORSE against lab 34 run 2 (+59 to +65 s), lab 32 (+13.9 %), lab 31 (+11.6 %) and lab 29 (+21.8 %). **Cause not established** (one more Kustomization or a larger change set; a Flux requeue is a hypothesis). A longer cascade; no probe failed, no node rolled.
  2. **Move wall and segments against older labs.** Move 1's 11:00 against lab 31's 6:41 (+64.6 %) and lab 29's 7:06 (+54.9 %); launch to ref switched 9:04 against 5:14 (+73.2 %); ref switched to rc 0 1:56 against 1:35 and 1:27 (+22 %, +33 %); `PLAN_ONLY` 1:53, 2:00 (move 2) and (lab 36) 1:57 against lab 31's 1:41 (+12 s, +11.9 %; +19 s, +18.8 %; +16 s, +15.8 %); (lab 36) ref switched to rc 0 2:01 against lab 32's 1:35 and lab 31's 1:27 (+26 s, +27.4 %; +34 s, +39.1 %). SAME against lab 32 (10:30, +4.8 %). Older change sets; cause not established.
  3. **Restore-test durations after the roll (first run, loaded host).** `cnpg-recovery-restore-test` 122 / 94 / 106 / 100 s against 83 / 83 / 78 / 83 s on the old image earlier in the same lab (+47 %, +13 %, +36 %, +20 %); job wall 7:20 against 5:44 (+27.9 %); the verifier 94 s against 52 s (+81 %). **Cause not established** (concurrency with six other Jobs and a drill, 71 more minutes of WAL, cold pulls). **Lab 36 ran the same Job once on a quiet host on the new image: 83 / 84 / 83 / 83 s, SAME as the old image's:** this neither reproduces nor explains the lab 35 slowness (it is consistent with the concurrency candidate, not a proof: another cluster, another load), so the row stays listed.
  4. **Lab 35 cluster B, fresh install.** (a) Zitadel setup Job 1:51 against 22 s and HelmRelease Ready 2:37 against 1:07, INCIDENT-class: a 330 MB helper image took 1m40s to pull on a cold node; (b) day-1 2:20 against 1:22 (+58 s, +70.7 %), host load 1.26 against 0.50 to 1.08; (c) restarts 18 main + 0 init against 15 (+20 %); (d) `cnpg` Progressing at rc 0 + 1:43 against 1:14 (+39.2 %) and `zitadel-db` 3 of 3 at + 7:49 against 6:53 (+13.6 %), INCIDENT-class (a PostgreSQL 18 pull of 3m10s on a cold node); (e) HTTP probe window 91 failed in 1:30 against 6 in 6 s. The cold-pull storm (237 pulls) is a correlation, not an isolation.
  5. **`cluster-verify` duration.** Lab 35 move 2's 1:18 against lab 34 cluster B's 54 s (+44 %); move 1's post-move 64 s (+18.5 %, at the edge). The 0.4.3 tag took 1:42 in the same harness, so 33 to 36 checks in 57 to 78 s is not slower than the arm; lab 36's 59 s and 60 s are under the 10 s floor. Cause not established.
  6. **The one-move ordering (lab 36).** Platform roll window 180.8 s against lab 35 move 2's 111.6 s (+69.2 s, +62 %); 44 of 44 Ready on the target 5:38 against 4:39 after the ref switch (+59 s, +21 %; more against the older labs of row 1). Cause of the longer cascade observed: `cnpg` held the roll and `forgejo-db` rolled after it, in a second wave (the order); why the gate held here and not in lab 35 is not established (a hypothesis: a race between Flux's health check and the operator); each Cluster's own roll is SAME or shorter; no bar touched; host load about 1.9 times lab 35's.
  - **Near-misses, NOT WORSE by the rule (under the 10 s floor):** `lab27-ha` switchover +1.3 to +1.7 s (+9 to +12 %); `grafana-db` +1.21 s (+8.5 %); `forgejo-db` +0.96 s (+6.7 %); forge patch to Ready +53 s (+8.2 %).
- **Not shown.** A tag-pinned move or install of 0.4.4; the Actions scan of the 13 added references and arm64 (a local read only); `ForgejoScrapeDown` firing; the by-FQDN egress path to a real host and the failing-send path of apps #207; the order "forge enabled when `cnpg` is already Ready"; a `cluster-verify` DURING a roll; a larger or busier database; OVH and Outscale; failover and zone drills; a Grafana MFA sign-in.

### Security (0.4.4; rung: OFFLINE and local Docker/Trivy for the code, LAB (3 CP + 6 W, n=1, moving branch refs, not a tag) where a bullet says so)

- **PostgreSQL images: the plain tags to the dated Debian 12 system builds (apps #162, #163).** The weekly scan of the 0.4.3 tag was red on exactly these two images (`ghcr.io/cloudnative-pg/postgresql:16.15` and `:18.6`, one CRITICAL each, certifi GHSA-6q48-6c7w-9gw2; no waiver, the verifier uses certifi); upstream no longer builds those tags. The 16 lines carry one dated tag per major (digests under "Upgrade from 0.4.3") and no digest in the pin; the images hold Debian 12.15, certifi 2026.10.23, PyJWT 2.15.1. Only image lines changed.
  - **OFFLINE.** Trivy 0.69.3 with the CI flags, amd64: exit 0 on both images, read three times (the last on 2026-11-07); the Docker tests on the new image pass.
  - **LAB, lab 35 move 2 (NOMINAL, n=1, moving ref at 77e5410).** 15 of 15 pods on the new images. **0 acknowledged commit lost** (147,120 ids on 7 writers, 2,197 forge pushes). **Never two writers:** detector 0 s over 57 minutes; pinned writers 0 acknowledgements after the promotion on 7 of 7 demotions, but the script printed FAIL once (`zitadel-db`, its stricter second criterion: 2 acknowledgements 75 ms after a reader error tick, 4.4 s BEFORE the promotion), read as not a second writer, whether those commits connected before the tick not established. **Collation unchanged** (`datcollversion` NULL in all 27 `pg_database` rows; the three collation queries 0 in the 21 connectable databases; no collation line in 15 pods' logs at one scan). **Cross-image restore:** the recovery test restored old-image backups and WAL on the new image, PASS 4 of 4, healthy in 94 to 122 s (row 3); PITR PASS on one of four combinations. Two new-image Backups restored in 64 s (startup only, ids not checked).
  - **LAB, lab 36 (ONE move from the released 0.4.3 tags; NOMINAL; n=1; the three platform Clusters only).** **0 acknowledged ids lost** (47,768 on 3 writers, 39,006 on 3 pinned writers); **never two primaries** (detector 0 s; pinned writers 0 acknowledgements after the promotion on 3 of 3, both criteria PASS); collation unchanged; archive MISSING 0 on timelines 1 and 2 (primary stores of `zitadel-db` and `grafana-db` read once); the recovery test PASS x4 in 83 to 84 s. A quorum standby was not observed at every step (the lag sampler is blind at each switchover, 13 to 14 s).
  - **Still not shown.** PITR on `grafana-db` and the replica stores (a new base backup makes the test SKIP for 2 hours); timeline-3 archive continuity on the primary store; the Pooler during a transaction; the Actions scan. The restore-test durations are WORSE (row 3). The weekly scan closes nothing by itself: apps #162 and #163 do not close until the release names them and the Actions scan is green.
- **The PyJWT VEX statement is dropped (CVE-2026-102268; apps #199, in part).** The new images carry PyJWT 2.15.1 and Trivy reports no PyJWT row at CRITICAL or HIGH; the statement leaves the VEX file. The restic statement (CVE-2026-60002) and the Longhorn ignore entry (SUSE-SU-2026:4619-1) remain, both ending 2026-12-04. OFFLINE; stands only with the image change.
- **The deny-metrics AuthorizationPolicy names its ports (apps #192).** `deny-metrics-paths` carries `ports: ["80", "443"]`: a DENY rule with HTTP attributes and no port makes Istio warn that it denies all TCP traffic in its scope, so a TCP or TLS listener added to the Gateway would have been cut; `scripts/check-gateway-routes.py` now fails on a policy with no port, a missing listener port or an integer port. OFFLINE. **LAB (lab 35 move 1, n=1):** 0 bodies in the sweep, and ONE `services-gateway` reconcile after the ref switch showed 0 Istio warnings (8 before). **Limits:** the sweep table is identical at the 0.4.3 tag (no regression, not an effect of the ports line); first hop only; no TCP or TLS listener exercised.
- **The OpenBao snapshot pod mounts no ServiceAccount token but one (apps #74).** `automountServiceAccountToken: false` on the CronJob's pod; a projected token only in the `snapshot` init container, none for `restic-push`. OFFLINE. **LAB (lab 35 move 1, n=1):** a manual Job was Complete in 27 s and the snapshot reached both repositories. Limit: one manual Job, not a scheduled run.

### Changed (0.4.4; rung: OFFLINE, rendered and tested, plus LAB reads (n=1, moving branch refs) where a bullet says so)

- **The chat and heartbeat hosts Alertmanager may reach are one input each, in a Kustomization of their own (apps #146; half).** The `vmalertmanager` CiliumNetworkPolicy loses its `toFQDNs` rule; the hosts and DNS are the policy `vmalertmanager-egress-hosts` of the new Kustomization `observability-alert-egress`, fed by `ALERT_CHAT_HOST` and `ALERT_HEARTBEAT_HOST` of the `cluster-identity` ConfigMap (defaults `hooks.slack.com`, `hc-ping.com`). OFFLINE. **LAB (lab 35 move 1, n=1):** the policy exists and the Kustomization was Ready 3 s after `observability`. **Limits:** read through a lab-only additive policy that kept the lab's stand-in receiver reachable, so the by-FQDN path to a real host and the gap of the swap were NOT measured.
- **New alert `ForgejoScrapeDown` (apps #203).** `up{job="services-observability/forgejo"} == 0` for 3 minutes, warning; silent without the forge brick. 3 minutes, not the proposed 10: an induced upgrade left `up` at 0 for about 7.5 minutes (INCIDENT, one run). A missing `forgejo-metrics` Secret and a forge at 0 replicas fire nothing. OFFLINE; LAB (n=1, two clusters): loaded, inactive, `up{...}` = 1. **The firing drill was NOT RUN** (the lab's classifier denied the command; nothing was worked around): delivered, firing unproven.
- **An inhibit rule mutes `CnpgWalArchiveFailing` while `CnpgWalNeverArchived` fires for the same Cluster (apps #188).** The two fired about ten minutes apart for one fault (pages at a receiver not checked). `equal` is `cnpg_io_cluster` alone, so same-named Clusters in two namespaces would share the mute. OFFLINE. **LAB (n=1):** the configuration was loaded and a SYNTHETIC pair showed the second alert inhibited; not the real WAL fault.
- **`RestoreTestNeverReported` also watches the dump-load restore test (apps #190, item 1).** Kind `dump`, older than 3 days with no series, for 1 hour; per kind, so one store that reports hides the other's silence. OFFLINE.
- **PostgreSQL image pins are hand-bumped, and checked.** `renovate.json5` loses the two regex managers that tracked the image (a regex on `MM.mm` goes silent on a dated tag; one on the whole tag would open a database roll per weekly upstream build). `scripts/check-image-pins.py` fails on a tag that is not `<major>.<minor>-<12 digits>-system-bookworm|trixie` and on two tags for one major; it reads the rendered DAG, 10 of the 16 lines (`docs/patching.md` covers the rest). OFFLINE.
- **A same-namespace ingress allow ships as a component (apps #112).** `apps/components/allow-same-namespace`: one CiliumNetworkPolicy, its own namespace, any port. OFFLINE. **No namespace includes it yet**, and it is not shown to suffice for an ambient namespace (lab 25 also needed an Istio ALLOW policy).

### Fixed (0.4.4; rung: mocked and offline for the scripts and manifests, plus LAB reads (n=1, moving branch refs) where a bullet says so)

- **vmalert sends to every Alertmanager pod (apps #207).** `notifiers` lists one URL per pod instead of the headless Service, through which vmalert resolved one pod at a time (one 0.4.3 sample: pod 0 held 5 alerts, pod 1 none). `VMAlertNotifierErrors` (critical) now means every address fails; the new `VMAlertNotifierPodErrors` (warning, 30 minutes) fires when one does. OFFLINE. **LAB (lab 35 move 1, n=1):** both pod names resolve from a vmalert pod; with `alertmanager-0` deleted the other answered at every 2-s sample for 41 s and nothing fired. **Limit:** the 21 s window was shorter than vmalert's send cycle, so the FAILING-send path was not exercised. **Residual:** the Flux notification Provider takes ONE address and still posts through the headless Service.
- **`forgejo-db` waits for its Cluster (apps #204).** `36-forgejo-db.yaml` carries the `healthCheckExprs` entry of `cnpg`; with `wait: true` alone it was Ready 6 to 7 s after the live patch with its Cluster at 0 of 3 on two of three installs. OFFLINE. **LAB (lab 35 cluster B, fresh install, ONE order, n=1):** Ready 5 s after its Cluster; lab 34 cluster B, same order on 0.4.3, Ready 73 s BEFORE it. **Limits:** one order (not "forge enabled when `cnpg` is already Ready"); and **the gate does not hold an image roll**: in lab 35 move 2 and in lab 36 it was Ready 1 s BEFORE the operator began rolling its Cluster. Only `forgejo` depends on `forgejo-db`, so Flux alerts do not see a held gate: read `kubectl -n flux-system get kustomization forgejo-db`.
- **The Pooler `bootstrap-controller` init limit goes from 64Mi to 128Mi (apps #205; mitigated, stays open).** At 64Mi it was OOMKilled at install on the 0.4.3 proof and the lab 35 arm; the peak was never read. OFFLINE. **LAB (lab 35, n=1 per cluster):** 16 pod starts at 128Mi, 0 init restarts (7 read pod by pod, 9 rest on the pods being Ready again within 3 to 8 s). **Limits:** the labs' Poolers are by-hand fixtures, so the lab does not show that the product delivers the value, and the contrast is not controlled. Count the Pooler pods' init restarts after the move.
- **A CNPG restore test that cannot read what it published before no longer drops the last success of the datasets it skipped (apps #190, item 3).** With `--publish` in `latest` and `pitr` modes, an unreadable ConfigMap `oa-restore-test-metrics` and no failed dataset make `restore_cnpg.py` print `ERROR: not publishing ...` and exit 1; a run with a FAILED dataset still publishes (`ok=0` raises `RestoreTestFailed`), so its skipped datasets lose their last success, as in 0.4.3. OFFLINE; not triggered in the lab.
- **The replica verdict of the backup verifier says "could not compare" when the primary store refuses the listing (apps #151).** Still a failed row. OFFLINE; the refusal path was not triggered in the lab.
- **`scripts/check-vex.py` fails a Trivy ignore entry on its last day (apps #200).** Trivy stops applying an entry from 00:00 UTC of its `expired_at` day (read in its source, not observed), so the check fails it when `expired_at <= today`; in 0.4.3 the Longhorn scans could be red on 2026-12-04 with the check green. OFFLINE.
- **`scripts/ops/vuln-issue.sh` retries each `gh` call (apps #209).** Three attempts, 5 s then 10 s apart (a 503 on `gh issue close` turned a clean weekly scan red on 2026-11-03); `gh issue create` is not idempotent, so a 503 sent after creation can leave two issues. The scan itself has no retry (apps #208, not built). OFFLINE; not seen in Actions.
- **The scan matrix goes from 40 to 51 images (apps #210).** `scripts/dev/gen-sbom.py` also reads images passed as `--*-image` arguments and `*_IMAGE` env values: Longhorn's engine, instance-manager, backing-image-manager and support-bundle-kit, six CSI sidecars and cert-manager's ACME solver (never run here): 11 added by this change, plus the 2 dated PostgreSQL references and minus the 2 plain tags: 40 to 51. Still outside the matrix: the Barman Cloud plugin sidecar image. OFFLINE. **Findings, a local Trivy read (0.69.3, CI flags, CRITICAL with a fix only, amd64, 2026-11-07; HIGH not asked):** 12 of the 13 added references exit 0, the two PostgreSQL references and Longhorn's three v1.12.1 images (under the libexpat ignore entry that ends 2026-12-04) among them. **`docker.io/longhornio/csi-provisioner:v5.3.0` exits 1: 2 CRITICAL with a fix upstream, both in the Go binary: `google.golang.org/grpc` v1.72.1 (CVE-2026-33186, fixed in 1.79.3) and Go `stdlib` v1.24.2 (CVE-2025-68121, fixed in 1.24.13, 1.25.7 and 1.26.0-rc.3); reachability in a CSI provisioner was not read.** This is the change working as intended (the image was invisible to the scan), not a regression, but once 0.4.4 is released the weekly scan is expected to go red on it and `vuln-issue.sh` to open `[vuln] docker.io/longhornio/csi-provisioner:v5.3.0` (predicted from the local read; the Actions scan has not run). The owner accepted this on 2026-11-08 (see "Known open").

- **CI: the kubeconform step validates the default of a `toFQDNs` `matchName` (apps #146).** The first CI run of this release's pull request failed on the new `vmalertmanager-egress-hosts` policy: its two hostnames are Flux variables (`${ALERT_CHAT_HOST:=hooks.slack.com}`) and the CRD catalog's schema checks `matchName` against a hostname pattern.
