The step now validates a copy of the rendered output where only `matchName: ${VAR:=default}` is replaced by its default (what an unconfigured cluster applies); every other substitution is left as rendered (replacing all of them exposes five other CRD defaults that the schema refuses, left as they are). Rung: the edited step replayed locally on the rendered tree with kubeconform 0.8.0: 571 resources, 474 valid, 0 invalid, 97 skipped; the Trivy config scan of the same render (0.69.3) rc 0. Limit: the CI job itself runs on the pull request.

### Documented (0.4.4; rung: text only, a reading of the manifests and sources unless a line says otherwise)

- **`docs/key-inventory.md` (apps #77).** Three rows for the Cluster API provider credentials (Scaleway, OVH, Outscale): created by hand in `capi-clusters`, **not in OpenBao**, not in git, not in the escrow attestation; the etcd snapshots hold them in clear (encrypted by restic) and a restore of OpenBao does not bring them back. Also the row apps #76 asked for (the External Secrets cert-controller can read every Secret; a static read).
- **The PKI runbook says which list shows a revoked certificate (apps #130, documentation leg only).** OCSP at once; the delta CRL within 15 minutes; the full CRL at its next scheduled rebuild (not measured). One OVH drill saw the delta CRL about 8 minutes later (no check times), so the page gives 15 minutes as the bound.
- **Why there is no alert on `require-security-context` denials (apps #82).** Kyverno 1.19.1 counts a denial under these Audit policies with an Enforce override as `audit` (the label comes from `validationFailureAction`, not `failureActionOverrides`). **Read on a cluster since (LAB, lab 35 move 1, n=1):** a really denied Pod was counted `audit`; `KyvernoPolicyViolation` excludes that policy by design, so such a denial raises no alert.
- **Runbook entries and corrections.** Entries for the new and changed alerts, the restore-test publish guard and `NodeClockNotSynchronised` (apps #94); `node_timex_sync_status` on a healthy Talos node, unread when the entry was written, **has since been read (LAB, n=1 on each of two clusters): 1 on 9 of 9 nodes**, so the rule is kept. Statements that over- or under-stated a source or a measurement are corrected (apps #213, partly: comments, the EOL calendar, four `docs/` pages), and `docs/database-failover-clients.md` says that `kubectl cnpg promote` output is not to be trusted (apps #36). **The rung words of four pages were brought to what the labs read (n=1, a stand-in receiver for the alert chain, what was not exercised stays stated):** `docs/alert-delivery.md` (it said "never run on a cluster" and "the drill is still to do"), the inhibit-rule and `NodeClockNotSynchronised` entries of `docs/runbooks.md` ("mocked only", "value not read"), the `forgejo-db` gate paragraph of `apps/flux/README.md` ("has not been run") and the `require-security-context` metric paragraph of `docs/kyverno-enforcement.md` ("not seen on a cluster"). `docs/alert-delivery.md` also no longer says that an infrastructure apply drops a `cluster-identity` key set by hand: nothing there removes it (a reading of the sources, not run).
- **`docs/patching.md` and `docs/supply-chain.md`.** The PostgreSQL hand-bump procedure (newest dated bookworm tag of the minor, CI scan flags, every line, `check-image-pins.py`), when to bump, and two follow-ups: CloudNativePG's full support of bookworm ended on 2026-06-10 and Debian LTS ends 2028-06-30, so a move to trixie is due before then; the `system` flavour is deprecated upstream. The procedure was not run in a lab.

### Upgrade from 0.4.3 (rung: LAB for the roll, the move and the reads below (n=1, NOMINAL, 3 CP + 6 W, moving branch refs, not a tag); OFFLINE or STATIC where a bullet says so)

**Path.** Supported before 1.0.0: the previous minor only, **0.4.3 to 0.4.4**, paired with **OpenAether-infra 0.4.4**. Apps is tagged first and the move is the apps-ref move of the infrastructure repository (`task cluster-up` with `git_ref = "refs/tags/0.4.4"`; the steps are in the OpenAether-infra 0.4.4 notes and CHANGELOG entry, and its `docs/upgrade.md` carries the standalone-verify caveat). The Flux controllers do not move (Flux 2.10.0). **What the labs ran:** lab 36 ran the real move once, from the released 0.4.3 tags to the candidate in ONE `cluster-up` on a MOVING branch ref (plan `0 to add, 3 to change, 0 to destroy`: the Talos inline manifest carries `git_ref`, so phase 1 shows `3 to change` on every ref change; 9 of 9 boot IDs identical afterwards); lab 35 ran it as two moves. **A move pinned to `refs/tags/0.4.4` was NOT run.** What follows is what the first sync does to a running cluster, in the order of the risk it carries.

- **Every PostgreSQL Cluster is rolled on the first sync.** CloudNativePG rolls `zitadel-db` (18.6, 3 instances), `grafana-db` (16.15, 3), `forgejo-db` (16.15, 3, with the forge brick) and any database profile an overlay includes (`ha` 3 instances with Poolers, `standard` 2, `light` 1).
  - **Old to new.** `ghcr.io/cloudnative-pg/postgresql:18.6` (index `sha256:899d3ed526b659d77935dde0e6bf2d69dbbf17d3d8c6486ca8cfd04bd3c18533`) to `18.6-202611030838-system-bookworm` (`sha256:605c6e45e733ce0e908505e9ac3c441637d6794d330ec0e9d8d6f631b6918b1e`); `:16.15` (`sha256:34cd4159d07b3410a1b29da35072e2e927a0905e717a61afa3814624a2e8859a`) to `16.15-202611020810-system-bookworm` (`sha256:639ef58e1ecca2ecb91ba6aa52f55a25fc59dd1792df99f2e99d81d85043a8d6`); all four resolved on ghcr.io on 2026-11-07. The base moves from Debian 11 to 12 (glibc 2.31 to 2.36, OpenSSL 1.1.1 to 3.0, ICU 67 to 72); PostgreSQL itself is the same release.
  - **What a Cluster does, and how long (LAB).** Replicas one at a time, the primary last, by a `Switchover` to a replica already on the new image; the old primary returns as a replica (its pod restarts twice); nothing proceeded while a pod was not Ready. First "Recreating instance pod" to healthy, lab 36: `zitadel-db` 91.6 s, `grafana-db` 96.2 s, `forgejo-db` 57.4 s (image on the node), the platform window 3:01 in two waves; write gap 14.97 / 14.13 / 14.05 s. Lab 35 move 2: 92.8, 89.7, 83.1 s, gap 14.91 / 15.48 / 15.19 s; `lab27-ha` 72.8 s, gap 16.33 s (16.13 s through the Pooler); `lab27-standard` (2 instances) 57.4 s, gap 9.71 s; `lab27-light` (1 instance, restart in place) 14.0 s. The target is 35 s (the old image gave 14.2 to 15.2 s). The roll starts AFTER `cluster-up` returns (1:14 later in lab 35, 0:36 in lab 36). `cnpg` and `forgejo-db` have a health gate with `timeout: 15m`: a Cluster slower than that fails the check and Flux retries (not seen). One run under the labs' writers (about 6 commits a second each): a larger or busier database is not covered.
  - **What the applications saw (LAB).** Lab 36: echo 0 of 1,075 failed and API 0 of 877; Zitadel `.well-known` HTTP 500 x 10 over 10 s at the `zitadel-db` switchover (plus one login-page 503 before the ref switch, unexplained); the forge `/api/healthz` HTTP 424 x 9 over 9 s at the `forgejo-db` switchover; lab 35 the same. Applications must reconnect at the switchover (`docs/database-failover-clients.md`); the `ha` Pooler hides the reconnect, not a transaction in flight (not examined).
  - **A Flux Ready is not a finished roll (LAB; the apps #204 limit).** `cluster-up`'s closing verify is taken BEFORE the roll. Whether Flux waits differed between the two labs (a race with the operator is the hypothesis, not isolated): in lab 35 move 2 `cnpg` was Ready 5 s after Progressing and 44 of 44 Kustomizations were Ready and on target 26 s before the last roll ended; in lab 36 `cnpg` waited 1:44 and the count coincided with the end of the roll. In both labs `forgejo-db` was Ready 1 s before its own roll began. After the apply, read the Clusters (`kubectl get cluster -A`, phase `Cluster in healthy state`), not Flux. A verify DURING the roll was not run; the 0.4.3 gate behaves the same (a reading of the sources).
  - **A 1-instance profile (`light`) restarts:** no replica to switch to, so the database is away for the restart and the image pull (14.0 s with the image on the node, no bar; one cold pull of the PostgreSQL 18 image took 3m10s). **Before the move,** check that every Cluster is settled: one whose pods do not all report the same configuration hash will not roll (`docs/resilience.md`). Each node hosting a PostgreSQL pod pulls the new image (about 265 MB compressed).
  - **Collation (LAB, then STATIC and Docker).** CloudNativePG initialises with locale C and the six Clusters bootstrap with no locale key; in both labs every database was `C/C/libc` with `datcollversion` NULL before and after. In Docker, a data directory made on Debian 11 and started on Debian 12 raised no warning for C databases; a database with an explicit libc locale raised the version WARNING, and an index on an explicit `sv_SE` collation failed `amcheck`. If a database of yours uses an explicit collation, list its objects with the query of the PostgreSQL documentation (`ALTER COLLATION`) before the move. Mixed replication ran through the roll (LAB) and streamed 20 000 of 20 000 rows with no `pg_rewind` (Docker).
  - **Rollback (LAB, lab 35, `lab27-standard` only).** A rollback of the whole ref move was NOT run, and this entry does not advise it. Reverting the image line on one fixture rolled it back in 46.4 s; a data directory written on Debian 12 starts on Debian 11 (Docker, PostgreSQL 18 only). **A bad tag (injected, INCIDENT):** the roll HALTED after the first replica (`ImagePullBackOff`), the primary kept serving (largest gap 0.44 s), and **reverting the pin alone did NOT recover it**: the stuck replica stayed in `ImagePullBackOff` for 2:42 until its pod was deleted by hand, then the Cluster was healthy 12 s later. **Recovery needs the revert AND a `kubectl delete pod` of the stuck replica.** The plain tags are to be removed from the registry with no date: keep the old image on the nodes.
- **The Alertmanager egress moves: remove your overlay patch, set two keys.** The 0.4.3 documentation told you to patch the `vmalertmanager` CiliumNetworkPolicy in your overlay for another chat or heartbeat host. That policy now has no `toFQDNs` rule, so **remove that patch**. Set `ALERT_CHAT_HOST` and `ALERT_HEARTBEAT_HOST` in the `cluster-identity` ConfigMap of `flux-system` instead (host name only; change the host and the secret URL together); with the defaults, `hooks.slack.com` and `hc-ping.com`, you do nothing. **The infrastructure repository does not write the two keys from the tfvars yet**; nothing there removes a key you set by hand (a reading), and a cluster built from scratch gets none: set them after the install. Between the removal of the rule from `vmalertmanager` and its re-creation by `observability-alert-egress` (a requeue of 30 s by default) the two hosts are not allowed. **Measured only partly (LAB, n=1):** the largest Watchdog gap was 210 s, on the lab's stand-in receiver; the swap on the by-FQDN path to a real host was not measured. Keep your heartbeat grace period as `docs/alert-delivery.md` says, above 5 minutes (the ping gap is 4 minutes at most; the largest gap read in the lab was the 210 s above).
- **One more Kustomization, two vmalert pods re-created, a changed alert chain.** `observability-alert-egress` makes the management render 41 Kustomizations (40 before) and the whole DAG 43 (42 before); a cluster with the forge ON read 43 of 43 at the 0.4.3 arm and **44 of 44 after the move**, one WITHOUT the forge should read 42 of 42 (derived, not observed). The notifier arguments change, so the operator re-creates the two vmalert pods (within about 16 s in both labs; in lab 36 during the `forgejo-db` roll, no effect seen). `VMAlertNotifierErrors` (critical) now means every address fails and no longer carries the `addr` label (a route, silence or inhibit rule that matches on it must change); `VMAlertNotifierPodErrors` (warning, 30 minutes) is new. The two pod names resolved from a vmalert pod in the lab; if they do not on your cluster, vmalert delivers to no Alertmanager and `VMAlertNotifierErrors` fires after 10 minutes (check with `getent hosts` on `vmalertmanager-alertmanager-0.vmalertmanager-alertmanager.services-observability.svc` from a vmalert pod; not run in the lab). If you change the Alertmanager `replicaCount` in an overlay, change the notifier list with it.
- **New alerts, scripts, Pooler pods and Gateway listeners.** `RestoreTestNeverReported` also watches the dump-load test (`kind="dump"`): on a cluster whose dump-load test has never reported it fires one hour after the move (its CronJob is already older than three days; the alert is right). `ForgejoScrapeDown` (warning) exists only with the forge brick; neither alert was seen firing. `backup-verify-scripts` and `backup-restore-scripts` change name: the `backup-verify-exporter` Deployment restarts once and the CronJobs take the new scripts at their next run; the OpenBao snapshot CronJob spec changed, nothing rolls. Pooler pods of a profile that has one are expected to be re-created for the init limit (a reading: the labs' Poolers were by-hand fixtures). `deny-metrics-paths` now names ports 80 and 443 (apps #192): if an overlay of yours adds a listener to `openaether-gateway` on another port, add that port to `ports` of the policy (STATIC). **Nothing else needs doing:** the scan, `check-vex.py`, `vuln-issue.sh`, Renovate and the documentation are repository-side; the restic statement and the Longhorn ignore entry end on 2026-12-04 and must be re-read or removed before then.

### Known open (0.4.4; limited to the defects this release touches; one line each)

- **apps #210 and `docker.io/longhornio/csi-provisioner:v5.3.0`.** The sidecar comes from the Longhorn chart 1.12.1; its 2 CRITICAL are in "Fixed". After the release the weekly scan is expected to go red on it and `vuln-issue.sh` to open `[vuln] docker.io/longhornio/csi-provisioner:v5.3.0` (predicted from the local read; the Actions scan has not run). The owner accepted it on 2026-11-08 as a known open item until the Longhorn chart ships a newer sidecar (Renovate bumps the chart); it has no VEX statement and no tag override. The Barman Cloud plugin sidecar image is still outside the matrix; apps #208 (a scan that did not run is retried by nobody) is not built.
- **apps #162, #163:** replaced and rolled in two labs (n=1, moving ref); they close only when the release names them and the Actions scan of the two references is green, which has not run.
- **Delivered and read once at most (n=1), open until a second reader and the owner close them:** apps #207 (failing-send path not exercised; **the Flux notification Provider posts to one Alertmanager pod**, no issue yet); apps #146 (the infrastructure repository does not write the two host keys, no issue yet in dis-bzh/OpenAether-infra; by-FQDN path and swap gap not measured); apps #192, apps #74, apps #188; apps #203 (never seen firing); apps #204 (does not hold an image roll, 2 of 2 labs); apps #205 (mitigated, the peak never read).
- **Also open:** apps #190 (items 1 and 3 delivered offline; item 4, apps #189: `status.stoppedAt` set on all 14 plugin Backups read); apps #94 (`node_timex_sync_status` read 1 on 9 of 9 nodes and the runbook says so; neither rule seen firing, no skew induced); apps #82 (no Kyverno alert shipped); apps #36 (the page says the plugin was not re-measured on operator 1.30.1; lab 35 read 8 promotes with the plugin 1.30.0 on operator 1.30.1, rc 0 and switched over each time, n=8; the plugin this repository pins and installs (1.30.1) was not run; the caveat stays as written); apps #130 (the PKI policy leg is an owner decision touching `configure.sh`); apps #213 (two sites deferred: editing them recreates running objects); apps #199 (the restic statement and the Longhorn ignore entry end on 2026-12-04; `check-vex.py` turns red that day for the ignore entry); apps #201 (sweeps on clusters installed from the released 0.4.3 tags read 0 bodies, first hop only; the close is the owner's).
- **Lab findings kept as drafts, not issues (one theme per release, inflow rule):** a Flux Ready waiting for an image roll differed between two labs (a race is the hypothesis, not isolated), and the roll order is not fixed; `observability-vm-customresources` fails and retries instead of waiting for the operator's CRDs (about 2 minutes at the lab 35 installs, 15 minutes in lab 36 while the operator chart was late); the Grafana first-migration replica race (two restarts on lab 35 cluster B); the install depends on github.com release assets (five charts); the cold-pull storm of the first 10 minutes and the 330 MB helper image of the Zitadel setup Job (performance, 0.5.0).
- **A comment older than the labs, left alone on purpose:** `apps/base/services-gateway/authorizationpolicy-deny-metrics.yaml` still says "NOT RUN on a cluster" (lab 35 move 1 read the sweep and the Istio warnings, see "Security"); a manifest file is not changed after the proven head, so the comment goes in 0.4.5. The "Security" bullet wins.
- **PostgreSQL follow-up, no issue by decision:** CloudNativePG supports a major upgrade only between images of the same distribution, so the Debian 12 base also binds the later PostgreSQL 16 to 17 or 18 move of `grafana-db` and `forgejo-db`.

## [0.4.3] — 2026-11-05

0.4.3 is a **security hygiene release** of 0.4.2, paired with **OpenAether-infra 0.4.3** (Flux 2.10.0 and the move procedure are in that repository). Its main change closes a defect that the cloud proof of this release found: **a forge behind the app load balancer answered `https://forge.<domain>//metrics` with its Prometheus metrics, with no credential, at 0.4.2 (measured on a cluster; 0.4.1 has the same `apps/` tree and was not run)** (F1). The 0.4.1 and 0.4.2 notes did not see it, because their sweeps had no forge host: see the correction under "Security (0.4.3)". The rest is image and waiver hygiene, a scan process that no longer reports a scan that did not run, and a health gate on the `cnpg` Kustomization. Durations are minutes:seconds (`13:05` is 13 min 05 s). What this release is, from the entries below:

- **Security, F1 (the forge `//metrics` bypass).** Three layers: istiod merges slashes in request paths (mesh-wide), the Gateway policy lists two more paths (`//*` and `/debug//*`), and the forge's `/metrics` asks for a bearer token that the vmagent scrape sends. Measured on Scaleway (3 CP + 6 W, n=1, branch ref, not a tag): 540 probes on a cluster moved from 0.4.2, 310 x 403 and **0 bodies, run twice with identical results**, against 5 bodies (all on the forge host) at the 0.4.2 arm; the same 540 probes on a fresh install, 310 x 403 and 0 bodies. In those sweeps only the forge host answered with metrics (Grafana, Zitadel, OpenBao and the test application: 0 bodies on the first response; the Longhorn opt-in route was not tested, redirects were not followed); a cluster without the forge brick showed no exposure in them. The limits (first hop only, the policy layer never alone on a cluster, spellings beyond the first hop) are in the Security entry.
- **Security, Zitadel.** `zitadel` and `zitadel-login` v4.19.5 to v4.20.1 (Next.js 16.4.3, sharp 0.35.5); the two Next.js VEX statements are dropped (apps #195, closed by this release).
- **Images.** alpine/k8s 1.36.6 on 12 manifests and `restore_longhorn.py`, 1.37.2 for the Zitadel helper, the Grafana sidecar k8s-sidecar 2.14.3, Forgejo 15.0.11 (chart 17.3.2).
- **Waivers.** Re-read and renewed to **2026-12-04**: restic CVE-2026-60002, PyJWT CVE-2026-102268 and the Longhorn Trivy ignore entry SUSE-SU-2026:4619-1; every other statement is gone with the image bumps (apps #196).
- **Scan process.** The `SBOM` workflow logs in to ghcr.io with the workflow token, and a scan that did not start opens no `[vuln]` issue (apps #197); the `release` skill carries the annotated, signed-tag step, and its two copies (this repository's and OpenAether-infra's) are now identical (infra #445).
- **Flux health gate.** The `cnpg` Kustomization waits for its Clusters to be Ready (`healthCheckExprs`, `timeout: 15m`, apps #180).
- **Evidence, and its limit.** Two proof clusters on Scaleway (3 control planes + 6 workers POP2-4C-16G + bastion, fr-par-1/2/3), at the heads **apps e8ca37f and infra d1d268c**, on the MOVING branch ref `refs/heads/claude/0.4.3-rc1`, never on a tag; one run per figure. **What is released is the proven head plus the cut:** the apps tag differs from e8ca37f by this file, `README.md`, `README.fr.md`, `apps/flux/README.md` (documentation: the section on the `cnpg` gate) and `docs/*.md` only (read from `git diff --stat e8ca37f..HEAD` when this entry was written: no manifest, script, workflow, image or `.vex/` file changed, and the only path under `apps/` is that README); the diff is stated again on the commit that is tagged, and this entry is corrected if it differs, or if the tag is pushed on another day (the date of this heading, 2026-11-05, is the day of the proof and of this draft, not the day of a tag: it is provisional until the tag is pushed). **A move or an install pinned to the tag is NOT proven.** Not a compliance claim.
- **For the release notes.** They name `git rev-parse 0.4.3^{commit}` in full: the commit the tag points at, which is the squash-merge commit on `main` when the pull request is squash-merged as for 0.4.1 and 0.4.2 (their tags point at squash commits, eb5c149 and f63f865), and NOT e8ca37f, which is the head of the pull-request branch and is not on `main` after a squash. They show e8ca37f21c57fb87e1a46e6c31edec76ce3151a6 beside it as the proven head, and the output of `git diff --stat e8ca37f21c57fb87e1a46e6c31edec76ce3151a6..0.4.3`, which lists only the files named above. That commit does not exist before the merge, so it is written at the cut and not here; no script of this repository checks it (the `release` skill has the notes carry the commit in full, to be compared by hand with `git rev-parse <tag>^{commit}`).

**Upgrade path.** Supported before 1.0.0: the previous minor only, **0.4.2 to 0.4.3**, paired with **OpenAether-infra 0.4.3**; a 0.4.1 cluster follows the path under `[0.4.2]` first. **Apps is tagged first, and the move is two commands of the infrastructure repository, in this order:** (1) `task cluster-bootstrap-sync` (a dry run that exits 3 while a controller differs from the pin, then the same with `APPLY=1`; Flux 2.9.6 to 2.10.0, infra #444), which moves the Flux controllers in place; (2) `task cluster-up`, with `git_ref = "refs/tags/0.4.3"` in the infrastructure tfvars (its examples carry it; a tfvars that leaves `git_ref` unset inherits the new default), which moves the apps ref. The Flux step comes first because `cluster-up` never touches the Flux controllers of a running cluster: without it they stay at 2.9.6 and `cluster-verify` is red on "Flux controllers run the pinned version" (the infrastructure notes' reading; `cluster-verify` was seen red on a controller one patch behind its pin in the first proof lab of 0.4.1, and `cluster-up` without the Flux step was not run). The lab started `cluster-up` 3 minutes after the Flux sync, once the transient below was over, and never tried it inside the transient. The commands, their order and the guidance when the apply stops part way are in the OpenAether-infra notes; **the move from `refs/tags/0.4.2` to `refs/tags/0.4.3` itself is not shown** (the tag does not exist before the release: the lab followed the branch ref). What the apps ref move changes is, read from the diff and seen on a cluster where stated:
- **istiod** gets `meshConfig.pathNormalization: MERGE_SLASHES`. Istio pushes it to the running proxies: on cluster A the two istiod pods and the three Gateway pods kept their names and restartCount 0, and Envoy printed `merge_slashes: true` (no node rolled).
- **The vault configure Jobs are re-created by Flux** (their script ConfigMap changed, and the image is alpine/k8s 1.36.6) and create `secret/forgejo/metrics` if it is absent, **whether or not the forge is enabled**. With the forge off, the twin ExternalSecret in `services-observability` is still created and the unconditional `VMPodScrape forgejo` reads it, so the vmagent configuration changes (reasoned from the manifests, not run on a profile without the forge).
- **The forge** (only with the brick ON): its Deployment gets `GITEA__metrics__TOKEN` from the Secret `forgejo-metrics`, so **the forge pod rolls** (Recreate: the forge is away while it does) and waits in `CreateContainerConfigError` until that Secret exists. The window, measured in both orders, is in "Security (0.4.3)". The scrape of the forge returns by itself.
- **Zitadel** server and login move to v4.20.1 (the setup hook Job runs `projection_tables`, the repeatable steps and `79_backfill_unique_constraint_owners`), the Grafana sidecar and Forgejo 15.0.11 roll, and the `cnpg` Kustomization gains its gate.
- **A request path with `//` now reaches its service merged** (mesh-wide, see "Security (0.4.3)").

**The Flux-step transient (cluster A, NOMINAL, 3 CP + 6 W).** The apply ended at 11:59:59. From 12:00:04, 7 HelmReleases read `SourceNotReady` (`istio-cni`, `istiod`, `metrics-server`, `zitadel`, `kube-state-metrics`, `loki`, `victoria-metrics-operator`) and were Ready again at 12:00:33 to 12:00:35 (29 to 31 s); `ztunnel` read `DependencyNotReady` (on `istio-cni`) for 60 s, back at 12:01:04; 10 Kustomizations were not Ready at 12:00:06 (33 of 43 Ready), and the last of them was Ready again at 12:02:02 (seen at 12:02:06), so the Kustomization tail lasts about 2 minutes. No continuous probe failed in that window (lab host and in-cluster HTTP, API, Zitadel, Grafana, forge) and the HelmRelease histories are identical before and after: no data or route regression was seen. **Cause not established.** Two readings exist, and neither was tested: the new `source-controller` fetching the HelmChart and HelmRepository artifacts again, and a node-local restart of the Flux controllers making every HelmRelease resolve its source again. The transient is documented in the OpenAether-infra upgrade notes, not removed.

**Cloud proof of the 0.4.3 heads (2026-11-05).** Rung: Scaleway, 3 CP + 6 W POP2-4C-16G + bastion, fr-par-1/2/3, one run per figure (n=1), the moving branch ref, apps e8ca37f and infra d1d268c. Labels: NOMINAL means no fault or provider incident in the step, INCIDENT means one was in it; WORSE is at least 10 s AND at least 10 % worse than the stated baseline; BETTER only without INCIDENT time and with the whole window observed. Every duration below is on that shape and is NOMINAL unless it says INCIDENT. The labs it is compared with (lab 24 and labs 29 to 32) are earlier proof labs of this project: lab 24 ran 3 CP + 4 W; labs 29 to 32 ran 3 CP + 6 W and counted 41 Kustomizations (no forge brick), where the 43 of 43 after the move counts the forge brick too.
- **Cluster A: the move from 0.4.2 (run 2).**
  - *The arm* ran at the 0.4.2 tags, so these rows describe 0.4.2, not 0.4.3: T0 to rc 0 11:25 INCIDENT (the load-balancer ACL trap; the second `cluster-up` alone 7:24 NOMINAL, against 7:33, 7:15 and 7:07: SAME), day-1 3:37 (lab 32 3:18: SAME; lab 30 2:44: **WORSE**, +32 %; run 1 of this lab at the same 0.4.2 tags, 2:08: **WORSE**, +70 %), 41 of 41 Kustomizations Ready 9:45 after rc 0 (lab 32 9:43: SAME; lower than labs 29 to 31 and not called BETTER), `cluster-verify` 29 passed and 0 failed, the forge pod Ready 79 s after the live patch (run 1 72 s; the 1:17 to 2:33 of `docs/forgejo.md`: SAME), and F1 reproduced: 540 probes, 200 x 403, 5 bodies, all on the forge host.
  - *The plan:* `PLAN_ONLY` with the move tfvars 1:50 (lab 32 1:52: SAME), `0 to add, 3 to change, 0 to destroy`.
  - *The Flux step:* dry run 20 s (exit 3, "the API server accepts 43 object(s)"), `APPLY=1` 27 s (the first move of seven controllers by that command, one minor; the command had run before on one controller put one patch behind by hand, 5 s, in the first proof lab of 0.4.1, and with nothing to apply in an earlier lab). The seven controllers run the new set (helm 1.7.1, image-automation 1.3.0, image-reflector 1.3.0, kustomize 1.10.1, notification 1.10.0, source 1.10.1, source-watcher 2.3.1): spec image AND pod imageID equal `flux-image-digests.lock`; 15 CRDs established, `alerts` and `providers` storedVersions `v1beta3,v1`; the HelmRelease histories are identical before and after; the `cluster-verify` Flux line is green on the new pins (it was already green on the old ones). The transient is above. The child-cluster copy of Flux (`cluster-api-clusters/flux-gotk`) stays at 2.9.6 (apps #103).
  - *The apps-ref move, ONE `cluster-up`:* **13:05 wall, WORSE** against lab 32's 10:30 (+2:35, +24.6 %), cause NOT established; against lab 31 (6:41) and lab 29 (7:06) it is worse by more (WORSE row 1). The host load is a hypothesis and cannot be re-measured: the observers ran on the 2-vCPU lab host, its load was read by hand as about 5.8 at 12:35 but was not archived during the move, and the A2 plan before the move, 1:50, has no load archived. The same `PLAN_ONLY` after the move took 3:10 and, repeated at 12:36:52 after the heavy observers were stopped, 1:43 (lab 32 1:45: SAME; load averages 2.54 / 4.95 / 5.06 before that re-run and 2.61 / 4.25 / 4.79 after it). The move is also not like for like: the earlier moves (lab 29 and lab 31, 0.4.0 to 0.4.1; lab 32, 0.4.1 to 0.4.2) changed no image of Zitadel, Forgejo or Grafana, and this one changes Flux, Zitadel, the Grafana sidecar, Forgejo and the Jobs. 43 of 43 Kustomizations Ready 3:34 to 3:40 after the ref switch (lab 32 4:05, lab 29 3:49, lab 31 4:10: SAME; those baselines count 41 Kustomizations, without the forge brick). The post-move `PLAN_ONLY` 3:10 against 1:45 in lab 32: **WORSE**; the re-run with the observers stopped, 1:43, is SAME. `cnpg` read `DependencyNotReady` from 12:13:39 to 12:15:44 (the dependency cascade of a ref move); **its health gate was NOT exercised by this move** (no Cluster changed).
  - *F1 after the move:* 540 probes (54 spellings x 5 hosts x 2 listeners), 310 x 403, 0 bodies, run twice with identical results; all 110 probes of the slash family (11 spellings x 5 hosts x 2 listeners) are 403.
  - *Zitadel* v4.19.5 to v4.20.1: setup Job 19 s (only `projection_tables`, six repeatable steps and `79_backfill_unique_constraint_owners`), HelmRelease Ready 4:15 after the ref switch (lab 24 4:12, on 3 CP + 4 W, of which 2:05 was a wait for the Flux dependency chain: SAME), the pins equal the declared digests, 0 pods left on v4.19.5, a session created before the move reads back, the PAT works, TLS 1.3 to CNPG works, authorize 302 then loginname 200. One 503 on 1 of 2,188 Zitadel probes at 12:16:34, cause not shown.
  - *Jobs on alpine/k8s 1.36.6:* both vault Jobs re-created by force inside their TTL (new uids); six CronJobs run by hand, all Complete: five of the six that use alpine/k8s as their main image (all but `cnpg-pitr-restore-test`, not run) and `etcd-snapshot`, whose init container uses it; the escrow check passed vacuously, "no escrow to attest". *Grafana sidecar 2.14.3:* a 61 s roll, restarts 0, a ConfigMap labelled `grafana_dashboard` hot-reloaded, `up` 4 of 4. *Forgejo 15.0.11:* six of six containers on the new image.
  - *Zero loss:* **226,978 acknowledged ids over 4 writers** (zitadel-db direct, lab27-ha direct, lab27-ha through the Pooler, lab27-light), **0 missing**, the ids kept outside the cluster; longest write gap 2.77 s (lab 32 1.26 s, lab 31 0.53 s: under the 10 s floor, SAME); 2 in-cluster API probe failures (a 3.0 s gap). After the move 5,000 seed rows with the same md5 on both product databases, OpenBao `secret/lab27/seed` still version 1. Restarts after the install: 21 (**WORSE** against lab 31's 10, +110 %; lab 32 34 and OVH 26 were higher); 12 of them Longhorn, no cause found, no issue yet. Alerts were sampled at two moments only: at 11:51:30, before the move, Watchdog, 4 `KyvernoPolicyViolation` on the lab fixture namespaces and `FluxKustomizationArtifactfailed` from the lab's throwaway error-event Kustomization; at 12:26:28, after the move, Watchdog alone.
- **Cluster B: a fresh install with the forge ON.**
  - *T0 to rc 0* **18:17 INCIDENT over 4 invocations** (worker-5 created `stopped` in fr-par-3, `out of stock` twice, the load-balancer ACL trap): **WORSE** against lab 29 10:13 (+79 %), lab 30 10:54 (+68 %) and lab 32 12:56 (+41 %); lab 31's 20:25 had the same incident classes. Provider capacity and a known trap (infra #361), not a product claim. The Talos image was built in this run (44 s); lab 29's run did not build one, labs 30 to 32 did.
  - *Flux 2.10.0 from the Talos inline manifest:* 7 of 7 controllers, spec image AND pod imageID equal to `flux-image-digests.lock`; 15 CRDs established, storedVersions `v1`; `cluster-verify` 32 passed and 0 failed, then 33 and 0, with the "moving ref" warning.
  - *Zitadel's first install at v4.20.1* on an empty database, through Flux: init 7 s, setup 22 s, 170 migrations, `status.failed` 0, no hook stuck, HelmRelease Ready 1:07, 0 pods on v4.19.5, authorize 302 then loginname 200, the PAT and a session work. There is no baseline for a first install at this version.
  - *The `cnpg` gate, an OBSERVATION (n=1), NOT a proof that the race of lab 32 is fixed:* the gate waited ("Health check passed in 6m0.4s") and `cnpg` was Ready 1 s after the last Cluster (lab 32 lost the race: Ready in 40.75 s with empty Clusters). Clusters 3 of 3 at 5:39 and 6:05 after `cnpg` went Progressing (compared with 5:50 to 6:13: SAME). 41 of 41 Ready 9:52 after rc 0 (lab 32 9:43 and cluster A's arm 9:45: SAME; labs 29 to 31, 11:47 to 12:16, are not called BETTER: the known `observability-vm-customresources` fault of 62 s lies inside the window and its first 1:21 was not observed). Grafana restarted 3 times on a replica race for the first migration lock (`failed to obtain lock` x2, `duplicate key` x1, 0 `connection refused`).
  - *F1 on a fresh install:* 540 probes, 310 x 403, 0 bodies, the same code table as cluster A after its move. The forge was enabled by the documented live patch at rc 0, 14 s BEFORE the vault Jobs were first created (favourable by construction): the pod started at the first try with 0 `CreateContainerConfigError` and was Ready 101 s after the pod was created (not comparable with the 79 s and 72 s patch-to-Ready figures); `up` = 1 with the bearer; push, clone and `feat%2Fx` work; a repository named `metrics` still reaches Forgejo.
  - *Day-1* 1:22 (lab 30 2:44 to lab 29 4:00: BETTER by the rule, cause not isolated; the host load may have differed: it was 1.18 at the start and 0.71 at the end of this day-1, and the load of cluster A's day-1 was not archived (its 2.5 to 5 was read after the move), so a load confound is possible). Restarts: 15 containers plus 3 init-container restarts (the Pooler's `bootstrap-controller` OOMKilled at its 64 Mi limit, then recovers) = 18, **WORSE** against lab 31's 10 either way (+50 % and +80 %). Alerts at three moments, Watchdog alone at the third only: the first answer, from one Alertmanager pod, was empty (the raw note: the first `vmalert` evaluation, on a 5-minute interval, had not produced Watchdog yet; why the pod answered empty is not shown); the second held four fixture alerts of the lab (`KyvernoPolicyViolation`) and install-time pending alerts (`CnpgMetricsAbsent`, `LokiRetentionNotSwept`, `VMAlertRuleEvaluationErrors`, and a fixture `ContainerCpuThrottled`).
  - *Zero loss:* 64,209 acknowledged ids on 2 writers (zitadel-db direct, lab27-ha direct), 0 missing, longest gap 0.73 s. **No failover ran, so "no split brain" is NOT tested.**
- The unfavourable order of the forge's Secret was tested on cluster B (B6): see "Security (0.4.3)".

**WORSE rows (listed plainly; none is claimed as a product regression, none is hidden; the owner re-decides them at the release gate).**
1. The apps-ref move wall, 13:05 NOMINAL: WORSE against lab 32's 10:30 (+2:35, +24.6 %; lab 32 was itself WORSE, +57 %, against lab 31), lab 31's 6:41 (+6:24, +96 %) and lab 29's 7:06 (+5:59, +84 %); ref moved to rc 0 2:43 against 1:35 and 1:27 (+72 % against the first); launch to ref moved 10:22 against 8:55 (+16 %); the post-move `PLAN_ONLY` 3:10 against 1:45 (1:43 when repeated at 12:36:52 with the observers stopped); cause not established, and not like for like (see the move bullet above).
2. T0 to rc 0, INCIDENT in both: cluster A's arm 11:25 against lab 29's 10:13 (+11.7 %, at the 0.4.2 tags); cluster B 18:17 against 10:13, 10:54 and 12:56 (provider stock-out and the known load-balancer ACL trap, infra #361; lab 29's 10:13 built no image, cluster B's run built it, 44 s).
3. Day-1 of cluster A's arm, 3:37 against lab 30's 2:44 (+32 %) and run 1's 2:08 at the same 0.4.2 tags (+70 %); SAME against lab 32's 3:18.
4. Restarts after the install: 21 (cluster A) and 15 + 3 (cluster B) against lab 31's 10 (lab 32 34 and OVH 26 were higher).
5. A provider incident of this lab, not a product claim: a Scaleway fr-par-3 POP2-4C-16G stock-out blocked one attempt for 33 minutes (11 `cluster-up` attempts, no cluster built).

**Not proven, or not fixed (honest list).** **Not shown:** a Grafana MFA sign-in, that is a sign-in with a second factor (`grafana-oidc` stays in `SecretSyncedError` with an empty `client_id` on a fresh install: known); failover and zone drills, so no split-brain test in this lab; a tag-pinned install or move; a wait of the forge pod beyond 15 minutes in the unfavourable order; the path where the vault Jobs are created fresh after their TTL expired (the move came at +28 minutes and re-created them by force inside the 3600 s TTL); a fresh install where a vault Job is still running when the forge is enabled (cluster B: the live patch came 14 s BEFORE the Jobs were first created; cluster A: the Jobs had finished before the forge Kustomization applied); a 0.4.1 cluster with the forge brick, swept (inferred only, from the empty `git diff 0.4.1..0.4.2` under `apps/` and `scripts/`; only the 0.4.2 tags were run); `cnpg-pitr-restore-test` by hand and the Longhorn canary and restore test (not deployed on this profile); the tag-run scan of the v1.10.1 `kustomize-controller` image (infra #444 stays open, see "Still red" below); the `cnpg` gate on a move that changes a Cluster; the policy layer alone on a cluster; redirects in the sweeps (not followed: 159 of 540 probes answered 301, 302, 303 or 307 in each after-move sweep and in cluster B's, and 257 of 540 at the arm, 196 x 301, 33 x 302, 28 x 303); `%2F`, `%5C`, case and `;param` spellings beyond the first hop; the Longhorn opt-in route and Zitadel's `/debug/metrics` body; the PostgreSQL image bumps (apps #162 and #163, planned for 0.4.4); OVH and Outscale (Scaleway only, by the owner's decision); Flux 2.10.0 as a version string on a live object (shown by the digests equal to the lock, no object carries "2.10.0"). **Unexplained, not described:** the Zitadel 503 above, 3 Zitadel server error lines before the move and 2 after, a 1.17 to 1.22 s write gap on both HA writers at 12:18:40 during the sweep, a Zitadel `/debug%2Fmetrics` answer that went from 301 to 307 between the arm and the move (no body either way), a `cilium-operator` pod in `CreateContainerConfigError` once at install on cluster B. **Not fixed, found by this lab (issue drafts exist; none was filed when this entry was written):** the open spellings; the token in `app.ini` and so in the daily backup of the forge volume; the token in the vmagent configuration Secret; the scrape of the forge in cleartext HTTP; no alert for the forge scrape (`up{forgejo}` was 0 for about 7.5 minutes in the unfavourable order); Alertmanager replica 1 received no alerts; the `forgejo-db` and `forgejo` Kustomizations are Ready before their objects; the Pooler's `bootstrap-controller` OOMKilled at 64 Mi; the Grafana first-migration lock race; the transient after the Flux step (HelmReleases `SourceNotReady` for 29 to 31 s, `ztunnel` `DependencyNotReady` for 60 s, the Kustomization tail about 2 minutes; see "The Flux-step transient" above); a new bucket suffix rebuilds the Talos image; the child-cluster Flux copy (apps #103); nothing orders the forge behind the vault Job. **Still red:** the weekly scan, on the two CNPG PostgreSQL images (certifi GHSA-6q48-6c7w-9gw2, no waiver, apps #162 and #163, 0.4.4). The red scan that `[0.4.2]` listed for the Forgejo image (apps #179) is closed by the image bump (the Forgejo scan is green in the Actions run named under "Fixed"). The one for the Flux image (infra #444, still open) moves with Flux 2.10.0 in the infrastructure repository, and whether its scan is green is to be confirmed by the tag-run scan there: no scan of the new `kustomize-controller` image was run for these notes (only its apk database was read, which lists the fixed brotli-libs). Everything else under "Not proven, or not fixed" in `[0.4.2]` and `[0.4.1]` that this entry does not name still stands. Not a compliance claim.

### Security (0.4.3; rung: a LOCAL RIG (k3s, Istio 1.31.3, Forgejo 15.0.11) and two proof clusters on Scaleway, 3 CP + 6 W, n=1, on the moving branch ref, not a tag; the advisories read; `scripts/check-vex.py` and its fixture tests)
- **F1: the forge `//metrics` bypass (shown on the released 0.4.2 tags, forge brick ON; 0.4.1 inferred, not run; fixed here).**
  - *What was found.* Lab 34 was the first sweep that included the forge host (the released 0.4.2 tags with the opt-in forge brick turned on by the documented live patch). `https://forge.<domain>//metrics`, `///metrics`, `//metrics/`, `//metrics?x=1` and `/\metrics` (Envoy turns the backslash into a slash) answered **HTTP 200 with the forge's metrics**: 66 metric families, 11,153 to 11,157 bytes (counters, build information, the Go version; no repository content, no credential), with no login, from the lab host. Run 1: 270 probes, 1 body. Cluster A at the arm: 540 probes (54 spellings x 5 hosts x 2 listeners), 200 x 403 and 5 bodies, all on the forge host, all on HTTPS. Plain `/metrics`, `/metrics/` and `/debug/metrics` gave 403 on every host; on :80 the `//` spellings gave 301. This was measured at the 0.4.2 tags; the 0.4.2 entry states no runtime change since 0.4.1, and `git diff 0.4.1..0.4.2 -- apps scripts` prints nothing, so 0.4.1 is INFERRED to be the same: it was not run, and no 0.4.1 cluster with the forge brick was swept.
  - *Why the sweeps could not see it.* `deny-metrics-paths` lists four paths literally and nothing merged slashes; Istio's default normalisation removes dot segments and decodes `%6D` but keeps `//`, and Forgejo cleans `//metrics` to `/metrics` itself. The 0.4.1 and 0.4.2 sweeps had no forge host (216 probes on Grafana, Zitadel, OpenBao and a test application: 112 x 403, 0 bodies) because the brick was not deployed in those labs. The other hosts redirect or answer 404 to the same spellings: safe by their own routers, not by design.
  - *The three layers, and their blast radius.* (1) `meshConfig.pathNormalization.normalization: MERGE_SLASHES` in the istiod HelmRelease: runs of slashes are merged before the policy, the routes and the service see the path. **Mesh-wide:** every Envoy istiod configures, that is the Gateway pods (the only way in for customer requests) and the unused `istio-waypoint`; ztunnel is L4 and has no path. (2) Two more paths in `deny-metrics-paths`: `//*` and `/debug//*`, a second mechanism with another failure mode that stays inert while layer 1 merges. (3) A bearer token on the forge's endpoint: `[metrics] TOKEN` from `secret/forgejo/metrics` through two ExternalSecrets (`services-git` for the Deployment, `services-observability` for the scrape), sent by the `VMPodScrape forgejo`; the Secret reference is NOT optional, so a pod of the new spec never starts without the token (a Helm rollback to the token-less spec after the 15 min timeout is reasoned, not run). `scripts/check-gateway-routes.py` models the slash merge (test cases 40 to 62). `DECODE_AND_MERGE_SLASHES` is valid and stricter but is NOT shipped: it forwards decoded slashes and no package-registry client was run against it.
  - *Rung.* On the LOCAL RIG: 530 probes, 5 bodies before and 0 after; a fuzz of 35 slash spellings, 25 leaking before and 0 after; each layer alone stopped every spelling. On the platform: after the move 540 probes, 310 x 403, **0 bodies, run twice with identical results**; on a fresh install 540 probes, 310 x 403, 0 bodies, the same code table. All 110 probes of the slash family (11 spellings x 5 hosts x 2 listeners) are 403 on cluster A after the move, and the code table of cluster B is identical. On cluster A layer 1 was shown alone (about 86 to 89 s before the policy landed; the 89 s mixes the lab host's clock with the cluster's) and layer 3 was shown straight to the pod (no bearer 401, a wrong bearer 401, the right bearer 200; `//metrics` with the Gateway layers alone 403).
  - *Limits.* First hop only: redirects were not followed, 159 of 540 probes in each after-move sweep and in cluster B's (114 x 301, 22 x 302, 22 x 303, 1 x 307) and 257 of 540 at the arm (196 x 301, 33 x 302, 28 x 303), so "0 bodies" on a redirect is true of the first response only. `%2F`, `%5C`, case and `;param` spellings reach the services as written (`/%5Cmetrics` and `/metrics%5C` among them: forge 303, no body); no body was seen, but a service that decodes one of them would leak. **The policy layer was NEVER alone on a cluster** (only on the rig), so "each layer alone" is shown on the rig for all three and on the cluster for layers 1 and 3. The first 403 on the forge's `//metrics` came 68 s after the apps-ref switch, at the same moment as the istiod upgrade: it is not a measure of the xDS push time (the rig saw 2 s with one istiod and one Gateway pod). The Longhorn opt-in route and Zitadel's `/debug/metrics` body were not tested.
  - *Mesh-wide consequence.* A request path with `//` now reaches its service merged. A route whose service gives `//` a meaning (an object key, a URL embedded in a path, a signed path) changes behaviour: reasoned, not run; nothing in this repository routes such a service.
  - *Observed on the cluster* (`docs/forgejo.md` said "read, not observed"): `/data/gitea/conf/app.ini` holds exactly 1 `TOKEN` line, equal to the Secret, so the daily Longhorn backup of the forge volume carries the token; the operator's generated vmagent configuration Secret contains the token; the scrape target's label is `job="services-observability/forgejo"`.
  - *The forge upgrade window, 0.4.2 to 0.4.3 with the forge ON, in both orders.* Nothing orders the forge behind the vault Job, so the order is luck. **Favourable order (cluster A, named row):** the vault Jobs were re-created by Flux inside their TTL (new uids, alpine/k8s 1.36.6) and wrote `secret/forgejo/metrics` at 12:15:27; both ExternalSecrets synced at 12:16:42 to 12:16:45; the forge pod rolled with **0 s in `CreateContainerConfigError` observed** (the watch records changes only, at a resolution of 1 to 3 s: both Secrets existed before the new pod was first seen); the forge was away **50.3 s NOMINAL** (last answer 12:16:45.6, first answer 12:17:35.9, 49 probe failures); the scrape gap was 65 s and returned with no manual step; 1,284 acknowledged pushes, 0 missing, `git fsck` clean. That order was favourable by 76 s and nothing enforces it. **Unfavourable order (cluster B, B6; INCIDENT, induced in the lab, 3 CP + 6 W: Flux suspended, the forge ExternalSecret pointed at a missing key, the upgrade triggered by an added env var; not the sequence of a late vault Job):** with the forge Secret unavailable during a Helm upgrade the pod waited **6:30 in `CreateContainerConfigError`**; the HelmRelease reached `UpgradeSucceeded` with **no rollback** (`timeout: 15m` is set in 0.4.3; Flux's default is 5 minutes and Helm retries or rolls back after it: reasoned, not run); the Deployment kept `GITEA__metrics__TOKEN` in 37 of 37 polls; the pod started by itself 19 s after the Secret came back. **A wait beyond 15 minutes is not shown.** During that wait `up{forgejo}` was 0 for about 7.5 minutes (90 samples), and no rule names the forge scrape, so no alert could fire (alerts were sampled at two moments only).
  - *The fresh install* (cluster B) is the favourable case by construction: the forge was enabled 14 s before the vault Jobs were first created; the case where a Job is still running is not shown.
- **CORRECTION NOTE for the 0.4.1 and 0.4.2 entries.** Two sentences are concerned. The 0.4.2 entry ("Cloud retest" paragraph) says "On both, `/metrics` and `/debug/metrics` answer 403 on every Gateway host (216 probes, 112 x 403, 0 bodies)": it covered the four hosts swept (Grafana, Zitadel, OpenBao, a test application) and was wrong for the forge, which was not deployed in those labs and never in the sweep. The 0.4.1 entry (the "shared Gateway refuses" bullet) says that the spellings the policy does not match "reach the service; none served metrics there": it was true of the hosts swept, and its Limits clause already said "the forge's route was not deployed in the labs, so the forge is NOT shown", but a reader could take it as covering every host. Released 0.4.2 with the forge brick ON answered the spellings above with the forge's metrics (measured); 0.4.1 has the same `apps/` tree (`git diff 0.4.1..0.4.2` touches nothing under `apps/` or `scripts/`), so it is INFERRED exposed, not shown: it was not run, and no 0.4.1 cluster with the forge brick was swept. In the sweeps only the forge host answered with metrics (Grafana, Zitadel, OpenBao and the test application: 0 bodies on the first response; the Longhorn opt-in route was not tested, redirects were not followed); a cluster without the forge brick showed no exposure in them. Fixed in 0.4.3. (The same sentences are quoted in the "Correction (added in 0.4.3)" line at the end of the `[0.4.2]` and `[0.4.1]` sections.)
- **Zitadel and zitadel-login v4.20.1 (apps #195; closes the Next.js waivers).** Both pinned by tag and index digest (`zitadel` `sha256:4817dbd8...`, `zitadel-login` `sha256:1508e832...`); the login ships Next.js 16.4.3 and sharp 0.35.5, above the fixes of GHSA-673q-mjm6-r5xr and GHSA-2464-fhr4-w9xw (16.4.1). The two VEX statements are **deleted, not renewed**, which ends the "renewed to 2026-12-04" of `[0.4.2]` for them. The Gateway rule that sends `/ui/v2/login/_next/image` to the server stays as defence in depth (the optimizer is still enabled in the build). Cluster evidence is in "Cloud proof" above (the move and the first install); the offline reading, the rollback tried offline and what was not run are in `docs/zitadel-hardening.md`.
- **Waivers re-read and renewed to `Expires: 2026-12-04`, three entries (apps #196).** restic CVE-2026-60002 (openssh in the image's alpine layer, never called; restic 0.19.1 is still the newest tag), PyJWT CVE-2026-102268 in the CNPG PostgreSQL images (imported only by `msal`, not used) and the Longhorn Trivy ignore entry SUSE-SU-2026:4619-1 (libexpat 2.7.1 in all six v1.12.1 images; a reading of package data and file searches, no Longhorn process was run or traced; the image scan sees three of the six). The three statements for `alpine/k8s` (CVE-2025-68121 and certifi) and `k8s-sidecar` (certifi) are deleted with the image bumps, as are the two Next.js ones. From 2026-12-05 `scripts/check-vex.py` fails if the three are not re-read again or removed, and Trivy stops applying the Longhorn entry at 00:00 UTC of its `expired_at` day, so those scans can be red on 2026-12-04 (`docs/supply-chain.md`). **Waivers are not fixes.**
- **Read at the cut (2026-11-05):** `scripts/check-vex.py` passes with 2 statements and the ignore entry, soonest expiry 2026-12-04; with `--today 2026-12-05` it exits 1 and lists exactly the two statements (CVE-2026-60002, CVE-2026-102268) and the ignore entry `SUSE-SU-2026:4619-1`. No Next.js statement exists any more. Not a compliance claim.

### Changed (0.4.3; rung: rendered and scanned offline; the image moves seen on cluster A (the move) and cluster B (the fresh install) where stated in "Cloud proof")
- **Images (apps #196, #179, #195).** `alpine/k8s` 1.36.6 on 12 manifests and in `restore_longhorn.py`, 1.37.2 for the Zitadel helper `tools.kubectl`; the Grafana dashboard sidecar `k8s-sidecar` 2.14.3 by tag and digest, ahead of the chart (its default, 2.14.1, carries a certifi flagged critical; a copy of certifi vendored inside the venv's pip is still in the image, below the fix and not reported); Forgejo 15.0.11 (chart 17.3.2, brotli CVE-2026-111497 gone with the rebuilt Alpine base). A local scan of 2026-11-04 (Trivy 0.69.3 and 0.75.0, amd64 only) and the Actions run named under "Fixed" read no CRITICAL with a fix upstream (the gate of the scan) on them. Zitadel is under Security.
- **The `cnpg` Kustomization has a health gate (apps #180).** `healthCheckExprs` for the `Cluster` kind: a Cluster is Current only when its Ready condition is `True`; `timeout: 15m` covers the databases' first start. Why, the figures and the silent wait of the dependents: `apps/flux/README.md` ("The cnpg gate"), pinned string for string by `scripts/dev/test-flux-health-gates.sh`. Seen once on a fresh install (an observation, not a proof), not exercised by the move: see "Cloud proof".

### Fixed (0.4.3; rung: mocked and offline for the scripts; the login seen in an Actions run on the branch)
- **The scan process (apps #197).** (1) The `SBOM` scan jobs log in to ghcr.io with the workflow token (no `packages` scope: the images are public); an anonymous pull was refused with `TOOMANYREQUESTS` on the 0.4.2 tag run. (2) Exit 1 of the scan is both "findings" and "Trivy stopped before it scanned": a new step tells them apart by the report on disk, and `scripts/ops/vuln-issue.sh` refuses a missing or empty report, so **a scan that did not start fails the job and opens no `[vuln]` issue**. (3) The `VEX statements` job keeps stderr in its report, so a VEX file `check-vex.py` cannot read (exit 2) says why. **Rung, seen in Actions:** workflow run 41927056554, a `workflow_dispatch` on the branch at 9fb9035 (six commits before e8ca37f; none of them touches an image, `.github/` or `.vex/`), 2026-11-04. The step "Log in to ghcr.io" printed `Login Succeeded` (read in three of the eight ghcr scan jobs: both red ones and one green one), all eight ghcr scans ran, and 38 of the 40 scan jobs were green. The two red ones are `ghcr.io/cloudnative-pg/postgresql:16.15` and `:18.6`, one CRITICAL each, certifi GHSA-6q48-6c7w-9gw2 (installed 2026.7.22, fixed in 2026.10.18): known, no waiver, apps #162 and #163, the images are replaced in 0.4.4. None of the eight ghcr scans was refused for the rate limit (six green, two red on a finding with a report); whether the login is what lifted the limit is not shown (one run, a limit reported per runner address). **Not run:** the tag-push attach with the new login (the `Attach to a draft release` job was skipped in that run, as it is on a dispatch without a tag), and a `[vuln]` issue being opened or closed by the new steps (a dispatch on a branch opens none, by design).
- **The release skill carries the signed-tag step (infra #445).** `.claude/skills/release/SKILL.md` now holds the annotated, signed tag step with the release key of the signer table in OpenAether-infra's `docs/signed-commits.md`, the check before the push (the script exists in the infrastructure repository only; here by hand), and says the `tags` ruleset of either repository blocks moving or deleting a tag. **The two copies of the skill (this repository's at e8ca37f and OpenAether-infra's at d1d268c) are now byte for byte identical** (same SHA-256, compared with `git show` and `sha256sum`; `check-skill-parity.sh` itself was not run, it needs the two checkouts side by side under their repository names). Text only, nothing run.

### Documented (0.4.3; rung: text only, the observations are those of the two proof clusters above)
- **`docs/forgejo.md`:** the items that were "read, not observed" are observed (the token in `app.ini`, in the vmagent configuration Secret, the scrape job label), the forge upgrade window is measured in both orders (its "0 s in `CreateContainerConfigError`" carries the watch resolution of 1 to 3 s), and the sentence that the lab must measure it is gone. **`docs/public-exposure.md`:** the results on the platform, their limits and the "Not shown" list. **`docs/key-inventory.md`:** the token row says observed. **`docs/supply-chain.md`:** the #197 rung. **`docs/zitadel-hardening.md`:** what was seen on a cluster at v4.20.1 and what was still not run. **`apps/flux/README.md`** (documentation under `apps/`; no manifest changed): the section on the `cnpg` gate reports the one observation of the gate (cluster B, n=1, the gate waited 6:00.4) as an observation and not a proof, and keeps "Ready at the first phase" and the cause of lab 32 as read or inferred, not shown.

## [0.4.2] — 2026-11-04

0.4.2 is a **patch release** of 0.4.1, paired with **OpenAether-infra 0.4.2**. Its one change is how a release is cut: the `SBOM` workflow now attaches the inventory (`openaether-apps-<tag>.cdx.json`) to a **draft** release, because an immutable release takes no file once it is published (0.3.2, 0.4.0 and 0.4.1 went out without it; infra #395). **No runtime change since 0.4.1.** What this release is, from the entries below:

- **Fixed (release process):** the `SBOM` workflow starts on the pushed release tag, creates the draft release when there is none (or uses the one you made with `--draft`), attaches the inventory to it, and refuses a release that is already published, in red, with a message. On publish the same workflow only checks that the file is there. The old trigger, `release: published`, failed for 0.3.2, 0.4.0 and 0.4.1 (the message "Cannot upload assets to an immutable release" was read in the 0.3.2 run, infra #395; the runs of 0.4.0 and 0.4.1 failed in the same job).
- **New procedure** (the `release` skill and `docs/supply-chain.md` hold it): push the signed tag; read the `SBOM` run and wait for its `Attach to a draft release` job to be green; write the notes and publish with `gh release edit <tag> --notes-file notes.md --draft=false`. Never `gh release create` without `--draft`. Fallback if the tag-push run did not attach the file: `gh release create <tag> --draft --verify-tag --title <tag> --notes-file notes.md`, then `gh workflow run sbom.yml -f tag=<tag>`.
- **No runtime change, checked at the cut:** the diff since 0.4.1 is eight files, the `SBOM` workflow (`.github/workflows/sbom.yml`), the `release` skill (`.claude/skills/release/SKILL.md`), `docs/supply-chain.md`, `README.md`, `README.fr.md`, this file, one sentence of `docs/zitadel-hardening.md` and the VEX file `.vex/openaether-apps.openvex.json` (two statements renewed on 2026-11-04; Trivy and `scripts/check-vex.py` read it, no cluster does); nothing under `apps/` or `scripts/`, no other workflow. The 42 bricks that `scripts/dev/render-dag.py` renders from the 0.4.1 tag and from this tree are identical (`diff -r` prints nothing). These statements and the date of this heading (and of `README.md` and `README.fr.md`) were checked at the cut, 2026-11-04; they are checked again on the commit that is tagged (`git diff --stat 0.4.1..HEAD`, the render comparison, the date), and corrected here if the tag is pushed on another day or `main` moved.
- **Security (waivers, no runtime change):** the two Next.js VEX statements on `zitadel-login` were re-read and renewed to 2026-12-04; five other statements and one Trivy ignore entry expire on 2026-11-20 and are not renewed. See "Security (0.4.2)" below.
- **Evidence, and its limit:** see "Rung of the SBOM fix" below. The 0.4.2 release is the first live proof of the procedure.

**Upgrade path.** Supported before 1.0.0: the previous minor only, **0.4.1 to 0.4.2**, paired with **OpenAether-infra 0.4.2** (apps ref moved by `task cluster-apps-ref`); a 0.4.0 cluster follows the path under `[0.4.1]` first. Nothing to run by hand. Read from the diff and from the rendered output (above): no manifest, CRD, script, CronJob or database spec changed, so the Flux sync after the ref move has nothing new to apply from this repository.

**Cloud retest of the 0.4.2 heads (2026-11-04):** a 0.4.1 cluster moved to apps 535813c and infra a925703 in ONE `task cluster-up`, on Scaleway (3 CP + 6 W, rc 0, 10 min 30 s NOMINAL, 0 failed probes, 102,218 acknowledged writes and none lost, no node rolled) and on OVH (3 CP + 6 W, rc 0, 9 min 04 s NOMINAL, 201,797 acknowledged writes and none lost, no node rolled). On both, `/metrics` and `/debug/metrics` answer 403 on every Gateway host (216 probes, 112 x 403, 0 bodies), the dump-load restore test skips then passes, and `cluster-verify` is green. The Scaleway move was WORSE in wall time than the previous retest (10 min 30 s NOMINAL against 6 min 41 s NOMINAL, one run, cause not isolated). It proves nothing about the SBOM workflow (first live proof: the 0.4.2 tag), followed the branch refs, and ran no failover drill. The full list, with every figure and its label, is in the `[0.4.2]` section of the OpenAether-infra CHANGELOG.

**Rung of the SBOM fix.** It is stated in one place, `docs/supply-chain.md` ("Rung of the attach"), so that it is corrected in one place. In short: only the upload step was replayed, by hand with the maintainer's own `gh` credentials from a shell on the maintainer's machine, **not as an Actions job**; everything else is not run, the `GITHUB_TOKEN` path inside Actions, the tag-push trigger and the job that runs on publish among it. **The 0.4.2 release is the first live proof.** If the attach fails there, the release is still a draft and nothing is lost: use the fallback (the procedure above; `docs/supply-chain.md`), and publish only once the file is on the draft.

**Not proven, or not fixed (honest list).** Not run: everything under "Not run" in the rung of `docs/supply-chain.md` (the `GITHUB_TOKEN` path inside Actions, the tag-push trigger, the draft creation, the job that runs on publish, the `workflow_dispatch` fallback). **Not fixed:** the weekly vulnerability scan is red, on three open issues here and one on the infrastructure side. Apps #179 (the Forgejo image `15.0.10-rootless`: CRITICAL `brotli-libs` CVE-2026-111497, installed 1.2.0-r0, listed as fixed in 1.2.1-r0); apps #162 and #163 (the CNPG PostgreSQL images 16.15 and 18.6, still failing in the runs of 2026-11-03); OpenAether-infra #444 (the pinned Flux kustomize-controller image: the same CVE as apps #179, upstream fix needed). The run started by the 0.4.2 tag will therefore be red on those scans; by the job graph the attach job needs only the inventory, so it can still be green (not observed yet). The VEX waivers with a date are in "Security (0.4.2)" below: two statements (Next.js on `zitadel-login`) renewed to **2026-12-04**, five other statements and one Trivy ignore entry that expire on **2026-11-20** and are not renewed here (the check turns red from 2026-11-21). The Zitadel bump to v4.20.1 is not done: 0.4.2 has no runtime change. The releases 0.3.2, 0.4.0 and 0.4.1 stay without an inventory (an immutable release cannot be given one); `docs/supply-chain.md` has the two commands that rebuild one from a checkout of the tag. Everything under "Not proven, or not fixed" in `[0.4.1]` still stands: this release touches none of it. Not a compliance claim.

### Fixed (0.4.2; rung: see `docs/supply-chain.md`; no runtime change)
- **The `SBOM` workflow attaches the inventory to a DRAFT release (infra #395).** Releases have been immutable since 2026-10: once a release is published GitHub refuses every upload to it, and the old trigger (`release: published`) uploaded after the publish, so it failed (with "Cannot upload assets to an immutable release" in the 0.3.2 run, infra #395; the 0.4.0 and 0.4.1 runs failed in the same job) and 0.3.2, 0.4.0 and 0.4.1 have no `openaether-apps-<tag>.cdx.json`. The workflow now (1) starts on a pushed tag matching `[0-9]*` and builds the inventory from that tag; (2) in the job `Attach to a draft release`, lists the releases (drafts included: the by-tag endpoint does not return them), creates a draft with `--verify-tag` when the tag has no release, uses it when it is a draft, and exits 1 with an explicit message when it is already published; (3) uploads `openaether-apps-<tag>.cdx.json` with `--clobber`; (4) on `release: published`, builds and scans nothing (the scan job has an explicit `if` for it) and, besides the VEX check that runs on every event, only checks (job `The published release carries the SBOM`) that the asset is listed, failing with the list of assets otherwise. `workflow_dispatch` takes an optional `tag` (empty: a plain run on the branch, as before) for the fallback. The attach job needs the inventory job only, not the image scans. Documented in `docs/supply-chain.md` (procedure, fallback, rung, open items) and in the `release` skill.
- **Rebuilding an inventory by hand (`docs/supply-chain.md`).** The page gives the two commands of the workflow's inventory step (`render-dag.py`, then `gen-sbom.py --rendered <dir> --helm --version <tag> --output ...`) for the three releases that have none. Run on a copy of the 0.4.1 tag on 2026-11-04 with fresh helm directories and the network: rc 0, CycloneDX 1.5, 56 components, 40 images with `--helm` (32 components, 16 images without it). The page's header no longer says that nothing has run on GitHub.

### Security (0.4.2; rung: the pinned image read and run offline, the advisories read, `scripts/check-vex.py` and its fixture tests; no cluster, no lab; no runtime change)
- **Two VEX statements on `zitadel-login` renewed, new `Expires: 2026-12-04`** (GHSA-673q-mjm6-r5xr, GHSA-2464-fhr4-w9xw; `.vex/openaether-apps.openvex.json`). They were due to expire on 2026-11-05. They were re-read on 2026-11-04, not renewed by moving the date, on the image the repository pins (`zitadel-login` v4.19.5, digest `sha256:2f8e5767...`; the amd64 image was read and run offline, the arm64 image of the same index was not read):
  - the image is unchanged: Next.js 16.3.6, alpine 3.24.2, musl node 24.22.0, `sharp` 0.35.4;
  - GHSA-673q (needs Cache Components and a `use cache` function returning typed arrays; next >= 16.0.0 and <= 16.4.0, fixed in 16.4.1): `cacheComponents`, `useCache` and `ppr` are false in `required-server-files.json`, and the built chunks hold no `RSC_SERVER_CACHE` reference. These flags are the control. A search for the `use cache` string is not a detector (the compiler consumes the directive) and is not relied on;
  - GHSA-2464 (libvips memory bug in `sharp` on glibc; next >= 10.0.0 to <= 15.5.27 and >= 16.0.0 to <= 16.4.0, fixed in 16.4.1): only the glibc `sharp` packages are present (`@img/sharp-linux-x64` and its libvips, no linuxmusl package), and `require('sharp')` from Next.js's directory fails with "Could not load the sharp module using the linuxmusl-x64 runtime". The advisory page does not say whether musl is affected: the statement rests on `sharp` not loading, and stays fragile (reachable if the image is rebuilt on a glibc base). The Gateway rule that sends `/ui/v2/login/_next/image` to the server (404) is still in `httproute.yaml` and `scripts/dev/test-zitadel-login-route.py` passes.
- **The date that forces the bump is 2026-12-04.** A fixed image exists since 2026-11-03: `zitadel-login` v4.20.1 (index digest `sha256:1508e83275f1471b20ed42b159174e6b3e10e6c2481e64c0aee7ed5bfeeec18b`, the form the repository pins) ships Next.js 16.4.3 and `sharp` 0.35.5 on alpine 3.24.3, still musl. The first drop condition of the `[0.4.1]` entry, a tag with Next.js >= 16.4.1, is therefore met, and the waiver stands on this 30-day renewal alone. **v4.20.1 is NOT bumped here** (0.4.2 has no runtime change). It is to be bumped, `zitadel` and `zitadel-login` together and pinned by tag and index digest, with a cluster proof, in the next release that changes the runtime; the two statements are dropped then. If that does not land before 2026-12-04, `scripts/check-vex.py` fails from 2026-12-05.
- **Five statements and one Trivy ignore entry expire on 2026-11-20 and are NOT renewed here:** restic `CVE-2026-60002` (openssh), `alpine/k8s` `CVE-2025-68121` (Go stdlib) and `GHSA-6q48-6c7w-9gw2` (certifi), CNPG `CVE-2026-102268` (pyjwt), `k8s-sidecar` `GHSA-6q48-6c7w-9gw2` (certifi), and the ignore entry `SUSE-SU-2026:4619-1` (libexpat in the Longhorn images, `.github/trivy-ignore.yaml`). From 2026-11-21 `scripts/check-vex.py` fails every pull request and the `VEX statements` job, and Trivy no longer ignores the Longhorn entry, so those scans go red again. Each one needs its own re-read against the current tags, or an image fix, before then.
- **Read at the cut (2026-11-04):** `scripts/check-vex.py` passes; with `--today 2026-11-30` it exits 1 and lists exactly the five statements and the ignore entry above, and neither renewed statement; with `--today 2026-12-05` the two renewed statements are expired. Not a compliance claim.

Correction (added in 0.4.3): "On both, `/metrics` and `/debug/metrics` answer 403 on every Gateway host (216 probes, 112 x 403, 0 bodies)" above meant the four hosts swept (Grafana, Zitadel, OpenBao, a test application); the forge was not among them, and **released 0.4.2 with the forge brick ON answered `https://forge.<domain>//metrics` and four other spellings with the forge's metrics**, with no credential (measured at the 0.4.2 tags). In the sweeps only the forge host answered with metrics; a cluster without the forge brick showed no exposure in them (Grafana, Zitadel, OpenBao and the test application: 0 bodies on the first response; the Longhorn opt-in route was not tested, redirects were not followed). Fixed in 0.4.3: see "Security (0.4.3 ...)" and its CORRECTION NOTE under `[0.4.3]`.

## [0.4.1] — 2026-11-03

0.4.1 is a **patch release** of 0.4.0, paired with **OpenAether-infra 0.4.1** (most of the work, the disaster-recovery chain, the tunnels and the roll gates, is in that repository). No new scope. What the apps side fixes, from the entries below:

- **Security:** the shared Gateway refuses `/metrics` and `/debug/metrics` for every route. Open at 0.4.0 (earlier releases were not checked): Grafana's `/metrics` answered HTTP 200 with 4,516 lines and no login (the app load balancer has no source ACL by default), and so did Zitadel's `/debug/metrics` (a third lab read it). With the policy a proof lab on Scaleway (3 CP + 6 W) read no Prometheus body and a 403 on the exact paths, on a fresh install and on a cluster moved from 0.4.0 (figures, hosts, the spellings that still pass and what was not shown, the forge among it: `docs/public-exposure.md`). A route of yours that serves `/metrics` on purpose gets the 403 too.
- **Restore tests:** the CNPG restore tests skip, with a reason, before the first completed base backup (#169) and before the first `cnpg-dump` (the dump flow #169 did not cover: a fresh install held a critical `RestoreTestFailed` for hours). The second was seen on a cluster twice (SKIP x4, then PASS x4), once on a 0.4.0 cluster moved to 0.4.1 (third lab, Scaleway, 3 CP + 6 W, NOMINAL), where the `RestoreTestFailed` it had been holding cleared 5 min 20 s to 5 min 35 s after the first run of the new test; the first only on a rebuilt cluster whose Backups the lab had deleted by hand.
- **Backups:** `backup-verify` says what an end WAL that is not archived means and what to do; the README says a completed Backup is not a restore point until that segment is archived.
- **Alert:** `CnpgWalNeverArchived`, a **proxy** (it reads the archiver counters, not the `ContinuousArchiving` condition) for a Cluster that archives nothing and fails now; it fires about 10 to 15 minutes after creation, not at once (apps half of infra #430, which stays open).
- **Documentation:** after an OpenBao raft restore check all three pods (#184); `iam-admin` and `iam-admin-pat` do not exist after a DR rebuild, so the documented Zitadel break-glass is gone (what was read of the restored identity data, and what was not: `docs/zitadel-hardening.md`); what the 0.4.1 proof labs showed, with the WORSE rows (`docs/resilience.md`); the Forgejo brick, the first Pooler login, the `FailoverQuorum` object, R4 on OVH and the 13 s load-balancer limit (apps #176, #177, #178, #182).
- **Evidence:** three proof labs on Scaleway (3 CP + 6 W). The first, at an earlier head, ran the 0.4.0 to 0.4.1 move and the drills and found five defects, two in this repository (`/metrics`, the dump-load test) and three in the DR chain of OpenAether-infra. The second, at the release-candidate heads, ran a fresh install, the `/metrics` probes, the dump-load skip and a DR rebuild with writers (no acknowledged id lost; figures: `docs/resilience.md`, "What the 0.4.1 proof labs showed"); it did not run the move. **The third, at the lab-proven heads (apps d028aed, infra 67be1e8; see "Move proof"), ran the move again**, from a fresh 0.4.0 install, with probes and acknowledged-id writers, and read `/metrics` and the dump-load test before and after it (figures: "Move proof" below). The new `backup-verify` failure message was not triggered there (the verify passed twice): it stays tested mocked, not on a cluster. Every line states its rung.

**Upgrade path.** Supported before 1.0.0: the previous minor only, **0.4.0 to 0.4.1**, paired with **OpenAether-infra 0.4.1** (apps ref moved by `task cluster-apps-ref`). Nothing to run by hand (read from the diff since 0.4.0): no CRD, no database spec change, no `cnpg-presync`. The new Gateway policy applies at the next Flux sync, not when the move command returns (2 min 38 s after the ref move in the third lab, resolution about 6 s, and 71 s after the command returned, NOMINAL; until then Grafana's and Zitadel's metrics stay open: see the upgrade note under Security); the restore-test and verify scripts change at the next CronJob run.

**Move proof, 0.4.0 to 0.4.1.** Run in one invocation in two proof labs (Scaleway, 3 CP + 6 W). Every duration in this paragraph and in the next is NOMINAL unless it says INCIDENT. The first ran it at an earlier head, 7 apps commits after 0.4.0: 7 min 06 s, 41 of 41 Kustomizations Ready 3 min 49 s after the ref move and 2 min 17 s after the command returned, no failed probe, 37,177 acknowledged ids and none lost. **The third ran it on a cluster installed from the 0.4.0 tags, at the lab-proven heads (apps d028aed, 42 commits after 0.4.0; infra 67be1e8), and moved Flux onto the branch of the release candidate (`refs/heads/claude/0.4.1-rc1`; the tag did not exist yet, so the move from the tag is not shown):** the command returned 0 after 6 min 41 s, NOMINAL except a local tunnel port held 7.2 s on purpose (one retry after 4 s); no node rolled (the nine boot ids and the restart total, 10, are the same before and after); 41 of 41 Kustomizations Ready on the new revision 4 min 10 s after the ref move, which is 2 min 43 s after the command returned; no failed probe of 560, 560, 1,102 and 1,102 on four vantages; 109,173 acknowledged ids on four writers, pulled to the lab host every 15 s (so recorded outside the cluster) and compared with the database rows, none missing; the database held 373 rows more than were pulled, which the audit reads as writes after the last pull (not checked one by one, not counted). Longest gap between acknowledged writes, the ten parallel slots of each writer merged: 296, 286, 448 and 532 ms on the four writers; one slot of the `zitadel-db` writer alone once waited 5.3 s, inside the command's run and before the ref moved, cause not established. **What is released is the lab-proven head plus commits that touch documentation only:** for apps, after d028aed only `CHANGELOG.md` and `docs/*.md` changed (read from the diff: no manifest, script, test, comment or message text). A later commit that touches anything else is not covered by that run.

**Labels, stated once for this entry and `docs/resilience.md`.** WORSE: a figure both at least 10 s and at least 10 % worse than the run it is compared with. BETTER: both at least 10 s and at least 10 % better, with no INCIDENT time in it. Otherwise SAME. A figure within the measurement resolution of the line is called "at the edge", not given a firm label. One sample per lab. Against the first lab: 6 min 41 s SAME (-25 s, -6 %); **4 min 10 s after the ref move at the edge, resolution about 6 s** (+21 s, +9 %: 2 s under the WORSE line of 4 min 12 s; the observer polled every 5 to 6 s and the ref moved between two samples, so it may be WORSE by that margin); **2 min 43 s after the command returned WORSE (+26 s, +19 %)**, cause not isolated (this move is larger: 42 apps commits against 7); probes and loss SAME. The plan-only run (`PLAN_ONLY=1`), 1 min 41 s against 1 min 31 s (+10 s, +11 %), is at the edge too (the 10 s floor itself, one sample each) and not like-for-like, since the first lab's plan listed machines it would create and this one planned 3 in-place changes: no firm label (the rule as written reads WORSE). **Two rows of the third lab are not 0.4.1 readings: they ran at the 0.4.0 tags, before the move, so they describe the n-1 arm, and they are not a 0.4.1 regression.** Day-1 (the seeding, PKI and certificate steps after install) took 3 min 19 s there, against 4 min at the 0.4.0 tags in the first lab (BETTER by the rule, same version) and 2 min 44 s at the 0.4.1 release-candidate heads in the second lab: against that 0.4.1 reading the 0.4.0 arm is +35 s (+21 %), which the rule reads WORSE, as the 0.4.0 arm against the 0.4.1 arm; the two 0.4.0 readings differ by 41 s between themselves, and no day-1 was measured at the lab-proven heads. `cluster-up` at install, T0 to rc 0: 20 min 25 s INCIDENT against 10 min 13 s (first lab, 0.4.0 tags) and 10 min 54 s (second lab, release-candidate heads), both INCIDENT too (the known load-balancer ACL error); the rule reads it WORSE, but all three hold INCIDENT time and the extra time here is the provider's capacity (a server created `stopped`, then three creates refused `out of stock`), so it is a record, not a comparison. No NOMINAL whole-install time (T0 to rc 0) at 0.4.0 was measured in that lab.
