**Not proven, or not fixed (honest list; the one place that holds it).** Not shown on a cluster: a Grafana to Zitadel sign-in end to end (the scripted finalisation was refused and no browser ran in the first lab; in the third, `grafana-oidc` stayed in `SecretSyncedError` all lab, which the header of `externalsecret-grafana-oidc.yaml` reads as expected until `secret/grafana/oidc` is seeded (a reading: the error message was not archived and the seed state was not read), and Grafana's sign-in redirect carried an empty `client_id`); the move onto the 0.4.1 tag itself (the third lab moved onto a branch of the release candidate, the tag did not exist yet, and infra pins it at release); the fresh-install window of #169; the forge's `/metrics` (the forge was not deployed); a sign-in of the pre-disaster Zitadel user after the rebuild, and anything of the restored Zitadel database beyond the original instance and that one user's events; a production-style OpenBao restore with the holders' recovery shares (the development bootstrap only); the OpenBao standby fix on more than one run (a race shown once); a repeat of the `openbao` step on the same cluster (in the first proof lab it left `openbao-0` sealed, cause unknown); a failover or zone-loss drill at these heads (so "no split brain" is not tested by this release's labs; a rebuild and a move have no failover); a NOMINAL whole-install time (T0 to rc 0) at 0.4.0 (the installs at the 0.4.0 tags in the first and third labs held INCIDENT time; the first lab's re-run, 7 min 15 s NOMINAL, is a second run, not a whole install); whether a destroy within `archive_timeout` of the last WAL switch really loses the last segment; Outscale and OVH (no run). One run per figure, one provider, one shape. **Not fixed:** the Zitadel break-glass after a rebuild (documented, no recovery designed); the known load-balancer ACL trap, which costs one failed run on every Scaleway build (infra); the provider's capacity errors at install, which `cluster-up` does not retry (OpenAether-infra 0.4.1, `docs/upgrade.md`, documents the `out of stock` answer for roll and replace-node and, since the third lab, at an install: run the same command again; in that one case the availability API said shortage in the zone and a later create succeeded, so it was not a usable predictor); `observability-vm-customresources` failing for about 2 minutes on each of the three fresh installs checked (`docs/traps.md`). **WORSE, accepted under the owner's delegation and never hidden:** the DR chain's NOMINAL steps are 25 % slower than in the first proof lab, the price of the fixes (a stable OpenBao before the databases, the identity group started after them); the figures and the other WORSE rows are in `docs/resilience.md`. The third lab's rows (one WORSE, two at the edge of the rule, two read at the 0.4.0 tags and so not 0.4.1 readings) are listed under Move proof. Not a compliance claim.

### Security (0.4.1; rung: the checker mocked, offline (the manifests and its fixture cases, the policy against the CRD schema CI uses); the policy itself observed on a cluster in two proof labs on Scaleway, 3 CP + 6 W, NOMINAL: no Prometheus body on any probe once the policy was applied)
- **The shared Gateway refuses `/metrics` and `/debug/metrics` for every route (open at 0.4.0, which guarded the forge's route only; earlier releases were not checked).** A proof lab on Scaleway (3 CP + 6 W) read `https://grafana.<domain>/metrics` through the public Gateway: HTTP 200 and no login, at 0.4.0 and at an earlier 0.4.1 head, from the lab host, which that lab's `app_lb_allowed_cidrs` allowed; a third lab read Zitadel's `/debug/metrics` open at 0.4.0 the same way (a Prometheus body of 53 `# HELP` lines). The app load balancer has no source ACL by default, so the exposure is to any caller that reaches the Gateway (low-medium: information exposure). New `apps/base/services-gateway/authorizationpolicy-deny-metrics.yaml`: an Istio AuthorizationPolicy (DENY) aimed at the Gateway for `/metrics`, `/metrics/*`, `/debug/metrics` and `/debug/metrics/*`, any caller, host and route; the Forgejo route's own backendless rule stays behind it. The scrapes are pod to pod and do not cross the Gateway. `scripts/check-gateway-routes.py` (CI, job "Flux DAG") fails when a path is not refused, or the policy is unlisted, narrowed, aimed elsewhere or too wide. **Seen on a cluster (a second proof lab, release-candidate heads):** no probe returned a Prometheus body, `/metrics` and `/debug/metrics` answered 403 on both listeners on every host probed (three product hosts and the lab's own test application), the ordinary paths were still served, and the rule survived a disaster-recovery rebuild. **Also in the third lab, on a 0.4.0 cluster moved to 0.4.1:** the same 216 probes gave 112 x 403 and no body, against 10 bodies (7 Grafana, 3 Zitadel) at 0.4.0; the policy took effect 2 min 38 s after the ref moved (resolution about 6 s; NOMINAL), and no body followed the first 403; before it, all 247 probes of each of the two paths (every 2 s, from the lab host) got an HTTP 200 with a body. **Limits:** spellings the policy does not match (`//metrics`, `/Metrics`, `/metrics;a=b`...) reach the service; none served metrics there, but a service that folds one of them into its metrics path would still leak; the forge's route was not deployed in the labs, so the forge is NOT shown; Istio warns, when it applies the policy, that it will deny all traffic to TCP ports (it names no port): harmless while the Gateway has only its HTTP and HTTPS listeners, but a TCP or TLS listener added later would be denied (`docs/public-exposure.md`). **Upgrade note:** a route of yours that serves `/metrics` on purpose gets the 403 too and no ALLOW overrides it; serve it under another path, or delete the policy with a Flux patch (opt-out and its limits, the Flux field UNVERIFIED: `docs/public-exposure.md`). The probe counts, the hosts, the scrapes (`up`), the spellings and the full list are in `docs/public-exposure.md`, the one place that holds them.

### Fixed (0.4.1; rung: mocked, offline, `scripts/dev/test-restore-test-cnpg.py` against the kubectl double, and read on a cluster in a proof lab on Scaleway, 3 CP + 6 W; the SKIP seen on a cluster as described in the first entry)
- **The CNPG restore tests skip, with a reason, on a Cluster none of whose Backups has completed yet (#169).** Before 0.4.0 the first base backup came with the first daily ScheduledBackup, up to a day after the Cluster was created, and `cnpg-recovery-restore-test` and `cnpg-pitr-restore-test` ended in `no target backup found` (a red `RestoreTestFailed` for up to a day, lab 25). Since 0.4.0 both ScheduledBackups are `immediate: true`: a base backup starts when the Cluster is created, so the window is the time that first Backup takes, or longer if it fails. A fresh install at the 0.4.1 heads (a proof lab on Scaleway, 3 CP + 6 W, NOMINAL) did not meet it: the immediate base backups of the two platform databases completed about 10 s after they started, two minutes before the CronJobs existed. The SKIP itself was seen on a cluster only on a rebuilt one whose platform Backups the lab had deleted by hand: the test Job ended in 10 s with 4 `SKIP ... no completed base backup yet` lines and no `oa_restore_test_ok` series, and passed 4 of 4 (275 s) after two new plugin Backups. The window of a real fresh install was NOT reached. `restore_cnpg.py` now lists the `Backup` objects of the namespace and keeps those whose `spec.cluster.name` is the Cluster, made by a ScheduledBackup or by hand; no label is read (a Backup made by hand, such as the DR chain's `dr-base-<cluster>`, has not been shown to carry `cnpg.io/cluster`). None `completed` on a Cluster under 30 hours old prints `SKIP <cluster>/<store>/<mode>: no completed base backup yet` and reports no `ok` series. As soon as one Backup of the Cluster is completed, all four combinations restore as before: there is no extra wait for the replica store, so the DR chain (forced copy, then `dr-check`, which refuses any SKIP) still gets its PASS lines; a copy that `cnpg-store-replicate` (every 15 minutes) has not made yet still fails the replica store with `no target backup found`. Kept as FAILURES, each with a test that goes red when the guard is removed: no completed `Backup` on a Cluster older than 30 hours (our figure: a day and a margin, which covers a failed immediate Backup), a failed listing of the Backups, and a restore that fails when a base backup exists. A `completed` Backup without `stoppedAt` counts as a base backup (whether the plugin sets it is not established; the point-in-time target then falls back to the old behaviour). Side effect: the point-in-time floor now includes a Backup made by hand, which a label selector would have missed; lab 25's PITR failure after a manual Backup may come from that, or from a missing `stoppedAt` (not established, to read on a real manual Backup). No RBAC change (the Role already lists `backups`).

- **The dump-load restore test skips, with a reason, on a store whose restic repository does not exist yet (the dump flow that #169 did not cover; found in a proof lab on Scaleway, 3 CP + 6 W, NOMINAL install).** `cnpg-dump` (02:47 UTC) creates the restic repositories and `cnpg-dump-load-restore-test` (05:40 UTC) reads them, so an install made in between failed all four store and database combinations (`Fatal: repository does not exist`) and held a critical `RestoreTestFailed` for hours (it passed in 14 s after a manual `cnpg-dump`). `restic-dump.sh` now reads restic's exit code 10 (`repository does not exist` and no other state, checked on the pinned restic 0.19.1 against the fake store) and leaves an `absent` marker; `dump_load.py` prints `SKIP <cluster>/<store>/dump: ...` and the store reports no `ok` series, so the Job succeeds and the `ok=0` series disappear at the next run. Every other failure stays a failure, each with a test that goes red when its guard is removed, **including a store that passed before and has no repository now** (a dump that existed is gone; if the publishing stage cannot read what it published before, every skipped store fails). What is skipped, what is not, and the limit of a skip that lasts: `apps/base/backup/README.md`. Rung: mocked, `scripts/dev/test-restore-test-dumps.sh` (stage 1, a real restic and fake stores) and `scripts/dev/test-restore-test-cnpg.py` (stages 2 and 3, kubectl double); **Seen on a cluster** in a second proof lab (Scaleway, 3 CP + 6 W, NOMINAL), before the first `cnpg-dump`: the Job ended in 9 s with 4 `SKIP` lines (primary and replica store, both databases) and published no `oa_restore_test_ok` series; after a manual `cnpg-dump` and `cnpg-store-replicate` it passed 4 of 4 in 18 s. `RestoreTestFailed` was read inactive only 5.5 minutes after the skip, inside its 10-minute `for`: the absent series is the evidence, not the alert. **Seen again, on a 0.4.0 cluster moved to 0.4.1 (a third proof lab, the lab-proven heads, Scaleway, 3 CP + 6 W; the durations here are NOMINAL):** at 0.4.0 the test failed 4 of 4 in 23 s before the first dump (`Fatal: repository does not exist`) and `RestoreTestFailed` went pending, then was read firing at the first sample 17 s after the move command returned (pending at the sample before, the poll being every 15 s: it fired between 1 and 17 s after), about 10 minutes after it went pending (its `for: 10m`), so that firing is not a pure 0.4.0 reading; after the move it skipped 4 of 4 (18 s) and passed 4 of 4 (19 s) after a manual `cnpg-dump` (18 s, on the second attempt: the first failed on a test table of the lab). The alert was gone 5 min 20 s to 5 min 35 s after the skip: VictoriaMetrics dropped the failing series within 80 s and the rest is vmalert's 5-minute evaluation interval, so a cluster already holding the 0.4.0 alert keeps it for about that long after the first run of the new test.

### Changed (0.4.1; rung: mocked, `scripts/dev/test-backup-verify.sh` on a real Barman archive in fake S3 stores; the failure it reports was seen once on a cluster, in a proof lab on Scaleway, 3 CP + 6 W)
- **`backup-verify` says what an unarchived end WAL means and what to do (`apps/base/backup/databases/verify/barman_check.py`).** The plugin `Backup` says `completed` before the WAL segment that ends it is archived (up to `archive_timeout` after the last switch). In the proof lab the verify failed on the primary store about 1.5 minutes (86 s) after both final Backups were `completed`, with `end_wal ...0C is not archived (newest 11)`: a decimal segment number, and no word on what to do. The message now names both segments in hex, says that the Backup is not a usable restore point until then and that the commits after the last archived segment are not in the archive, and gives the three steps (force a WAL switch on the primary, wait until it is archived, let the replica copy follow and verify again) with a pointer to `apps/base/backup/README.md`. The check itself is unchanged. Test, red first: a new case of `test-backup-verify.sh` removes the end segment and every later one from the primary archive; with the old message 1 of its 3 new checks failed (the message), with the new one all pass. The runbook for `BackupVerificationFailed` says this message is not a corrupt backup. It is the only change to `backup-verify`'s output in this release; nothing else in its archive logic moved (the restore tests and the dump flow gained their own SKIP lines, see Fixed).

### Added (0.4.1; rung: mocked, the rule and its 2 new cases run offline with the pinned `vmalert-tool` v1.154.0 on synthetic series shaped on lab 28C; fired on a cluster once at the 0.4.1 heads, a proof lab on Scaleway, 3 CP + 6 W, NOMINAL: delay in the entry)
- **`CnpgWalNeverArchived` (critical, `for: 5m`), a PROXY for a Cluster that archives nothing and fails now (apps half of infra #430, which stays open).** A Cluster created over the WAL of another database system archives nothing (lab 28C: archived 0, failed 39, `ContinuousArchiving=False`) while it looks healthy, and its first failover wedged in 4 of 4 reproductions; `CnpgWalArchiveFailing` needs a failure with no archive in the same 10 minutes plus `for: 15m`. The new rule is `increase(cnpg_pg_stat_archiver_failed_count[5m]) > 0` while the sum of `cnpg_pg_stat_archiver_archived_count` over the Cluster's pods is 0, per namespace and Cluster. It reads two metrics already scraped and catalogued (no new scrape, no RBAC, `alert-metrics.yaml` unchanged). Limits, none seen on a cluster: it reads the archiver, **not the `ContinuousArchiving` condition** (the design of the issue, a kube-state-metrics `customResourceState` entry plus `list`/`watch` on `clusters.postgresql.cnpg.io`, is a new RBAC grant and is NOT delivered here); the counters restart at 0 after a crash recovery and on a promoted or re-cloned instance, so a Cluster that archived before can fire it when its archive then fails; a new Cluster whose S3 keys are not seeded yet fires it until they are; the unit test evaluates every 10 minutes, so it cannot tell `for: 5m` from `for: 10m`. **It is not instant: about 10 to 15 minutes after the Cluster is created.** Observed once on a cluster (a proof lab on Scaleway, 3 CP + 6 W, NOMINAL, a Cluster over the WAL of another database system); the range is an estimate from that one observation. The figures, the cause (the group's 5-minute evaluation interval plus `for: 5m`) and how to read them are in `docs/runbooks.md#cnpgwalneverarchived`, the one place that holds them. An earlier one-off `vmalert-tool` run with a 1-minute evaluation said 6 and 16 minutes: wrong.

### Documented (0.4.1, the proof labs on Scaleway (3 CP + 6 W) and the audit that followed; rung: observed on a cluster in those labs, docs and comments only)
- **A completed Backup is not a restore point until its end WAL is archived (`apps/base/backup/README.md`, the one authoritative place; pointers in `docs/runbooks.md`).** The plugin reports `completed` before the segment that ends the Backup is archived. Seen once: `backup-verify` failed about 1.5 minutes (86 s) after both final Backups were `completed`; a forced `SELECT pg_switch_wal();` archived the segment 2 to 4 s later and the verify then passed on both stores. A destroy at that moment would have ended the archive one segment earlier. NOT measured: how often, and whether the next `archive_timeout` would have archived it before the destroy (the proof lab forced the switch and then destroyed). The page gives the order for a planned destroy, a migration or any final backup, with the branch for a forced switch that is not archived (archiving is failing), and notes that the verify Job ends `Complete` when a check fails, so a gate on the Job status passes. The replica store has had the same rule since 0.4.0 (`cnpg-store-replicate`).
- **`iam-admin` and `iam-admin-pat` do not exist after a disaster-recovery rebuild, so the documented Zitadel break-glass is gone (`docs/zitadel-hardening.md`, new section; pointers in `apps/base/backup/README.md`, `docs/key-inventory.md`, `scripts/zitadel-apply-policies.py` and the HelmRelease comment).** Observed once after a rebuild by the DR chain, at the 0.4.1 head (0.4.0 was not probed): no Secret with `iam-admin` in its name, `iam-admin-pat` `not found`. The cause is a reading (`FirstInstance` is read once, at instance creation; the restored database already holds the instance; nothing in this repository stores the two Secrets in OpenBao); whether the machine user is still in the restored database was not checked. **What was read of the identity data:** in SQL, the restored `zitadel` database held the original instance (events from the first install, so not a new instance) and the user created before the disaster. Nothing else was examined: no other user, not the machine user, no password or second factor. A sign-in of that user after the rebuild was NOT shown (the scripted path needs the PAT; no browser run). A human IAM_OWNER who can sign in can create a new machine user in the console (not run on a rebuilt cluster); with no human admin able to sign in there is no documented recovery in 0.4.1. The design of one is for a later release.
- **`docs/resilience.md`, "What the 0.4.1 proof labs showed":** the one place that holds the audited figures of the DR rebuild with writers, the fresh install and the WORSE rows of the DR chain, each with its rung; the figures of the move are under "Move proof" above and the page only points to them. Its wall times carry INCIDENT time and are not a like-for-like BETTER, and a rebuild has no failover, so it says nothing about split brain.
- **`docs/traps.md`, Observability: on a fresh install `observability-vm-customresources` fails for about 2 minutes, then converges (expected).** Timings read on the three fresh installs whose Flux events were kept (2 min 2 s to 2 min 3 s each, NOMINAL, two at the 0.4.0 tags and one at a release-candidate head); the cause (a CRD not registered yet) is a reading and the failure message was not archived. No alert fires. Left for 0.5.0 (performance only).
- **`docs/public-exposure.md` and `docs/public-exposure.yaml` carry what the second proof lab read** (the 403, the scrapes, the spellings) in place of "expected, UNVERIFIED"; the forge stays unshown, and so does the opt-out of the policy. The third proof lab adds to `docs/public-exposure.md`: Zitadel's `/debug/metrics` was open at 0.4.0 too, the policy reaches an upgraded cluster with the Flux sync and not at the end of the move, the `up` readings are now kept (4 series, all 1), and the Istio warning about TCP ports with what it means for a listener added later. **`apps/base/backup/README.md`, "Order of a full recovery"** now says the chain was run on clusters in proof labs (not unattended) and points step 5 (check logins) to the `iam-admin` page above.

### Documentation (0.4.1; rung: text only, nothing run; the lab 28B observation it records was made on a cluster)
- **`CnpgWalNeverArchived` fires about 10 to 15 minutes after the Cluster is created, not "in minutes".** One observation, in a proof lab on Scaleway (3 CP + 6 W, NOMINAL). `docs/runbooks.md#cnpgwalneverarchived` holds the figures and the fast signal; `docs/detection.md` and `docs/traps.md` point to it, and the earlier "6 and 16 minutes" of `docs/traps.md` are gone.
- **`docs/traps.md`, the Cluster on a polluted archive path, says what 0.4.1 delivers: detection, not prevention.** `task cluster-verify` (OpenAether-infra) now reads the profile Clusters and the proxy alert exists; an alert on the condition itself and a `serverName` per incarnation are deferred (infra #430 stays open). The line "a cluster-side mitigation is planned for 0.4.1" is gone.
- **After an OpenBao raft restore, check all three pods, not two (#184).** In lab 28B (Scaleway, 3 CP + 6 W, DR chain) `openbao-0`, the pod the restore ran on, stayed a standby with no active node address (`local node not active but active cluster node not found`, logins 500 for 25+ minutes: INCIDENT, the time until a person restarted it) while the documented step restarts only `openbao-1` and `openbao-2`. `apps/base/foundation/vault/README.md` (Disaster recovery, step 3) and `docs/runbooks.md` now say: `bao status` on each of the three pods, restart the one with no `Active Node Address` (#184: lab 28B noted "no active address"; the exact field name was not re-read on a cluster); `docs/detection.md` records that no alert sees it while another pod is active. The restore script (OpenAether-infra) is a separate change: this text does not describe it. The restore is documented as run with the development bootstrap only (7.4 s in lab 28B, 3 CP + 7 W, and 8 s in lab 27, 3 CP + 5 W, both NOMINAL); a production-style restore with the holders' recovery shares was not run.

### Documented (0.4.1, apps #176, #177, #178, #182; rung: docs only, nothing changed in the manifests, nothing run for this entry; the figures come from the 0.4.0 lab journals (labs 27 to 28E, Scaleway and OVH), the mechanisms marked "reading" from the CloudNativePG 1.30.1 source)
- **The Pooler of a freshly created `ha` database may not log in (`docs/traps.md`, `docs/database-profiles.md`, apps #177).** `role "cnpg_pooler_pgbouncer" does not exist` for 9 minutes at one first install (lab 27), cured 20 s after the Cluster annotation; not met in the 6 other first installs the journals speak of. Cause not found, no change to the component; every lab applied the examples by hand, so the Flux path is UNVERIFIED.
- **A deleted `FailoverQuorum` object is not recreated for minutes (`docs/traps.md`, apps #178).** Seen once (lab 27, Scaleway): still missing after more than 2 minutes, back after the `cnpg.io/reloadedAt` annotation. By reading, the operator does not watch that kind and refuses a failover while the object is missing or empty, so a primary lost in that window would not be replaced: UNVERIFIED on a cluster, the lab never lost a primary then.
- **R4 gray failure on OVH: write gaps of 104-299 s, a documented limit (`docs/resilience.md`, apps #182).** Three drills, one sample each, INCIDENT by design on NOMINAL clusters: Scaleway 3 control planes + 5 workers 88.0 / 133.8 / 104.3 s (`zitadel-db` / `ha` / pooler), OVH 3 + 4 256.8 / 213.0 / 184.5 s, OVH 3 + 6 103.7 / 299.3 / 278.0 s, with the failover start, the late "promotion request noticed" (26 s to 3 min 37 s) and the HTTP shares. Zero acknowledged commit lost. No target, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-27; the mechanism is the lab's reading of the timing; whether a fault longer than 300 s would last longer was not measured (UNVERIFIED).
- **The 13 s of the load-balancer limit is no longer called the providers' minimum (`docs/resilience.md`, infra #424).** The 0.4.0 text said the load balancer takes up to 13 s to drop a dead member, "already the minimum the providers allow, so OpenAether cannot shorten it". It is the setting OpenAether chose (a check every 5 s, 2 failed checks, a 3 s timeout, computed and not measured); the floor holds for the Outscale interval and threshold only, a shorter Scaleway check was never tried, and the 13 s there rests on an interval model that may not fit Scaleway. Same wording as OpenAether-infra `docs/resilience.md`. The 15 s limit itself is unchanged, and the 0.4.0 entry below that gives the old reason is history, left as it was. The R4 section title and its first paragraph now point to the OVH subsection.
- **Forgejo brick: what the 0.4.0 labs settled and what stays open (`docs/forgejo.md`, apps #176).** The stale "UNVERIFIED" and "still know two databases" lines are corrected with the lab 27B to 28 figures (convergence, the volume backup selected at creation and restored, `backup-verify` covering `forgejo-db`, six hard reboots with the forge in them (five of its node, one of its whole zone), three partitions of its node and twelve graceful zone power-offs on Scaleway, every acknowledged push present, the forge away 85 to 178 s); the 15-minute window between the database and the volume backups is documented with a way to narrow it (UNVERIFIED, no `forgejo-db` restore run); the capacity readings (313 %, 262 %, 302 % of a worker) are listed and the forge's +400 m stays DERIVED. The sections on `core.fsync`, `dirsync` and the lock cleaners keep the lab 27 figures, and the sentences in them that said UNVERIFIED, "the next drill" or "the only loss seen" now name lab 27 and say what labs 27B to 28C showed; the two sections on the durability of a push also open with a status line (the cost of `core.fsync` measured once, +30 to +60 ms per push; `dirsync` seen on the mount; the cleaner proven on a hand-made lock, not on the lock of a real interrupted push; the sweeper's 666 s a NOMINAL test); the durations are labelled NOMINAL or INCIDENT with the cluster shape, and the labs list and the convergence range (1 min 17 s to 2 min 33 s) include labs 28B and 28C; the one burst left UNVERIFIED at a fresh install is the load on the S3 endpoint, not whether the three first base backups complete; the file header, the opt-in paragraph and the base-backup paragraph no longer say that the brick never ran or that the first backup is unverified.

Correction (added in 0.4.3): the sentence above that the spellings the policy does not match "reach the service; none served metrics there" was true of the hosts swept, and its Limits clause already said that the forge was not shown (it was not deployed in those labs); the forge turned out to be exposed: **released 0.4.2 with the forge brick ON answered `https://forge.<domain>//metrics` and four other spellings with the forge's metrics** (66 families, no credential; measured), and 0.4.1 has the same `apps/` tree (`git diff 0.4.1..0.4.2` touches nothing under `apps/` or `scripts/`), so it is INFERRED exposed, not shown: it was not run, and no 0.4.1 cluster with the forge brick was swept. In the sweeps only the forge host answered with metrics; a cluster without the forge brick showed no exposure in them (Grafana, Zitadel, OpenBao and the test application: 0 bodies on the first response; the Longhorn opt-in route was not tested, redirects were not followed). Fixed in 0.4.3: see "Security (0.4.3 ...)" and its CORRECTION NOTE under `[0.4.3]`.

## [0.4.0] — 2026-11-01

0.4.0 is the **resilience release**: a node, a zone or a pod can be lost without losing an acknowledged commit and without a split brain. What it adds, from the entries below:

- **Databases:** Barman Cloud plugin backups with WAL archiving and a base backup at creation (`immediate: true`), restore tests, verified replica store, CNPG profiles `light` / `standard` / `ha` for customer applications, a bounded planned primary restart (26.3-33.6 s measured), a PgBouncer that keeps the IP of `-rw` (`dns_max_ttl: 3600`) so the pooler path follows the database through a zone loss, and backup alerts that read the plugin's series.
- **Critical path:** 3 Gateway replicas hard-spread over nodes and zones with drain-safe termination, 25 workloads pinned in `critical_path` (`scripts/ha-allowlist.yaml`), the Barman Cloud plugin hard-spread over nodes, Longhorn `node-down-pod-deletion-policy` set to delete both pod kinds.
- **Platform:** Istio 1.31.3 and Gateway API CRDs 1.6.0 for a FRESH install, the Zitadel login reachable through the Gateway, a second audit-log sink outside the cluster (opt-in), an optional Forgejo git forge (dogfooding, V1), monitoring and alerts catalogued in `docs/monitoring-0.4.md`, run-books and traps for the failures met in labs 19 to 28E.
- **Evidence:** labs 25 to 28E (Scaleway and OVH): zero acknowledged commit lost and no split brain in every zone and node drill, on HA, standard and platform databases and the forge. Every line states its rung (observed on a cluster, mocked, UNVERIFIED).

**Upgrade path.** Supported before 1.0.0: the previous minor only, **0.3.x to 0.4.0**, paired with **OpenAether-infra 0.4.0** (apps ref moved by `task cluster-apps-ref`, not by a machine-config change). The 0.4.0 database spec must not reach a 0.3.x Cluster in one change: run `task cnpg-presync` of the infrastructure repository first (see "moving existing CNPG databases" and `docs/resilience.md`). `immediate: true` does not trigger a backup on an existing ScheduledBackup; the Istio 1.31 move is for a fresh install; restart the pooler Deployment after re-creating a Cluster under the same name; a `light` cluster loses its data at a node replace (`docs/database-profiles.md`).

**Not proven on a cluster at the final heads (release gate 0.4.0, 2026-11-01).** Every Outscale row (E1, E2, E3, E4b, E6, R2, R3, G11b) is OPEN, waiting for the quota; the `union()` form of `CnpgBackupFailed` / `CnpgBackupStale` is covered by engine unit tests only (the plugin-only candidate was measured on a live store); the residual pooler exposure (a pod started or a DNS cache expired inside the first ~100 s of an outage) is sampled on 6 drills only; I5 (OVH hard reboot at 90 s), the scheduled backup-verify run, a production-style OpenBao share restore and G1 in one invocation (0.4.1) are not shown either. 14 gate rows are WORSE and accepted by the owner or by his delegation, never hidden. Not a compliance claim.

### Security

- **Two CRITICAL Next.js advisories on `zitadel-login` v4.19.5 (Next.js 16.3.6) are recorded as not affected, with an expiry of 2026-11-05** (GHSA-673q-mjm6-r5xr, `use cache` typed arrays; GHSA-2464-fhr4-w9xw, image optimization). Read from the v4.19.5 image: cache components and `use cache` are off; the vulnerable `sharp` binary cannot load on the musl base image (fragile: reachable if the image moves to glibc). The Login V2 UI is not routed through the Gateway in 0.3.x (apps #161); fixing #161 must also block `/ui/v2/login/_next/image`. Zitadel v4.20.0 still pins Next.js 16.3.6 (read from its source tag, not an image). Re-check at the first tag with Next.js >= 16.4.1. Not a compliance claim.


### Fixed (0.4.0, lab 28E, 2026-11-01; rung: the defect and the candidate comparisons MEASURED on the live store of lab 28E (Scaleway, 3 control planes + 6 workers, apps 29d78ba); the shipped expressions and their 5 new cases mocked, offline, with the pinned `vmalert-tool`; the shipped head is NOT re-read on a cluster, the cluster was torn down)
- **`CnpgBackupFailed` and `CnpgBackupStale` read the Barman Cloud plugin's series as well as the CNPG collector's (`apps/base/observability/vm-customresources/vmrule-backup.yaml`).** Every backup of the platform goes through the plugin, and for those the CNPG collector reports `cnpg_collector_last_available_backup_timestamp` = 0 (and a real `last_failed`). Effect measured in lab 28E: after ONE failed backup (`zitadel-db`, `NoSuchBucket` on `UploadPart` at 01:59Z; the next, 03:10Z, completed) `CnpgBackupFailed` stayed true for good (1 series, 1793498646 > 0) and would have fired after its `for: 10m` for ever; `CnpgBackupStale` could never arm (the metric is 0): a stale-backup alert that never fires. The plugin exports the real values as `barman_cloud_cloudnative_pg_io_last_available_backup_timestamp`, `..._last_failed_backup_timestamp` (and `..._first_recoverability_point`, not read) on the same `cnpg` scrape with the same `cnpg_io_cluster` label (9 series each, 3 Clusters x 3 pods; `zitadel-db` last_available 1793502616, last_failed 1793498646; `grafana-db` last_available 1793503516, last_failed 0; ages 33 to 64 minutes). On the live store the plugin comparison returned 0 series (correct) where the shipped expression returned 1, and the stale guard `> 0` returned 3 series (`grafana-db`, `lab27-ha`, `zitadel-db`), so the rule can arm. Now each side takes the largest of the two names per Cluster, `max by (cnpg_io_cluster) (union(cnpg_collector_..., barman_cloud_cloudnative_pg_io_...))`, so a Cluster whose backups only the collector reports keeps working and an old collector value (an upgraded cluster) does not hide a fresh plugin one; `for:` unchanged (10 min, 1 h). `scripts/dev/alert-metrics.yaml` catalogues the two plugin names (existing); both checkers (`test-alert-metrics.py`, OpenAether-infra `check-alert-metrics.py`) read the four names from the expressions. Test: `scripts/dev/vmrules-unittest.yaml`, 5 new cases (a failure then a later success with the collector at 0 must not fire; a failure with no later success fires; the collector alone still works; a 60 h old plugin backup fires and 40 h, 10 h or none do not; an old collector value does not hide a fresh plugin one). On the shipped expressions 4 of the 5 fail (`test-vmrules.sh`: unit test FAILED, 75 cases before the change), after the change `vmalert-tool unittest: 80 cases pass`; a plugin-only mutant fails 2 of them. `docs/runbooks.md`, `docs/detection.md`, `docs/monitoring-0.4.md`, `apps/base/backup/README.md` say what the alerts read and why.

### Changed (0.4.0, lab 28D, I26 / apps #158, decision of 2026-11-01 taken by the lead under the owner's delegation; rung: the effect observed on a cluster in lab 28D with the value set by hand on a live Pooler, the manifest and its test mocked, offline; the shipped head is NOT yet re-proved, a re-proof of at least 3 zone drills is planned in the last proof lab)
- **The `ha` profile's PgBouncer caches the IP of `<name>-rw` for an hour: `dns_max_ttl: "3600"` (default 15 s) in `apps/components/cnpg-profile-pooler/pooler.yaml`.** Lab 28D (Scaleway, 3 control planes + 6 workers, 19 zone power-offs): the pooler path stayed dark until the zone returned in 4 of 14 baseline drills (214.8-308.0 s) and at least one pooler pod was stuck in 7 of 15 drills (11 of 30 pods), while the direct path was back in 61-93 s; with the value, 8 of 8 drills and 16 of 16 pods had no failed lookup and the pooler gap equalled the database gap (61.3-69.6 s). One-sided Fisher p = 0.004 per pod and 0.026 per drill with a stuck pod; whole path dark 0 of 8 against 4 of 14, p = 0.14, too few. **Encouraging, explained by the mechanism, not a proof of zero.** Residual risk: a lookup inside the first ~100 s of an outage still pins with probability 1 in 3. Side effect: the pooler keeps the IP for up to one hour, so restart the pooler Deployment after re-creating a Cluster under the same name. 3600 and not 300: the Service IP is stable and 300 s keeps about ten times more lookups in the window. The CNPG webhook allows the parameter (`internal/webhook/v1/pooler_webhook.go`, `AllowedPgbouncerGenericConfigurationParameters`; listed in "PgBouncer configuration options" of the CloudNativePG `connection_pooling.md`, v1.30.1). Test: `test-cnpg-profiles.py` fails (1 failed) without the line and passes with it (`dns_max_ttl` present and at least 300).

### Documented (0.4.0, lab 28D, I26 / apps #158 and labs 27B, 27C, 28B; rung: observed on a cluster in the lab, docs and comments only)
- **"Zone loss through the pooler" is rewritten with the proven mechanism (`docs/database-profiles.md`, apps #158).** No UDP socket at rest; at a fault PgBouncer's DNS cache has expired, c-ares opens one UDP socket to the cluster DNS Service and keeps it; the service load balancer at the pod interface picks the CoreDNS replica once and pins it in conntrack (`bpf-lb-sock-hostns-only: true`, required by Istio ambient and the Gateway API, so no socket-level load balancing in pods); if that replica is the one in the lost zone (1 in 3) it stays `terminating-not-serving` and Cilium keeps routing the existing flow to it until the zone returns. Proof: 109 probe sockets read before the fault, 38 of 38 pinned to the dead replica blocked, 0 of 71 pinned to a live one. The page also gives the baseline, the evidence for `dns_max_ttl` with its limits, the residual risk, the run-book line (delete the pooler pod that logs `DNS lookup failed`; 8 of 8 restarts effective), the one-hour staleness side effect, and why Cilium's socket-termination option does not apply. Observed on ONE cluster family (Scaleway, 3 control planes + 6 workers). The profile table, the gate table and the "Not done" list say the same.
- **The planned primary restart is MEASURED, not expected: 26.3-33.6 s (`docs/resilience.md`, `apps/components/cnpg-bounded-shutdown`).** The estimate of 12-25 s "UNVERIFIED until the next lab" is replaced by three NOMINAL runs on Scaleway, 3 control planes + 5 workers, `zitadel-db` primary with the Zitadel pool connected and `smartShutdownTimeout: 5`: 33.6 s (lab 27B, 6 s of it a cold sidecar image pull), 26.3 s (lab 27C), 31.4 s (lab 28B). Over the 30 s aim in two of three, inside the 35 s target the owner set on 2026-10-31 in all three.

### Documented (0.4.0, owner decision of 2026-10-31 (option A) on the load-balancer limit, and lab 28B step S9; rung: observed on a cluster in the lab, docs only, nothing changed in the manifests)
- **The failure limit of a window that depends on a provider load balancer's health check moves from 10 s to 15 s (G5, R3, E6).** `docs/resilience.md` points to the authoritative text in OpenAether-infra `docs/resilience.md`: the HTTP probe through the app load balancer on a node or zone loss and the `/readyz` probe through the API load balancer on a control-plane loss are now at most 15 s; every other window keeps 10 s, and the 10 % failed-share limit, the database write-gap targets and the zero-acknowledged-loss rule are unchanged. Reason: the load balancer takes up to 13 s to drop a dead member (a check every 5 s x 2 failed checks + 3 s timeout), already the minimum the providers allow. The earlier entry about the HTTP criterion below keeps its 10 s: it was the criterion of its day.
- **Archive generation `-g2` is no longer marked UNVERIFIED on a cluster.** `apps/base/backup/README.md`: lab 28B step S9 (Scaleway) found generation 1 and rebuilt `zitadel-db` and `grafana-db` as `-g2`, and the PITR recovery brought the seed rows back with the same md5; a third generation, a by-hand recovery from the old path and the 30-day policy on the old path stay UNVERIFIED.

### Documented (0.4.0, lab 28B/28C findings I25 and I17, owner decisions of 2026-10-30 and 2026-10-31; rung: observed on a cluster in the lab, docs only, nothing changed in the manifests)
- **A Cluster created on an archive path that holds another database's WAL never finishes its first failover (I25).** `docs/traps.md` gives the symptom (`ContinuousArchiving=False` from the first minute, `Failing over` for ever, `WAL file is from different database system` in the target's log), the cause (the standbys' `restore_command` replaces their valid WAL with the foreign segment; the operator 1.30.1 waits with no timeout), the command that shows it before a failure, the prevention (a new `serverName` or an empty path per incarnation) and the release (`status.targetPrimary` back on the current primary). Polluted path: stuck 4 of 4 (labs 28B, 28C); healthy path: 0 of 19 comparable zone power-offs, DB gap 59-86 s in the 14 of lab 28C; no acknowledged commit lost. A `cluster-verify` line, an alert on the condition and a unique `serverName` per incarnation are planned for 0.4.1 (no date).
- **A node replace destroys the data of a `light` cluster (I17).** `docs/database-profiles.md` says it plainly: node-local storage, no backup, 42,583 acknowledged commits lost that way in lab 28B; outside the zero-loss rule by the owner's decision of 2026-10-30; use `standard` or `ha` first, or accept the loss. A roll/replace survey that names such Clusters and asks for an explicit yes is planned for 0.4.1 (no date).

### Fixed (0.4.0, lab 27 part C, Outscale, 2026-10-28; rung: mocked, offline; UNVERIFIED on a cluster)
- **The Barman Cloud plugin spreads over nodes with a HARD rule (E4).** `apps/base/cnpg/patch-barman-ha.yaml`: the hostname constraint is `DoNotSchedule`, `maxSkew` 1, `nodeTaintsPolicy: Honor` (the Gateway's recipe); the zone term stays a preference. Lab 27 part C met both plugin pods on one node twice, and a partition of it blocked the failover of a primary for 204 s (target 75 s) because the CNPG operator cannot reconcile a plugin-enabled Cluster without a reachable plugin. A pod that is already running keeps its place: the infrastructure repository's `task cluster-rebalance` (run by `cluster-up`, `cluster-roll`, `cluster-replace-node`; `cluster-verify` fails on a stacked pair) puts a stacked pair back. With no room for a second pod it stays Pending rather than stacking. Cost: 0 requests. Test: `scripts/dev/test-cnpg-barman-plugin.py` (hard hostname term, soft zone term). The Gateway spread was already hard; its pods did not come back because nothing re-reads a spread rule after scheduling (same rebalance step). To prove on a cluster: a fresh install and a node loss with both plugin pods Ready and none Pending, and the 204 s drill repeated.

### Changed (0.4.0, critical-path HA audit and owner decisions of 2026-10-28; rung: mocked, offline, UNVERIFIED on a cluster)
- **The Gateway runs 3 replicas, hard-spread over nodes and zones, and terminates without dropping connections; the public Service stays `externalTrafficPolicy: Cluster`** (owner). HPA floor 2 -> 3 (ceiling 4 -> 6); the Kyverno mutation `spread-gateway-pods` now sets `DoNotSchedule` spreads on the hostname and the zone with `nodeTaintsPolicy: Honor` (they were `ScheduleAnyway`), a `preStop` sleep of 5 s, `TERMINATION_DRAIN_DURATION_SECONDS=45` (above the load balancer's 5 s x 3 x 2 = 30 s) and `terminationGracePeriodSeconds=60`; `GatewayReplicasLow` fires below 3. Cost about +100 m CPU / 128 Mi of requests (derived). `test-gateway-ha.py` is rewritten to pin all of it; the `kyverno-gateway-spread` fixture compares the mutated pod.
- **`critical_path` in `scripts/ha-allowlist.yaml`** pins 25 workloads of the request and operation path (replicas and a zone term, errors in `scripts/check-ha.py`; the zone term stays a warning for the others). `docs/resilience.md` and `docs/runbooks.md` (GatewayReplicasLow) updated.

### Fixed (0.4.0, lab 27 part B, OVH, 2026-10-27; rung: mocked, offline, UNVERIFIED on a cluster)
- **Forge: a stale git lock no longer blocks a branch for minutes (F-LOCKAGE).** After a HARD power loss during a push (OVH, run b3c) `refs/heads/<branch>.lock` stayed, the new pod started 2 minutes later and the cleaner, which took only locks older than 5 minutes and ran only at pod start, left it: every push to that branch was refused for 9 minutes until a deliberate pod restart. The init container `clean-stale-git-locks` now removes locks of any age (at pod start no git process runs on the volume: Recreate, ReadWriteOnce), and a sidecar `sweep-stale-git-locks` looks again every 60 s for locks older than 10 minutes (a restart of the main container does not run an init container). Tests: `test-forgejo-brick.py` runs the one script of both, in both modes, with 20 new mutants. Not measured on a cluster; the next lab must kill the node and the main container mid-push (`docs/forgejo.md`).
- **The backup verifier covers `forgejo-db`, and a failed backup Job keeps its log (D13).** `barman_check.py` read `zitadel-db` and `grafana-db` only: the forge's database was never verified. It is in the default list, as in the store copy job; a profile without the forge has no archive for it, which reads `skip`, never a failure (test: `test-cnpg-archive-generation.py`, with and without the forge and with a hole in its WAL). The three backup CronJobs that ran `restartPolicy: OnFailure` (`cnpg-dump`, `openbao-snapshot`, `etcd-snapshot`) are `Never`: with OnFailure the Job controller deletes the pod when the backoff limit is reached, and lab 27 part B had a nightly dump fail on a table its role could not read with no pod and no log left. Test: `test-backup-job-logs.py` (every CronJob of the brick, and the checker's own defects). Not run on a cluster.

### Changed (0.4.0, owner decisions of 2026-10-27 after lab 27 sitting 3; rung: mocked, offline, UNVERIFIED on a cluster)
- **Longhorn `node-down-pod-deletion-policy` is `delete-both-statefulset-and-deployment-pod`.** Lab 27 sitting 3 (Scaleway, one lab): the forge was away 372 s on a zone power-off and 341 s on a partition with Longhorn's default `do-nothing` (the replacement pod waits for the volume to detach from the lost node), 128 s on the same partition with the value applied by hand. Two carriers, one value: the HelmRelease `defaultSettings.nodeDownPodDeletionPolicy` (fresh installs; the documentation does not say that an upgrade rewrites an existing Setting) and a Flux-managed Setting object in a new Kustomization `storage-settings` (group 21d, companion of `storage`, so an upgraded 0.3.x cluster gets it too). Test: `test-longhorn-node-down-policy.py`, mutation-checked, in CI. Side effects (force deleting StatefulSet and Deployment pods of a down node), the RWX share-manager gap and a partition of a live node are NOT measured: `docs/resilience.md`. Check: `kubectl -n longhorn-system get settings.longhorn.io node-down-pod-deletion-policy -o jsonpath='{.value}'`.
- **Forge: `dirsync` mount and a stale git lock cleaner.** `longhorn-x2-rwo-forgejo` carries `mountOptions: [dirsync]` (an experiment on the one acknowledged push lost of 59 at the zone power-off: UNVERIFIED, performance cost not measured, new volumes only), and an init container `clean-stale-git-locks` removes `refs/**/*.lock`, `packed-refs.lock`, `HEAD.lock`, `config.lock` and `shallow.lock` older than 5 minutes from the bare repositories before the app starts (an interrupted push left a lock that refused every later push to the branch for good). It does not recover a lost acknowledged push; if the next drill still loses one, the forge stays optional and is documented as NOT lossless on abrupt power loss. Tests: `test-forgejo-brick.py` (the script is run against a temporary tree; mutation-checked), Kyverno policies on the rendered pod. `docs/forgejo.md`.
- **Documented, not changed:** R4 gray failure as a known limit with no tuning of the primary lease; R3 (etcd leader partition) with what is not understood (`docs/resilience.md`).

### Changed (0.4.0, owner decisions of 2026-10-26; rung: mocked, offline, UNVERIFIED on a cluster)
- **`immediate: true` on the ScheduledBackups of `zitadel-db`, `grafana-db` and the customer profile** (`apps/components/cnpg-profile-backup`), as already on `forgejo-db`: a new Cluster gets a base backup at creation instead of WAL with no base backup until its 03:xx slot. One ScheduledBackup per Cluster, cron schedules untouched. CloudNativePG v1.30.1 creates the immediate Backup only while `status.lastCheckTime` is empty, so an existing object of an upgraded 0.3.x cluster does NOT trigger a backup (source in `docs/forgejo.md`). A fresh install starts up to three base backups together once, a one-time burst of writes to the one S3 endpoint. Tests: `test-cnpg-barman-plugin.py` and `test-cnpg-profiles.py` fail without it. UNVERIFIED on a cluster.
- **The HTTP criterion of the lab drills is re-posed:** longest run of consecutive failed samples at most 10 s AND failed samples at most 10 % of the drill window (1 Hz probe); measured: longest run at most 9 s in labs 25-27, fractions 2-3 % from a pod in the cluster and up to 9.2 % (40 of 436) from the lab host. The 10 % is fitted to these measurements; the old criterion (at most 5 isolated failures) was never met since lab 15c; two vantage points because one lab-host log had holes (10.9 s). See OpenAether-infra `docs/resilience.md`.

### Added (0.4.0, the Forgejo git forge, apps #170: PR #173 brought to the 0.4.0 standards; rung: mocked, offline, NOT run on a cluster)
- **What it is.** PR #173 (merge commit d6cc926, "dogfooding V1") put a Forgejo brick on main, written to the 0.3.x patterns, with a list of what it did not do. It is merged into this branch and brought up to what the other platform bricks are in 0.4.0. Forgejo **15.0.10** (LTS, end of support 2027-07-15), single replica, repositories on a `longhorn-x2-rwo` volume, in `services-git`, at `forge.openaether.local`. Nothing of it has ever run on a cluster. `docs/forgejo.md` has the whole account, the capacity arithmetic and the list of what the next lab must prove.
- **Defects of the first version found by reading it offline, all fixed:** the chart repository answered 404 (the chart is published only as `oci://code.forgejo.org/forgejo-helm/forgejo`; `type: oci` now and the Flux egress policy admits `code.forgejo.org`; `scripts/ha_render.py` and `test-chart-limits.py` render OCI repositories); chart 6.1.0 shipped Forgejo 7.0.3 and rendered a `postgresql-ha` and a `redis-cluster` next to the forge (chart 17.3.1: one Deployment, no sub-chart); the admin Secret lacked the `username` key the chart reads; nothing seeded `secret/forgejo/*`; the Gateway's egress policy did not admit the forge (a route that would have timed out; `istio/networkpolicy.yaml` has the rule now); self-registration was open and `/metrics` routed to the Internet on a route that answers anyone; the init containers had no limits, the pod no `runAsNonRoot`/`seccompProfile`, and `code.forgejo.org` was not on the Kyverno registry allow-list.
- **`forgejo-db` in the 0.4.0 form, copied from `grafana-db`:** 3 instances, quorum synchronous replication (`any`, 1, `required`, `failoverQuorum`), Barman Cloud plugin `ObjectStore`s for the primary and the replica store (with the `${BACKUP_*}` defaults and the `.invalid` placeholders of the platform), the Outscale checksum variables on the sidecar, zone preference, 30 s tolerations, `switchover` updates, a daily `ScheduledBackup` through the plugin, the same image line (`postgresql:16.15`, with #162/#163 open for it as for the others; no image added). **New in 0.4.0, so there is no 0.3.x upgrade path and nothing to presync.** It is its OWN Flux Kustomization (`forgejo-db`, group 36, `wait: true`; `forgejo` depends on it), not part of `cnpg`: the `workload` profile does not install three database pods it does not use, and a slow `forgejo-db` cannot hold `cnpg`, identity or observability back. `cnpg-store-replicate` lists `forgejo-db` (empty prefixes are a note).
- **OPT-IN, OFF by default (owner decision, 0.4.0 release gate: nothing may get worse).** The brick adds +400 m of CPU requests and 1.4 GiB of memory requests and has never run on a cluster, so `apps/flux/management` and `apps/flux/workload` do NOT render `forgejo` and `forgejo-db` (a `$patch: delete` each; both stay in the DAG, in the catalogue and under the `forge` alias of `bricks.yaml`). To run it: point the root Kustomization at the new overlay `apps/flux/management-forge` (the management DAG plus the two), or generate a profile with `python3 scripts/pick.py forge ... -o apps/flux/<name>` (OpenAether-self enables it in its own profile). A default install renders the same Deployment/StatefulSet/CNPG Cluster requests as at 0d7328a, before the merge (render diff in `docs/forgejo.md`). The vault configure Job still creates `secret/forgejo/db`, `secret/forgejo/admin` (generated, never printed, create-only) and `secret/forgejo/oidc` (a public placeholder) whichever profile runs: harmless with the brick off (three unread KV entries, like `minio/root`), and enabling the brick later needs no seeding step. Seeding is tested against a real OpenBao (`test-openbao-bootstrap.sh`). It is **not** a claim that the forge converges: no cluster has run it.
- **A default daily Longhorn backup of the repositories' volume, when the brick is on.** The PVC carries the group `oa-forgejo` (the chart's `persistence.labels`) and `RecurringJob forgejo-repos-backup-daily` (backup, 03:55, 14 kept, `apps/base/forgejo/recurringjob.yaml`) selects it, to the platform's Longhorn backup target (`backup_target_url`, the `BACKUP_TARGET_URL` identity value). The database and the volume are NOT backed up at the same instant (nothing quiesces the forge), so a restored pair can disagree; a restore of the volume has not been run on a cluster (the opt-in `longhorn-restore-test` component is the documented test). Rung: mocked, offline.
- **Capacity, only WITH the brick on (derived, not measured).** +400 m of CPU requests (forgejo-db 3 x 100 m, Forgejo 100 m): `scripts/check-ha.py` audits BOTH renders, the default one against the unchanged budgets (CNPG 600 m, Deployments 3300 m) and the `management-forge` one against a CNPG budget of 900 m (the Deployments stay at 3140 m of 3300 m); about +10 points of a worker, 4-worker examples from 223-228% to about 233-238% against the roll gate's 300%, with the brick only. A single-replica entry for `Deployment/services-git/forgejo` in `scripts/ha-allowlist.yaml`: no push or pull while the pod is rescheduled after a node loss (unmeasured), the volume survives the node.
- **Tests that fail without these changes:** `test-forgejo-brick.py` (+ `--selftest`, 26 mutations, among them the forge back in the default profile, a PVC without the backup label, a RecurringJob on another group, a snapshot job, an hourly one, one at the time of another job; with the Kyverno CLI it also renders the chart's pod and proves 3 more) pins the seeding of every OpenBao path the brick reads, the `username` key, the OCI source and its egress host, the separate `forgejo-db` Kustomization, registration off and `/metrics` unrouted; the CNPG tests (`barman-plugin` and its new "every Cluster under apps/base is rendered here" check, `archive-generation`, `identity-defaults`, `s3-checksum-env`, `eviction`, `primary-update`, `sync-replication`, `zone-spread`, `container-bounds`) now include `forgejo-db`; `test-chart-limits.py` renders the Forgejo chart; `test-scrape-paths.py` the `forgejo` scrape; `test-cnpg-store-replicate.sh` an absent `forgejo-db`; `test-openbao-bootstrap.sh` the three paths. `apps/flux/README.md`'s health-gate prose (it said 37 Kustomizations and 13 without a gate; the list and the test say 39 and 14 for the default `management` render, with or without the forge) is corrected.
- **Not delivered (each an open item, `docs/forgejo.md`):** SSH ingress, OIDC login, Valkey/multi-replica, image digest pinning, the proof that the repositories' volume restores (the backup job is delivered, no restore ran), an application-consistent copy of database and volume together (`forgejo dump`), `forgejo-db` in the restic dump, canary, restore tests and verifier of the backup brick, a rule on Forgejo's metrics, `readOnlyRootFilesystem` (the one Kyverno Audit finding left), the mail relay (#174) and the status page (#175).

### Documented (0.4.0, lab 27 sitting 2 finding F-PLUGIN-LOSS; rung: upstream source read, nothing changed in the manifests)
- **F-PLUGIN-LOSS** Why losing a node that holds a Barman Cloud plugin pod stalls the operator's reconcile of every Cluster for tens of seconds: in CloudNativePG v1.30.1 the plugin call timeout is the constant 30 s (`internal/cnpi/plugin/connection/connection.go`), the gRPC client has no keepalive, and the pooled connection is only dropped after a failed ping; it is not configurable, and the Service drops the dead pod only when its node is marked NotReady. `docs/resilience.md` gives the levers (failure-detection time, an upstream request with issue text); no manifest change is safe, none was made.

### Fixed (0.4.0, lab 27 sitting 2 findings F-FORGE-TOL, F-FORGEDB-BASE; rung: mocked, offline, UNVERIFIED on a cluster; lab 27 reproduced each on Scaleway)
- **F-FORGE-TOL** The Forgejo pod was only evicted 300 s after its node turned NotReady (Kubernetes' default toleration), so a node or zone loss kept the forge away about 8 minutes (push gap 471.8 s in the zone drill). The HelmRelease now sets the chart's `tolerations` to the databases' 30 s `not-ready` and `unreachable` NoExecute tolerations; `test-forgejo-brick.py` checks the value and the rendered Deployment (it fails without the change). The expected gap is about 2.5 to 3 minutes, projected from the drill's attach time and not measured; what is not known about the Longhorn re-attach on a partitioned node is in `docs/forgejo.md`. `scripts/ha-allowlist.yaml` says it.
- **F-FORGEDB-BASE** `forgejo-db` had a WAL archive and no base backup until the 03:40 ScheduledBackup, so no point-in-time recovery or restore for up to a day after the forge was enabled. Its ScheduledBackup now sets `immediate: true` (`spec.immediate`, checked in the vendored CRD): one base backup when the object is created, the daily schedule unchanged, still one ScheduledBackup. `zitadel-db`, `grafana-db` and the customer profile were left as they were at first and now set it too (see the "Changed" entry above). Not shown on a cluster: whether the backup succeeds while the Cluster still bootstraps.

### Fixed (0.4.0, lab 27 sitting 1 findings FI, FK, FG, FF; rung: mocked, offline, UNVERIFIED on a cluster; lab 27 reproduced each defect on Scaleway)
- **FI** The in-place restart of a primary (forced by the Barman plugin sidecar) waited out `smartShutdownTimeout` 180 s with the Zitadel pool connected: a 206.4 s write gap, not the 17-22 s of the client-less reduced lab. Every platform and profile Cluster now declares `smartShutdownTimeout: 5` from one shared component, `apps/components/cnpg-bounded-shutdown` (listed by `cnpg`, `forgejo-db` and `cnpg-profile-common`); the infra `cnpg-presync` sets the same value before the move. The value is read by the instance manager from its cached Cluster object, no restart needed. Expected gap 12-25 s, UNVERIFIED until the next lab. Test: `test-cnpg-shutdown.py` (mutation-checked, selftest), wired in CI. Docs: `docs/resilience.md`.
- **FK** (DATA LOSS, opt-in forge) Acknowledged pushes were lost on a zone power-off and the repository was left with 75 zero-length loose objects: git does not fsync loose objects by default. `gitea.config.git.config.core.fsync: all` is now in the HelmRelease (rendered as `[git.config]` `core.fsync=all`); `core.fsyncMethod` is left at git's default. Push latency cost UNVERIFIED; the fix itself is UNVERIFIED on a cluster until the zone drill is re-run with it. Test: `test-forgejo-brick.py` (values and rendered app.ini, mutation-checked). Docs: `docs/forgejo.md` (why Longhorn's two replicas do not cover it).
- **FG** The forge's daily Longhorn backup never selected its volume (`Found 0 volumes`): Longhorn does not copy a PVC label to its Volume. The volume now comes from the new StorageClass `longhorn-x2-rwo-forgejo` (the parameters of `longhorn-x2-rwo` plus `recurringJobSelector` naming the group `oa-forgejo`), set as the chart's `persistence.storageClass`; the PVC label is gone and the RecurringJob is kept. A volume created before keeps its class and needs the label by hand (`docs/forgejo.md`). Test: `test-forgejo-brick.py`. The restore of the volume was run once in lab 27 (289 MB backup, scratch volume, clone OK) after labelling by hand.
- **FF** (docs only) Enabling the forge on a running cluster is a live `kubectl patch` of the root Kustomization path; `docs/forgejo.md` gives the exact patch and says `cluster-up` neither sets nor reverts it. No tfvar was built.

### Documented (0.4.0, study LAB-B2: moving existing CNPG databases from the 0.3.x spec to this one; rung: mocked, offline, nothing changed in the manifests; evidence: a reduced real lab)
- `docs/resilience.md` ("The Barman Cloud plugin, two replicas") says why the 0.4.0 database spec must not reach a 0.3.x Cluster in one change: CloudNativePG 1.30.1's configuration-uniformity gate with a lost reload (cloudnative-pg#11627), and the freeze of
  every pod that predates the plugin sidecar once a plugin is enabled. The recipe (`task cnpg-presync` in the infra repository, before the apps ref moves) was measured on a REDUCED lab (one k3s node per VM, no Flux, Talos, Cilium or zones, run twice: write gap 17-22 s,
  0 acknowledged commits lost, WAL archive contiguous); it is UNVERIFIED on the full platform. No manifest changed. Not a compliance claim.

### Documented (0.4.0, infra #408: what the apps ref and the child versions are tied to; rung: mocked, offline, nothing changed in the manifests)
- Supported upgrade path, owner decision 2026-10-24: before 1.0.0 only the previous minor to the current one (0.3.x to 0.4.0). The in-place move of the 0.3.x content was observed once (lab 25); the supported, tested path is the one the release notes name.
- The apps ref a running cluster follows (`GitRepository openaether`, `GIT_REF` of `openaether-platform`) is moved by infra's `task cluster-apps-ref`, not by a machine-config change; `cluster-verify` compares it with `git_ref`.
- Gap, not built: nothing compares the `K8S_VERSION` / `TALOS_VERSION` declared in `apps/clusters/*.yaml` with what a child cluster runs (`scripts/check-child-clusters.py` is static).

### Fixed (0.4.0, lab 25 finding D9: a missing `cluster-identity` replica key turned the whole database layer NotReady; rung: mocked, offline, UNVERIFIED on a cluster)
- The replica ObjectStores (`apps/base/cnpg`, the `cnpg-profile-backup-replica` component, the restore-scratch copies) and the child-cluster identity default `BACKUP_REPLICA_S3_BUCKET` / `BACKUP_REPLICA_S3_ENDPOINT` to a NON-empty placeholder (`replica-not-set`, `https://replica-not-set.invalid`) instead of the empty string. Cause, reproduced with `flux build` 2.9.3: kustomize prints `endpointURL: ${VAR:=}` without quotes, Flux substitutes afterwards, and an empty result is a YAML null, which the ObjectStore CRD refuses (`must be of type string: "null"`), so `cnpg` and its 8 dependants stayed NotReady on a cluster whose ConfigMap predates the key. With the keys present nothing changes. The audit sink hosts already had non-empty defaults. `test-cnpg-identity-defaults.py` renders with the keys absent, empty and present, and fails on any other whole-field empty default not on its short list. Still open, listed in the test: the Longhorn `BACKUP_TARGET_URL`, the primary `BACKUP_S3_ENDPOINT` and `CNPG_ARCHIVE_SUFFIX` keep an empty default (their keys exist on every cluster). The values themselves reach a running cluster only through infra's `task cluster-identity-sync`.

### Fixed (0.4.0, lab 26 finding I7: the replica store could hold a base backup that cannot be restored; rung: mocked, offline, with the Job's own image)
- `cnpg-store-replicate` copies a base backup's `backup.info` last AND only once the WAL segment named by its `end_wal` is on the replica store; otherwise it says "waits" (counted as `deferred`, not a failure) and the next 15-minute run copies it. In lab 26 the copy ran a minute before the plugin archived the end segment, and the first recovery from the replica store failed six times ("could not locate required checkpoint record"). A `backup.info` without `end_wal` is copied as before. `test-cnpg-store-replicate.sh` covers deferral, the next run, the order, and an end segment already on the replica.

### Fixed (0.4.0, lab 26 finding B1: the second Barman Cloud plugin pod was never Ready; rung: mocked, offline, UNVERIFIED on a cluster)
- The plugin runs without `--leader-elect`. In v0.15.1 its gRPC server starts only on the pod holding the lease, so the standby of the two replicas stayed 0/1, the Deployment reached ProgressDeadlineExceeded, `cnpg` and its 8 dependants stayed NotReady and the plugin's PodDisruptionBudget blocked every drain (`task cluster-roll` refused). Without the flag both pods serve and are Ready; the ObjectStore controller runs twice, which is harmless (it patches Roles idempotently). Upstream fixed this in plugin-barman-cloud PR 1105 (merged 2026-10-02, in no release yet): put the flag back with that release. The false comment in `patch-barman-ha.yaml` is corrected and `test-cnpg-barman-plugin.py` fails if the flag returns. Lab 26 saw both pods 1/1 within about a minute with the flag off (lab-only commit, no drill); the drills to run are in `docs/resilience.md`.

### Fixed (0.4.0, lab 25 finding D3: a zone loss left every database without a primary for 388-402 s; rung: mocked, offline, UNVERIFIED on a cluster)
- The Barman Cloud plugin runs two replicas (hostname and zone spread, 30 s tolerations, RollingUpdate, a PodDisruptionBudget) and is no longer on the single-replica allowlist. In lab 25 one plugin pod in the powered-off zone kept the CNPG operator from failing over any plugin-backed database (write gap 388-402 s, zero acknowledged commit lost); with a second pod outside the zone the gap was 81-89 s. `test-cnpg-barman-plugin.py` fails on one replica, a Recreate rollout, a missing spread, long tolerations or a missing budget. To prove on the next lab: the zone drill again, and a node loss with the plugin pod on the node.

### Fixed (0.4.0, lab 25 finding D1: Grafana could not complete its OIDC token exchange; rung: mocked, offline, UNVERIFIED with real DNS)
- Grafana's egress policy now admits the Gateway pods on port 443. Its `token_url` and `api_url` are the public name of Zitadel, which is the Gateway: without the rule the callback hangs 30 s and returns to /login (a real Chromium sign-in in labs 24 and 25 only worked with rules added by hand). `test-zitadel-login-route.py` pins the rule. To prove on a cluster with public DNS and a trusted certificate: the lab used a host alias and a throwaway CA.

### Changed (0.4.0, apps #96: Istio 1.24.2 to 1.31.3 and Gateway API CRDs 1.2.1 to 1.6.0 for a FRESH install; rung: mocked, offline, NOT yet shown on a cluster)
- **Pins.** `istio-base`, `istiod`, `istio-cni`, `ztunnel` charts and the ztunnel image tag are 1.31.3; the vendored Gateway API standard-channel CRDs are v1.6.0 (the release Istio 1.31.3 is built against, its go.mod; the lock `apps/vendored-dumps.sha256` moved with it). Istio 1.31 needs Kubernetes 1.32 to 1.37 (endoflife.date), the cluster runs 1.37. Nothing else of our values was renamed or removed in the four charts (rendered with our values and `--kube-version 1.37.1`, 1.24.2 against 1.31.3 compared). A running cluster is not covered by this entry (CRDs and Istio must move together there).
- **The chart repository moved.** Istio 1.31 no longer publishes to `istio-release.storage.googleapis.com` (its index stops at 1.31.0-rc.0, 1.31.3 returns 404). The `HelmRepository` now points at `https://blob.istio.io/istio-release/charts`, which answers 308 to `istio-release.r2.istio.io`; the two Cilium egress policies of Flux allow both names instead of the old one. The charts pulled that way are byte-identical in content to the OCI ones (`ghcr.io/istio/release/charts`).
- **The waypoint Gateway used the wrong class.** `istio-waypoint` was a Gateway of class `istio` with an HBONE listener; istiod 1.31 refuses HBONE outside the waypoint controller (listener not Accepted, "only supported for waypoint proxies"). It is now class `istio-waypoint`; that class names the ServiceAccount `istio-waypoint` (no `-istio` suffix), so the principal in `allow-istiod-ztunnel` changed with it. No workload uses a waypoint (`docs/resilience.md`), the NetworkPolicy selects by the unchanged `gateway-name` label.
- **Unchanged on purpose, read in the 1.31.3 source:** the auto-provisioned names stay `openaether-gateway-istio` (Deployment, Service, ServiceAccount, label `gateway.networking.k8s.io/gateway-name`), the SDS authorization is still a SubjectAccessReview `list secrets` (the Role of `gateway-sds-rbac.yaml` still fits), `istiod` does not create an HPA or a PDB for the Gateway unless a `parametersRef` or GatewayClass overlay asks (so `gateway-ha.yaml` and the Kyverno spread policy keep working; the 1.31 overlay route is a possible simplification, not adopted).
- **Behaviour changes that touch us (upstream upgrade notes 1.25 to 1.31):** ambient DNS capture on by default (1.25/1.29, new pods only); CNI iptables reconciliation on by default; the CNI agent now honours `excludeNamespaces` (our three namespaces stay out); the `istio-cni-node` pod no longer runs with `hostNetwork`; `istiod` XDS debug endpoints on 15010 need authentication (`ENABLE_DEBUG_ENDPOINT_AUTH`); the standard CRDs of 1.6 add TLSRoute, TCPRoute, UDPRoute, ListenerSet and a `safe-upgrades` ValidatingAdmissionPolicy; Istio now reads BackendTLSPolicy v1 without an alpha switch (we use a DestinationRule). `meshConfig.meshMTLS.minProtocolVersion` is new and NOT set.
- **Calendar and VEX.** `docs/eol-calendar.yaml` and `.md`: Istio 1.31 ends 2027-02-28 (endoflife.date, read 2026-10-23; istio.io says "~Feb 2027 (Expected)" and 1.32 is due about mid-November, so the date can move); the Istio 1.24 waiver is gone, the two VEX statements for istiod and the CNI image (grpc, Go stdlib) are removed with it. Renovate: the Gateway API hold is now minor and major only.
- **Checks run offline:** yamllint, `check-eol.py` (+ `--check-doc`, its test), `check-vex.py`, `check-object-collisions.py` (549 objects), `check-gateway-routes.py`, `test-zitadel-login-route.py`, `test-gateway-ha.py`, the vendored-lock check and its test (made independent of the version it mutates), `helm template`/`helm lint` of the four charts, and every Gateway API object of the tree against the v1.6.0 CRD schemas (CEL rules not evaluated).
- **To prove on the next lab (nothing of this ran):** Flux fetches the index and the charts through the 308 redirect and the new FQDNs; the four HelmReleases become Ready in order; ambient dataplane with Cilium chaining (CNI conflist, `istio-cni-node` without `hostNetwork`, DNS capture on); HBONE between ztunnels; the Gateway auto-deployment (`openaether-gateway-istio` Ready, listeners Accepted with the 1.6 CRDs and the `safe-upgrades` policy); the waypoint Gateway Programmed under its new class; ztunnel upgrade behaviour (not part of a fresh install); Zitadel, Grafana, Vault UI flows through the Gateway (including the DestinationRule TLS to OpenBao); Kyverno spread and the HPA/PDB on the Gateway pods; an `openssl s_client` TLS floor check on the Gateway listener; and a trivy scan of the 1.31.3 images (the removed VEX statements were for the 1.24.2 images, nothing was scanned for 1.31.3).

### Fixed (0.4.0, apps #161: the Zitadel login page is reachable through the Gateway; rung: mocked, offline)
- The HTTPRoute of Zitadel sends `/ui/v2/login` to `zitadel-login:3000` and the Gateway's egress policy admits that pod on port 3000, nothing else of it. Until now a person in a browser ended on a 404 at the authorize step; only a script calling the Session API got through. `/ui/v2/login/_next/image` (the Next.js image optimizer, subject of the recorded Next.js advisories) is sent to the server and answers 404 on purpose; the VEX justification says so and `scripts/dev/test-zitadel-login-route.py` (run by CI) keeps the three rules. **Not yet shown with a browser:** a sign-in with a second factor into Grafana is a check for the next lab.

### Added (0.4.0, infra #318: a second sink outside the cluster for the audit log, class A, opt-in)

- **Component `audit-log-sink`, off in every profile** (a default install changes in no way): a shipper DaemonSet on the control planes reads the apiserver audit files read-only by byte range and stores them with `restic backup --stdin --no-lock` in an object-locked bucket outside the cluster (another provider, the same provider in another region, or both: the infra tfvars choose), client-side encrypted, one repository per node and UTC day, a position `(inode, offset)` acknowledged only when the snapshot is stored and **read back from the sink at start** (no local state). The apiserver never waits for it (no webhook backend). A file lost before it was shipped becomes an explicit gap snapshot. Component `audit-log-sink-secondary` is the same for the second sink.
- **Delivery check and alerts**: `audit-sink-check` lists the sink every 5 minutes (no password) and exports `oa_audit_sink_ok{sink}`, `oa_audit_sink_last_object_age_seconds{sink,node}`; `AuditSinkDown`, `AuditSinkStale`, `AuditSinkEmpty`, `AuditSinkShipperUnavailable`, `AuditSinkCheckAbsent` (critical, runbooks, vmalert unit cases); the four series are catalogued **UNVERIFIED** until a lab.
- **Narrow by construction**: own namespace `services-observability-audit-sink` (created by the component only), no ServiceAccount token, no Role, root with `DAC_READ_SEARCH` alone and a read-only root filesystem, egress to DNS and the sink's host on 443 only (host from `cluster-identity`, impossible default), Kyverno exemptions by pod name (`audit-sink-shipper-*`) in four policies, the check does not get the repository password. Two create-only paths (`audit/sink`, `audit/sink-secondary`) added to the `secret-seeder` OpenBao policy.
- **Tests**: `test-audit-sink-shipper.sh` (real restic 0.19.1 and busybox: whole lines, contiguous ranges, restart, rotation with `eof`, outage with no loss and no repeat, gap, no lock object), `test-audit-sink.py --selftest`, vmalert cases, Kyverno CLI cases, the OpenBao policy cases. Docs: `docs/audit-sink.md`, runbooks. **Rung: mocked/static plus a real restic in a container; never run on a cluster, Talos or a provider store.** Not a compliance claim.

### Added (0.4.0, owner rule of 2026-10-22: no acknowledged data loss)

- **Synchronous replication on both databases.** `zitadel-db` and `grafana-db` carry `postgresql.synchronous` (`method: any`, `number: 1`, `dataDurability: required`, `failoverQuorum: true`): a commit is acknowledged only after one replica has it, and a failover refuses a replica that may miss an acknowledged commit. `grafana-db` goes from 2 to 3 instances (with one replica, `required` would block every write on losing it). Writes now BLOCK, not lose data, when no replica streams: new critical alert `CnpgSyncReplicaMissing` with a runbook (the metric `cnpg_pg_replication_streaming_replicas` is UNVERIFIED on a cluster). The Kyverno guard of the restore-test scratch Clusters refuses a synchronous setting, the scratch manifest never has one. Tests: `test-cnpg-sync-replication.py` (keys against the vendored CRD, durability, `number` <= instances - 1 with a replica to spare, the scratch Cluster), a Kyverno case, a vmalert case.
- **Capacity, said honestly.** `scripts/check-ha.py` did not count the CNPG instance pods; it now does (instances x (instance + Barman sidecar request): 600 m for 6 instances, 500 m before, budget 600 m). Deployments and StatefulSets stay at 3015 m of 3300 m. The extra instance is about 100 m of CPU, 384 Mi of memory and 5 Gi of local-path storage; the 4-worker examples still pass the roll gate (about 226-231% against 300%), the two-worker zone still does not.
- **Data classes and the meaning of zero interruption** (`docs/resilience.md`): class A (identity, dashboards, secrets, application data) loses no acknowledged commit; class B (metrics, logs) has a bounded, documented loss, to be confirmed by the owner; a single-primary database has a bounded write gap that clients mask by retrying, a true 0 s write path needs a distributed database and is not planned. The lab pass criteria of the zero-loss proof (acknowledged-id writer at 100/s, pod kill, node power-off, forced reboot, zone loss, and an asynchronous control) are written there. NOT run on a cluster.
- **Database connections** (`docs/database-failover-clients.md`): Zitadel recycles connections after 5 minutes (`MaxConnLifetime`, `MaxConnIdleTime`), Grafana after 300 s (`conn_max_lifetime`, `max_open_conn`, `max_idle_conn`); `test-db-client-settings.py` reads both in the rendered charts. That Zitadel's pinned version reads these keys is UNVERIFIED. A short guide for client applications: idempotent retries, pool recycling, timeouts, no long transactions.
- **Fixed:** `process_start_time_seconds` (read by `FluxReconciliationStalled`) was missing from the alert-metric catalogue, so `test-alert-metrics.py` failed on the branch.

### Added (0.4.0, apps #157: CNPG database profiles for customer applications)

- **Three named profiles, off by default** (`apps/components/cnpg-profile-{light,standard,ha}`, copy `cnpg-profile-examples/<profile>` per application): `light` (1 instance, no replication, no backup: a node loss is downtime), `standard` (2 instances, quorum `ANY 1`, durability `preferred`: commits acknowledged while the replica is missing can be lost; one backup store), `ha` (3 instances, quorum `ANY 1`, durability `required`, failover quorum, two stores, PgBouncer). Each: TLS and SCRAM only in `pg_hba`, no superuser access, the owner's password from an ExternalSecret, a per-database CiliumNetworkPolicy, restricted PSA, 30 s tolerations, hostname and zone spread. One generated ConfigMap of values feeds Kustomize replacements. Nothing in the default install changes. Not run on a cluster: `docs/database-profiles.md` marks every number `UNMEASURED` and lists what is unverified.
- **PgBouncer Pooler for `ha`**: transaction mode, 2 pods, a disruption budget of one, spread, `query_wait_timeout` 150 s so a client waits through a failover, `server_login_retry` 1 s, TCP keepalives, protocol-level prepared statements; its limits (session state, SQL-level `PREPARE`, advisory locks, in-flight transaction at a failover) and the UNVERIFIED `verify-full` to the pooler are written down.
- **Benchmark and zero-loss tooling**: `scripts/ops/db-bench.sh` (pgbench read-write and read-only against the primary Service and the pooler: TPS, average and exact p50/p95/p99 from the transaction logs, in the table format of the doc), `db-zeroloss.sh` (acknowledged-id writer, a fresh connection per commit, and its checker), also as suspended Jobs in the opt-in component `cnpg-profile-bench`. Tests on a real PostgreSQL: a lost id and a deleted row are caught, a crash restart loses no acknowledged id.
- **Checks**: `test-cnpg-profiles.py` (render, vendored CRD shape, guarantees, values mechanism, platform Kyverno policies with CLI 1.19.1), `test-database-profiles-doc.py`, `test-db-bench-parse.py`, `test-db-bench.sh`; `check-ha.py --profiles` audits the examples like the bricks and bounds their CPU requests (100, 600, 1100 m).

### Observed and fixed (lab 23, Scaleway + OVH buckets, 3 control planes + 5 workers, rung: observed on a cluster)

- **Zero-loss proof, observed**: an acknowledged-id writer through `zitadel-db`, an `ha` profile Cluster and its pooler lost **0** acknowledged ids in a pod kill, a worker power-off, a forced reboot, a zone power-off (twice), both replicas down (three lengths) and a primary lost together with a replica (FailoverQuorum waited, no promotion); the asynchronous control lost **817 of 15916 / 1409 of 16432** in the double fault. Gaps, costs (about -25% write TPS, +2.2 ms per commit across zones) and the table: `docs/resilience.md`, `docs/database-profiles.md`. Zitadel's `MaxConnLifetime` is read (a connection closed at 301 s).
- **Database profiles on a cluster** (the first time): numbers (3 runs, median) for `light`, `standard`, `ha` (direct and pooler), security checklist observed, failover through the pooler measured.
