Fixed on the way: the CNPG operator's egress reached only two namespaces, so a profile elsewhere stayed at 1 of 3 instances (`apps/base/cnpg/networkpolicy.yaml`); `verify-full` to the pooler failed (the profile now names the pooler in `serverAltDNSNames`); the pooler accepted plaintext clients (`client_tls_sslmode: require`); the bench Jobs had no egress to their database (policy in `cnpg-profile-bench`); profiles were not scraped (a `VMPodScrape` per profile in `cnpg-profile-common`); the pooler's init container had no limits (Kyverno `require-limits`); `CnpgSyncReplicaMissing` fired for good on the `light` profile (it needs `cnpg_collector_sync_replicas{value="expected"} >= 1` now). Each pinned in `test-cnpg-profiles.py` or the rule unit tests.
- **Audit sink, first run on a cluster**: delivery, lock, escrow decryption, a 2 h 29 min window without a gap across 7 rotations, a shipper restart and a 40 minute sink outage, network drop (`docs/audit-sink.md`, infra `docs/audit-sink.md` section 10). Fixed: the shipper chased the growing audit file (a snapshot every one to two seconds, killed by its own liveness probe): a cycle now ships what the file held when it began (`test-audit-sink-shipper.sh`: 21 snapshots in one cycle before, 1 after); `AuditSinkDown` fired after 16 min 41 s against a 15 minute target and now needs two failed listings (`for: 5m`).
- Not proven: `standard` and `light` under a node loss; Longhorn under a profile; the cause of a 314.5 s zone-loss gap through the pooler (once; 60 s on the repeat).

### Observed and fixed (lab 22, Scaleway, 3 control planes + 5 workers, rung: observed on a cluster)

- The lab 21 follow-up changes ran on a cluster (database failover 44-64 s for a primary on a powered-off worker, 6 s with a CoreDNS replica lost, kube-state-metrics at two replicas); a database primary inside a lost ZONE took 122 s because the kube-controller-manager leader was in the zone (`docs/resilience.md`, "Measured in lab 22").
- **Kyverno Audit noise (#47)**: `readOnlyRootFilesystem` on the VictoriaMetrics CRs, a memory limit on the operator's `config-reloader` / `config-init` (not on a VMAlert's `config-init`: the operator refuses the patch), `spec.resources` on `zitadel-db`. `require-ro-rootfs` 14 -> 0, `require-limits` 13 -> 2 on an idle cluster; table in `docs/kyverno-enforcement.md`. Static tests extended (`test-container-security-context.py`, `test-container-bounds.py`).
- **`FluxObjectSuspended`** exempted `alloy-audit` in the wrong namespace (`services-observability` instead of `services-observability-audit`) and would have fired after 24 h on every default install; fixed, unit test corrected.
- `BackupCopyJobFailed` stays firing after the cause is fixed until the failed Jobs are deleted: runbook says so.
- **`FluxReconciliationStalled` false positive after a node loss**: the counters of a replaced Flux controller's dead pod stay in the 150 minute window as flat series (`increase()` = 0), so seven HelmReleases raised this critical alert 19 minutes after a zone loss on a healthy cluster. The rule now needs a live controller older than its window (`process_start_time_seconds` joined on the pod); the new unit case fails without the join.
- **Kyverno background and reports controllers crash-looped** (lab 21 and 22: `client rate limiter Wait returned an error` on their lease, `failed to wait for cache sync`): the shipped 500m CPU limit throttled 89-97% of their CPU periods (use 0.35-0.62 CPU). They now get 2 CPUs and `--clientRateLimitQPS=100 --clientRateLimitBurst=200`; stable for 15 minutes afterwards on the lab. The two changes were made together, so which of them matters is not separated. A static check pins both.

### Added (0.4.0, owner decisions of 2026-10-21)

- **Kyverno: why Audit is the default and how to switch a namespace** (#37): `docs/kyverno-enforcement.md` records the decision, the label steps, the rollback and what the labs proved; the remaining noise stays in #47.
- **Repo-settings check: informational checks** (#141): `.github/informational-checks.txt` declares the checks that run on purpose without being required (Commit messages, Pipeline audit, OpenBao bootstrap, Zitadel and Grafana authentication);
  `check-required-checks.sh` accepts them, and flags a stale declaration (required, or no longer reported). The check turns green once the owner binds the five required checks to the GitHub Actions app and adds Backup brick checks,
  Loki chart render, Observability RBAC, VMRules and Manifest scan as required (**owner action in the ruleset**). Self-tested.
- **Renovate holds `kubernetes-sigs/gateway-api`** until the Istio upgrade (the CRDs stay at 1.2.1, #57).
- **Named identities** (#87): `apps/base/platform/rbac/README.md` states that 0.4.0 uses short-lived client certificates, that there is no individual revocation, what deleting a binding does, and the 1.0.0 plan for an optional user-supplied external OIDC issuer (not built).

### Added (0.4.0, milestone M4: loss of a node or a pod without interruption)

Rung: **mocked/static**; nothing observed on a cluster (drills and pass criteria: OpenAether-infra `docs/resilience.md`, and `docs/resilience.md` here). Not a compliance claim. Style: six files that
broke the yamllint line length (commits 577a0b3, 5747fd8, e7f04b5, 2eb74d6) are rewrapped.

- **HA audit in CI** (`scripts/check-ha.py`, `scripts/ha-allowlist.yaml`, `test-check-ha.py`): every Deployment and StatefulSet of the bricks (charts rendered), and the VM and CNPG pods, has two replicas or a
  reasoned allow-list entry saying what its loss means, a hostname spread, exactly one PodDisruptionBudget allowing exactly one disruption, nothing that strands a pod on three workers; the CPU requests are bounded.
  35 workloads audited, 13 single-replica listed with reasons, 1 deliberate budget exception (Loki), 1 strict spread (OpenBao).
- **Fixes the audit found**: cert-manager and istiod spread, budgets; ESO controller (leader election) and webhook, Kyverno admission (apps #82, first point), the CNPG operator, metrics-server and the Zitadel
  login at two replicas with spread and budgets; Alertmanager at two replicas with gossip allowed in the network policy; Longhorn UI at one, listed. About 345 m of CPU requests added (derived). Two operators of CNPG,
  ESO leader election and the Alertmanager gossip are UNVERIFIED on a cluster.
- **Alerts that name the command** (`vmrule-loss.yaml`): `ControlPlaneMemberMissing`, `FailureDomainLost`, `WorkloadReplicasBelowDesired`, `ClusterCannotLoseWorker`, `OpenBaoReplicasMissing`,
  `AlertmanagerClusterDegraded`; `NodeUnreachable` after 3 min and `NodeNotReady` after 5; runbook section "Loss of capacity" (which task, and what not to do); vmalert unit cases. `kube_node_role`, the zone label
  and `alertmanager_cluster_members` are UNVERIFIED series (`docs/monitoring-0.4.md`).

### Fixed (lab 21 follow-up, offline: apps #154, #155, #156; to be proven by the next lab)

Rung: **mocked/static**. Not a compliance claim; the lab pass criteria are in `docs/resilience.md` (last section).

- **CNPG primary eviction** (#154): both Clusters tolerate `unreachable` and `not-ready` for 30 s instead of 300 s, so a primary on a lost node is replaced after about 60 s, not 328-333 s; 30 s equals the liveness window of the instance manager (an isolated primary stops itself first). `test-cnpg-eviction.py` in CI. The 30 s write gap of a power loss is NOT reachable this way (28-33 s are detection and promotion); the other stateful workloads and a Kyverno mutation were evaluated and left alone, with the reasons.
- **kube-state-metrics at two replicas** (#156): zone and hostname spread, the existing budget, the scrape drops `pod` and `instance`, vmstorage and vmselect deduplicate (`-dedup.minScrapeInterval=30s`), `KubeStateMetricsDuplicated` (with vmalert cases) says if the merge breaks. CPU requests of the audited workloads 3005 m -> 3015 m. The loss rule group is evaluated every 30 s.
- **Zone spread** (#155): a `ScheduleAnyway` zone term next to the hostname one for cert-manager, ESO, istiod, metrics-server, Kyverno admission, Zitadel, Grafana, OpenBao, Loki, VMCluster, vmagent, vmalert, Alertmanager and the Gateway pods; `check-ha.py` prints a warning list and a coverage line.

### Fixed (found by lab 21, Scaleway, loss drills, 2026-10-21)

Rung: **observed on Scaleway** for the drills and the fixes below (3 control planes, 4 then 5 workers, three zones; results in OpenAether-infra `docs/resilience.md` and the lab results). Not a compliance claim.
Style: the loss alerts are checked by vmalert-tool cases and the runbook anchors by `test-vmrules.sh`.

- **`NodeScrapeDown`** (new, `vmrule-loss.yaml`): a node whose kubelet is not scraped for 3 minutes. kube-state-metrics is ONE pod, and every kube_* loss rule is blind while it sits on the lost node (no node alert at all during a 5 minute loss); `up{job=kubelet} == 0` is seen at 1 min 51 s. First version suppressed itself when kube-state-metrics said the node was unknown, which restarted its `for`: neither alert fired in a 5 minute loss. Now without the suppression (a loss pages twice, this one first, 4 min 44 s - 4 min 53 s measured).
- **`FailureDomainLost` reads the kubelet scrape** (the zone label comes from the Node through the VMNodeScrape), not kube-state-metrics, and has no `for`: 3 min 11 s from the power-off of a zone with `for: 1m` through kube-state-metrics, 1 min 42 s - 2 min 2 s now.
- **`AlertmanagerClusterDegraded` could never fire for a lost replica**: `alertmanager_cluster_members` stayed at 2 on the survivor (the dead peer stays counted) and `alertmanager_cluster_failed_peers` keeps a ghost for hours. It also fires when fewer Alertmanager pods answer the scrape than replicas.
- **Zone spread of the databases and the CNPG operator**: both grafana-db instances and both operator replicas had landed in one zone (hostname spread only; `failure-domain-check` red). A preferred zone anti-affinity term on both CNPG Clusters and a ScheduleAnyway zone spread on the operator; `test-cnpg-zone-spread.py` in CI. Placement of pods that already exist does not move: delete the replica's pod and PVC (clone under a minute). After it, losing the zone that held grafana-db no longer takes the database (drill 4c).
- Catalogue: `kube_node_role` and `alertmanager_cluster_members` are existing (read in lab 21); `kube_node_labels` left it (no alert reads it any more).
- Documentation: `resilience.md` (the 4 x 4 vCPU examples), runbook for NodeScrapeDown, the Alertmanager metric.
- **Not fixed, with issues**: both CoreDNS replicas on one worker (cluster DNS outage, owned by Talos), a database primary on a lost node failed over after about 5 minutes (328-333 s write gap, target 30 s), zone-level spread of the other two-replica workloads, kube-state-metrics as a single point for the remaining loss alerts and their 4-7 minute latency.

### Fixed (0.4.0, lab 20 finding #153)

- **A cluster rebuilt by the DR chain archives under a new `serverName`, and everything that reads the archive follows it.** The Clusters' plugin parameter is `serverName: <cluster>${CNPG_ARCHIVE_SUFFIX:=}`; Flux fills the suffix from the optional ConfigMap `cnpg-archive-generation` (flux-system) that the infra `dr-restore-plan` writes. No ConfigMap means no suffix:
  installs, the lab 19/20 paths and the ObjectStore `destinationPath` do not move. `backup-verify` (`barman_check.py`) reads `cnpg/<cluster>/<cluster><suffix>/` through `cluster-identity`, the CNPG restore tests take the serverName of the live Cluster, `cnpg-store-replicate` already copies the whole
  `cnpg/<cluster>/` prefix (both generations; now covered by a test). The previous generation stays in both stores as the recovery source (README "Archive generation", `docs/traps.md`). Tests: `test-cnpg-archive-generation.py` (render with and without the suffix, the Flux wiring, the verifier against an
  in-memory store holding an old and a new archive, the restore manifest), `test-cnpg-store-replicate.sh` (a `-g2` path). No alert changed. Rung: **mocked**; UNVERIFIED on a cluster (that CNPG applies a changed `serverName` to an existing Cluster, Flux's optional `substituteFrom`, the plugin's Backup); pass criteria in the
  infra `docs/disaster-recovery.md`.

### Added (0.4.0, milestone M3: restore tests)

- **Scheduled restore tests, no person involved** (`backup/databases/restore`, `cnpg`, README "Restore tests"): `cnpg-canary` writes a hash-chained canary row into zitadel-db and grafana-db every 5 minutes; `cnpg-recovery-restore-test` (daily) recovers each
  Cluster from the primary AND the replica store into a one-instance scratch Cluster in the new namespace `backup-restore-test`, compares the restored canary chain and tables with the live database and deletes the Cluster; `cnpg-pitr-restore-test` (weekly) recovers to
  the time of one canary row and must end at exactly that row; `cnpg-dump-load-restore-test` (daily) loads the restic dumps of both repositories into a private PostgreSQL. Series `oa_restore_test_*` (ok, last success, last run, duration, recovery seconds, data lag) through the verifier's
  exporter, which now merges the verifier's and the restore tests' ConfigMaps (`serve_metrics.py`). A failed comparison fails the Job, so the M1 alert `RestoreTestNotSucceeding` sees it too.
- **Opt-in component `longhorn-restore-test`**: a 1 GiB canary volume Longhorn backs up with the daily recurring job (24 files, each a function of its generation number), and a weekly Job that restores the newest backup into a scratch Longhorn Volume and checks every file. No cluster-scoped right:
  a static PV and PVC stay bound to a name; the Job creates and deletes one Longhorn Volume and one pod.
- **Alerts** (`vmrule-restore-test.yaml`, runbooks, vmalert cases): `RestoreTestFailed`, `RestoreTestNoRecentPass`, `RestoreTestNeverReported`, `RestoreRecoverySlow`, `RestoreScratchLeftover`, `CnpgCanaryNotWriting`, `LonghornRestoreTestNeverReported`, `LonghornCanaryNotWriting`; the new series are
  catalogued UNVERIFIED.
- **A restore test never touches a live object**: the scratch namespace (restricted, ResourceQuota: one Cluster, 3 CPU, 3 GiB), a Role there and read-only elsewhere (no ClusterRole), the script's own guard (`backup-restore-test/rt-*` only), and the Kyverno policy `restrict-restore-test-clusters` (Enforce, `failurePolicy: Ignore`: an `rt-`
  Cluster outside the scratch namespace, or one that archives or has several instances, is refused). `ObjectStores` there carry no retention policy. The namespace joins the ClusterSecretStore conditions for its two S3 key pairs. Scratch objects are removed on failure and on SIGTERM (finalizer-safe); `activeDeadlineSeconds` on every Job.
- Tests: `test-restore-test-cnpg.py` (the comparison, the guard, the metrics, the whole flow against a kubectl double for each damage, SIGTERM, a stuck finalizer), `test-restore-test-cnpg-sql.sh` (real PostgreSQL: writer, snapshot, each damage, the dump load as user postgres), `test-restore-test-dumps.sh`
  (real restic, two fake S3 stores), `test-restore-test-longhorn.py`, `test-restore-test-brick.py` (rights, quota, ObjectStores, tokens), the Kyverno CLI guard cases, 8 vmalert cases. Rung: **mocked/real tools, nothing ran on a cluster**.
- UNVERIFIED until a lab: the plugin recovery into `backup-restore-test` (RBAC the plugin creates, the egress policy, the operator reaching the scratch instance), the lag budgets (primary 20 min, replica 50 min), the Longhorn `Volume` fields and the static PV, the exporter merge, every new series, the quota sizing on 4 vCPU workers.
  The OpenBao raft snapshot is still only restored as far as its sums (the DR chain in the infra repository restores it into an OpenBao); no `bao operator raft snapshot inspect` check was added.

### Added (0.4.0, milestone M2: backups complete, replicated, verified)

- **CNPG moves to the Barman Cloud plugin** (#114): both Clusters leave the in-tree `barmanObjectStore` that CNPG 1.31 removes. Plugin v0.15.1 vendored next to the operator (lock, EOL calendar, Renovate), one `ObjectStore` per Cluster
  with the SSE-S3 request (`AES256`), the Outscale checksum fix (#132) now in the sidecar environment, `ScheduledBackup` with `method: plugin`, `cert-manager` in the `dependsOn` of `cnpg`, a network policy for the plugin. **Encryption, exactly:**
  the plugin has no client-side encryption (its CRD offers `AES256` and `aws:kms`, both server-side); the archive is protected by TLS in transit, the store's encryption at rest if it honours the header (measured with the same header on artifacts on Scaleway and OVH, not through barman, not on Outscale) and the key pair. The client-side encrypted copy stays the daily restic dump. The plugin form of a recovery has not been run on a cluster (lab 19 ran it: PITR into a new Cluster in 72 s from the primary store, 66 s from the replica store alone). What IS client-side encrypted: the restic datasets (`openbao`, `cnpg-dump`, `etcd`, key = restic password). What is NOT: the WAL and base-backup bytes of the plugin and their copy on the replica store (`cnpg-store-replicate` streams the same bytes and asks for SSE-S3; the header was read back on the OVH objects in lab 19). Open as #149.
- **Replica copy of the CNPG archive**: `cnpg-store-replicate` (every 15 min) copies `cnpg/<cluster>/` to the replica store with the replica key pair only, prunes only what left the primary 45 days ago and never against an empty primary or more than half of the replica; `*-replica` ObjectStores and a `cnpg-backup-s3-replica` ExternalSecret serve a recovery from the replica store; `cluster-identity` gains optional `BACKUP_REPLICA_S3_*`.
- **Scheduled etcd snapshot** (`backup-etcd`, `etcd-snapshot` 04:10 UTC): Talos API access with the role `os:etcd:backup` only (infra `etcd_snapshot_api_access`), memory-backed volume, gate on the bolt magic, restic to both stores with the retention of the other datasets. Replaces "nothing runs it for you".
- **Backup verifier** (`backup-verify`, 05:10 UTC, `backup-verify-exporter`, `VMServiceScrape`): exports `oa_backup_verify_ok` and `oa_backup_verify_last_success_timestamp_seconds` (the series the monitoring alerts wait for) plus per-store series: restic data subset, restore of the openbao and etcd snapshots with content checks, Barman base backup read to its end and WAL continuity on both stores, replica lag, Longhorn backup state. Alerts: `BackupCopyJobFailed`, `CnpgStoreReplicationStale`, `EtcdSnapshotStale`, `BackupVerifierStale`, `BackupStoreVerificationFailed`.
- `check-backup-key-rights.sh` tries the `AES256` server-side-encryption request that the ObjectStores and the copy send.
- Policy and hardening: the image registry policy (Audit) admits `ghcr.io/siderolabs/talosctl` alone for the etcd Job; the plugin's two mTLS Certificates name `rotationPolicy: Always`; `test-backup-kyverno.sh` runs the Kyverno CLI on the rendered workloads of the backup and CNPG bricks.
- Tests: `test-cnpg-barman-plugin.py` (render, CRD shape, Kyverno CLI), `test-cnpg-store-replicate.sh`, `test-backup-verify.sh` (real restic, a real Barman archive from a throwaway PostgreSQL, a real etcd snapshot, damage injected), `test-etcd-snapshot.sh`, `test-backup-independent-stores.sh`; `scripts/dev/testdata/fake_s3_server.py` is the store they share.

### Fixed (found by lab 20, Scaleway + OVH replica store, 2026-10-20)

- The etcd snapshot Job's discovery init container failed on a real cluster: kubectl 1.36 ignores the in-cluster config when `--request-timeout` is passed and asks `localhost:8080` (the #150 fix had only met a fake kubectl). The timeout is the shell's now; the manifest test refuses the flag.
- The Longhorn canary volume was never backed up: it is detached except for the seconds the hourly writer holds it, and Longhorn's recurring jobs skip a detached volume unless `allow-recurring-job-while-volume-detached` is true (default false). The opt-in `longhorn-restore-test` component sets it.
- `BackupNotSucceededRecently`, `BackupCronJobStale` and `BackupCronJobSuspended` did not know the etcd snapshot CronJob (a failed Job was already seen by `BackupCopyJobFailed`, but a snapshot that stops being taken or succeeding was not). They select it now; unit case added.
- A stale exclusive restic lock (a pod killed during `forget --prune`) made `restic cat config` fail, and the openbao, cnpg-dump and etcd push scripts read that as a repository of another cluster and stopped (lab 20: every push and the verifier failed on the primary store until `restic unlock` was run by hand). The scripts remove stale locks before they look at the repository.
- The CNPG restore test failed a good copy of the primary store when the canary wrote a row between the live snapshot and the end of the recovery ("restored canary row N is newer than anything live"; the archive of the primary store is immediate). A copy that ends after the live snapshot passes when it still holds the live newest row with the same hash; another history is still refused.
- The Loki HelmRelease gave up on a cold install: three replicas need about 12 minutes to start (the pods exit after ten failed memberlist joins until one is Ready) and the install budget was the default 5 minutes, so after 4 attempts the release stayed Stalled (RetriesExceeded) with the pods Running for hours and `FluxObjectNotReady` firing. The install gets the upgrade's 15 minutes.
- `BackupJobFailed` also fired for a failed `cnpg-dump-load-restore-test-*` Job (its name starts like `cnpg-dump-`) as a critical "backup failed"; the restore tests have their own alerts (`RestoreTestFailed`, `RestoreTestNotSucceeding`). The Job is excluded.
- The Longhorn restore test could never find its backup: it read the BackupVolume by the volume's name, but Longhorn names it `<volume>-<8 hex>` (the label `backup-volume` carries the volume), so the Job always skipped with "no backup yet". The offline double had the same wrong assumption; it now refuses a lookup by bare name.
- The Longhorn restore test verified `/data` while the canary writer puts its files in `/data/canary`: a good restore ended "no MANIFEST on the restored volume". It verifies `/data/canary`; the kubectl double now refuses any other path.
- The Kyverno policy `restrict-restore-test-clusters` did not enforce on a real cluster: created before the CNPG CRDs existed, Kyverno 1.19 kept it without its kind and admitted an `rt-x` Cluster into `foundation-databases` (a plain annotation update made it deny, and a copy of its rule created later denied at once). It lives in its own brick `kyverno-policies-cnpg`, after `cnpg`, like the External Secrets guard; the enforcement test checks the brick and its `dependsOn`.

### Fixed (found by lab 19, Scaleway + OVH replica store, 2026-10-20)

- `backup-databases` never became Ready: the generated verifier ConfigMap had no namespace (`test-backup-namespaces.sh`).
- vmagent could not scrape `backup-verify-exporter` (egress 9101 closed): no `oa_backup_verify_*` series until fixed (`test-scrape-paths.py`).
- The verifier reported a locked restic repository as a skip, and let the last CNPG cluster hide a failure of the other (a WAL gap in `zitadel-db` was invisible); both now fail loudly (`test-backup-verify-listing.sh`, `test-backup-verify-aggregate.py`).
- `LonghornVolumeDegraded` / `LonghornVolumeFaulted` read `longhorn_volume_robustness` as 1/2/3; Longhorn 1.12.1 exports a series per `state` (one-hot), so they could never fire. `PdbBlocksDisruption` fired after 1 h on every healthy cluster (CNPG `*-primary`, Longhorn `instance-manager-*` budgets): excluded.
- The 41 metrics marked UNVERIFIED were read on a cluster and are now `existing` (only `longhorn_backup_state` stays UNVERIFIED).

### Fixed (second pass on lab 19: #150, #151, #152; offline only, to be proven by the next lab)

- **The scheduled etcd snapshot (#150).** As shipped the Job stopped at "nodes are not set for the command", and the endpoint of the Talos grant (`talos.default`) resolved to nothing. A first init container (`discover-controlplane`, its own ServiceAccount `etcd-snapshot` with get/list on Nodes only, the token mounted in that container alone) lists the control-plane nodes through the Kubernetes API, tries each InternalIP on port 50000 in random order until one accepts a connection, and writes a copy of the grant's talosconfig whose endpoint and node are that address; no node found, no answer or a refused API call fails the Job (`BackupCopyJobFailed`). Nothing to seed; the network policy opens the Kubernetes API. Proven offline: the real `alpine/k8s` image with a fake `kubectl`, `nc` and `talosctl` (`test-etcd-snapshot.sh`). **Not proven:** the Job on a cluster (lab 19 worked only with `-e <ip> -n <ip>` by hand: 97.7 MB, both stores, verifier PASS), nor `talosctl bootstrap --recover-from` on a throwaway Talos cluster. The port probe is a TCP connect, not a Talos handshake: a node that accepts the connection but fails the call fails the Job, the next run picks again at random.
- **A wiped primary store (#151).** `cnpg-store-replicate` exited 0 and the verifier said SKIP after the primary bucket was emptied. The copy Job now fails when the primary lists no object while the replica holds some (nothing is pruned on the replica then); the verifier turns a store without repository or archive into a failure (`oa_backup_verify_ok` 0, `store_ok{store="primary"}` 0, `MISSING` message) when another store of the same dataset (the same cluster for `cnpg`) passed. Both stores empty stays a skip, and so does both stores wiped at once (nothing to compare: not detected). Tests: fake stores, a unit test, the real-restic verifier test with a wiped primary.
- **Monitoring (#152, the small ones).** `CnpgInstanceMissing` (a vanished pod exports nothing: instances reporting against the maximum of the last day; a deliberate scale-down looks the same for up to a day). `PodPendingTooLong` leaves out Pods whose init containers run (`kube_pod_init_container_status_running`, UNVERIFIED until a lab reads it). `ClusterCpuRequestsHigh` / `ClusterMemoryRequestsHigh` count Running pods. `AlertDeliveryFailing` looks at 15 min, not 1 h. The heartbeat route repeats every 3m (group_interval 1m): at most 4 min between pings (5m measured 360 s); `test-alert-delivery.py` keeps repeat + group within 5 min. Documented: stopping Alertmanager needs `spec.paused: true` before `scale --replicas=0`; an OVH replica pair cannot be isolated per bucket. Each rule has a vmalert unit case that fails without the change. **Not changed:** the Flux revision bump that turns every Kustomization briefly "not up to date" (guarded by `for: 20m`, never fired).

### Fixed (backups)

- **A failing destination no longer keeps the other from receiving its copy**: in `openbao-snapshot` and `cnpg-dump` the first failing store ended the Job before the replica was written; failures are now recorded and fail the Job last.

## [0.3.2] — 2026-10-22

0.3.2 is a **security patch** of 0.3.1, paired with OpenAether-infra 0.3.2 (which only changes its `git_ref` pin). It ships the fixes that were merged on main after 0.3.1
(below, "Fixed") and, above all, the **Zitadel v4.19.5** update. No new scope. Rungs: **mocked** (static checks, a real Zitadel v4.19.5 and PostgreSQL in containers through
`scripts/dev/test-zitadel-config.sh`, Kyverno CLI on the rendered workloads) and **real cloud, once, by hand** (lab 24, Scaleway, 3 control planes + 4 workers, 2026-10-22): an upgrade from the 0.3.1 content (Zitadel v4.19.2) to this patch (4 min 12 s from the switch to a Ready HelmRelease; the generated session-cookie key was created before the login pods rolled; a 1 Hz probe of the public Zitadel URL saw 620 samples and 0 failures; Grafana OIDC logins through the rollout all passed) and a fresh install of the patch (cluster-verify 15 passed, 0 failed, all Flux objects Ready). Not observed: the open login sessions ending at the rollout (no browser in the lab), the device-flow consent screen, and a repeat on OVH or Outscale.
It fixes neither #42 nor #375 (infra), **does not fix #134** and does not make the Zitadel recovery automatic: a person still runs it. Not a compliance claim.

### Security

- **Zitadel server and login v4.19.2 -> v4.19.5**: three HIGH advisories, published 2026-10-15, fixed in v4.19.5 (affected 4.0.0 to 4.19.4): GHSA-mchc-246r-6m76 (account takeover through
  header injection), GHSA-84fg-x5h4-w8xv (Login V2: forgeable email verification proof), GHSA-qj3q-235g-qr6w (one-click account takeover through the device code). Zitadel is the one brick
  routed on the public Gateway. The chart stays 10.4.0 (11.0.0 is beta); `image.tag` and `login.image.tag` are set in the HelmRelease as `tag@digest`, digests re-read from the registry.
  The login ships next 16.3.6: the image scan of the v4.19.5 login (by digest) reports no CRITICAL finding with a fix, where v4.19.2 had three on next.
- **The login's session cookie secret is a generated secret**: `login.sessionCookieSecretName` reads `zitadel-login-session-cookie`, an ExternalSecret over `secret/zitadel/session-cookie`
  (48 random characters, created by `configure.sh`, create only). Before, the chart set nothing and the login derived the key from its service key (deprecated fallback). **Effect on a running
  cluster:** open login sessions end once at the rollout. Until the OpenBao key exists the new login pods wait (`CreateContainerConfigError`) and the old ones keep serving.
- **wait4x 3.6 -> 3.7.2** (the readiness init container of the login pods; digest pinned): clears its 5 CRITICAL findings.
- **`gen-sbom.py` skips `helm.sh/hook: test` resources**: the bats pod of the Zitadel chart is never deployed (Flux runs no `helm test`) and was failing the scan on a perl CVE.
- Also in v4.19.5, handled in `docs/zitadel-hardening.md`: list endpoints enforce query limits (nothing in this repository lists objects) and the device flow has a consent screen (no client of
  the platform uses the device flow).

### Image scan follow-up

The weekly scan (`sbom.yml`) had been red since the 0.3.0 release (3 runs) and nobody was told. Now:

- a failing scan opens or comments on ONE issue per image, titled `[vuln] <image>`, and closes it when the scan passes (`scripts/ops/vuln-issue.sh`, `issues: write` on that job only; a
  `workflow_dispatch` on a branch opens none). The failing criterion is unchanged: CRITICAL with a fix upstream. HIGH findings stay invisible to this scan.
- `.vex/openaether-apps.openvex.json` now records the findings accepted with a reason, each with ONE expiry date: restic 0.19.1 openssh (CVE-2026-60002), the Go stdlib in the `alpine/k8s`
  kustomize (CVE-2025-68121), the `alpine/k8s` certifi (GHSA-6q48-6c7w-9gw2, published 2026-10-20; also in k8s-sidecar, see below) and PyJWT in the CloudNativePG PostgreSQL images (CVE-2026-102268) until 2026-11-20; the
  Istio 1.24.2 grpc and stdlib findings (CVE-2026-33186, CVE-2025-68121) until 2026-12-14, the date of the end-of-support waiver. The Longhorn 1.12.1 libexpat (SUSE-SU-2026:4619-1) is in
  `.github/trivy-ignore.yaml` (Trivy's own format, `expired_at` 2026-11-20) because Trivy 0.69 does not match the product of the Longhorn UI and manager images in a VEX file. The products
  use Trivy's `index.docker.io` repository URL for Docker Hub images. Checked against Trivy 0.69.3 on the real images: the statements suppress exactly these findings.
  `scripts/check-vex.py` (CI step, and a job of the scan workflow that opens `[vuln] VEX statements`) fails on a statement without justification or expiry, or past its date. Trivy does not
  read the dates of a VEX file; the check does.

### What this patch does NOT fix

- **The Login V2 UI is not reachable through the Gateway, in 0.3.0, 0.3.1 and this patch (apps #161).** The HTTPRoute sends only `/` to `zitadel:8080`, so `/ui/v2/login/...` answers 404, and the Gateway egress policy does not allow `zitadel-login:3000`. Found in lab 24; the OIDC code flow through the Session API works, a browser user cannot get past the authorize step. Not fixed here because it changes what the public Gateway serves.
- **Istio 1.24.2 stays**: its grpc finding is waived (above), and Istio's HIGH advisories of 2026-10-16 and 2026-03 (fixed in 1.29.x and later) remain open until the Istio 1.31 upgrade (apps #96).
  The line has been out of support since 2025-06; the waiver in `docs/eol-calendar.yaml` ends 2026-12-14.
- The Longhorn, restic, `alpine/k8s` and CloudNativePG findings above are **waived, not fixed**: no patched image exists yet. The reasons are analyses of reachability, not proofs.
- **certifi in the CloudNativePG PostgreSQL images 18.6 and 16.15 (GHSA-6q48-6c7w-9gw2, CRITICAL, fixed in certifi 2026.10.19, published 2026-10-20) is NOT waived, and stays red.**
  What it does there: certifi 2026.7.22 sits in the images' Python (barman-cloud, which the in-tree `barmanObjectStore` runs for every WAL archive and base backup), and botocore uses
  `certifi.where()` as its default CA bundle for TLS to the S3 endpoint. So the code is on the execution path. The attack needs a leaked emSign intermediate key AND an on-path position
  between the pod and the S3 endpoint; unlikely, not excluded, so no waiver. Registry check (2026-10-22): the plain tags `18.6` and `16.15` were not republished (built 2026-08-31, Debian 11,
  no dated rebuilds exist); only the `-standard-*` / `-minimal-*` / `-system-*` variants are rebuilt (2026-10-19) and they scan clean, **but they do not contain barman-cloud** (checked: no
  `barman-cloud-wal-archive`), so they would break the WAL archiving and backups this release's Clusters rely on. They are not an option while the in-tree `barmanObjectStore` is used; the
  way out is the Barman Cloud plugin (0.4.0 line) or a plain-tag rebuild by CloudNativePG. The scans of these two images fail until then and the issue mechanism reports them.
- **certifi in `quay.io/kiwigrid/k8s-sidecar:2.14.1`** (the Grafana dashboard sidecar) is waived until 2026-11-20 with a reason read in the image: certifi is only consulted by `requests`, which
  there only calls `REQ_URL`, set by the Grafana chart to `http://localhost:3000` (plain HTTP); the Kubernetes API client verifies against the ServiceAccount `ca.crt`. No newer tag exists
  (2.14.1, 2026-10-08, is the latest). The waiver ends if an HTTPS `REQ_URL` or a sidecar URL annotation is configured.

### Fixed

- **Longhorn: a first install that fails once is retried, PARTIAL** (#134, stays open): the HelmRelease sets `install.strategy: RetryOnFailure` (`retryInterval: 2m`, `install.remediation.retries: 0`),
  accepted by helm-controller 1.6.5, with no Longhorn uninstall Job ever created. `test-longhorn-values.py` asserts the strategy, its CRD enum and pattern, and that the upgrade keeps the default.
  Lab 18: a first-install failure undone inside the first retry period recovered by itself in about 2 minutes (the retry runs as an *upgrade* over the failed release). **But** if the cause
  lasts longer than one period, the retry fails as an upgrade (`upgrade.remediation.retries: 0`), the HelmRelease goes `Stalled=True` (`RetriesExceeded`) and stays so until a manual reconcile
  with the `requestedAt` and `forceAt` annotations. A deliberate upgrade failure is not retried (expected). So the change heals a transient shorter than 2 minutes only; see apps #143.
- **Zitadel: a setup killed half way has a tested recovery** (#133): `scripts/ops/zitadel-clear-stuck-migration.sh` lists the steps started and never finished (dry run by default), and with `--apply`
  deletes exactly the `started` event and the `migration_started` row of each, in one transaction. It refuses a step younger than `--min-age` (600 s), a running setup pod and a database that holds
  more than the bootstrap admin. `scripts/dev/test-zitadel-migration-lock.sh` (new CI step) runs it against a real Zitadel v4.19.2 and PostgreSQL.
  Lab 18, for the stuck state of step `05_last_failed`: dry run exit 1, `--apply` cleared it, `flux reconcile helmrelease zitadel --reset --force` brought the release Ready, then the script said
  "nothing to clear"; the kubectl path to the CNPG primary worked; the refusals for a running setup pod and for the minimum age were observed; the human-user refusal was observed only on a
  synthetic database. **Limits:** a plain pod delete never creates the stuck state (Zitadel handles SIGTERM), the state was reproduced with a hard kill. A kill inside `03_default_instance`
  (not atomic) leaves instance data behind and the setup then fails for good with `Errors.Instance.Domain.AlreadyExists`: **not handled**, apps #144. Trap: wait for the Job to be `Failed`
  before deleting it while Helm waits on its hook, or the hook times out and the rollback fails. Nothing clears the migration by itself (0.4.0).
- **The version comments of two vendored dumps are checked** (#106): `check-vendored-lock.sh` fails when `vendored from vX` in the local-path-provisioner or gateway-api kustomization differs from the dump
  (Renovate bumps only the comment). Two mutation cases in `test-check-vendored-lock.sh`. Rung: mocked.
- **`FluxObjectSuspended` excludes the HelmRelease `alloy-audit`**, suspended by design. Lab 18: Alertmanager showed the Watchdog alert only; the vmalert pending state was **not** read (a network policy
  blocks queries to vmselect from outside), so the absence of a pending alert is not proven.

### Documentation

- `docs/patching.md` (first install that stalls) and `docs/traps.md` (Zitadel migration) describe the changes above; the cluster-wide default-deny policy now says in its header that a new namespace's pods
  accept nothing, even from their own namespace (#112, the per-namespace template stays an issue); one French comment in `22-backup.yaml` is English. Rung: mocked.

### Known limitations

- Open and unchanged: #134 (partial, see above), #143 (stall beyond one retry period), #144 (kill inside `03_default_instance`), infra #42 and #375. The other limitations of 0.3.1 are unchanged.
- An ownership conflict does not make a Flux install fail (Flux takes ownership), so it is not a way to test #134.

## [0.3.1] — 2026-10-19

0.3.1 is a **patch release** of 0.3.0: no new scope, the hotfix for the problems that block the goals of 0.4.0 (resilience and monitoring). It pairs
apps 0.3.1 with OpenAether-infra 0.3.1 (whose `git_ref` default and tfvars examples pin `refs/tags/0.3.1` of this repository). Every fix below reached the rung
**mocked** (static checks, unit tests, Kyverno CLI) and was then **observed on a real cloud in lab 17 (2026-10-19)**: Scaleway (3 control planes, 3 workers, fresh install on the
branch) and OVH (reduced profile), both torn down. Not a compliance claim. It does not fix what the issues list as open.

### Fixed

- **OpenBao bootstrap Jobs retry instead of ending Failed on a slow start** (#34): the terminal `activeDeadlineSeconds` is gone from `openbao-init` and
  `openbao-vault-bootstrap` (its clock counted the wait for the StatefulSet); both retry in place eight times, the waits stay bounded by the scripts,
  and `check-bootstrap-jobs.sh` refuses a deadline that does not cover every attempt. A Job that exhausts its retries still comes back about 70 minutes later.
  **Lab 17: PASS** on Scaleway and OVH: both Jobs Complete 1/1 (41 s and 46 s on Scaleway; 69 s and 80 s on OVH), no Failed pod, one recovery Secret, no OpenBao restart.
- **The three OpenBao replicas spread over the workers** (#129): the hostname constraint is `DoNotSchedule` (`maxSkew: 1`, not-Ready workers counted, control
  planes left out), so a replica waits for a second worker instead of joining the first two on it. **Lab 17: verified for placement** on Scaleway and OVH (one replica per worker,
  three volumes pinned to three different nodes, PodDisruptionBudget `minAvailable: 2`); a drain of a worker holding a replica was **not blocked** (55 s). A real node-replacing
  roll was **not run**. A cluster whose pods already share a worker is not moved by this (the volumes are pinned): `foundation/vault/README.md` has the one-replica-at-a-time
  procedure. Not covered: a node that has not registered at all when the pods are placed; a one-control-plane-only cluster (the control planes are excluded, the pods stay Pending);
  a second concurrent drain waits by design.
- **`CnpgWalArchiveFailing` tells "failing now" from "failed once"** (#138): it needs a failure counted in the last 10 minutes with no WAL archived in the same 10
  minutes, for 15 minutes, so the isolated failures around a drained reboot or a switchover never fire. A vmalert unit test replays continuous failure, two isolated failures
  with archiving resuming, and two isolated failures on an idle database. **Lab 17: verified.** The metric `cnpg_pg_stat_archiver_archived_count` is present; across two drained reboots (one with
  the primary of `zitadel-db`, with a real one-failure transient after the failover) the alert was never pending; with the archive credentials of one cluster broken it went pending after the last good archive aged
  out and fired 29 minutes after the break (24 minutes after the first failed archive), reaching Alertmanager as `critical`.
- **Flux errors reach Alertmanager** (#139): a `Provider` of type `alertmanager` (the in-cluster VMAlertmanager, no URL to seed) and an `Alert` for `error` events, with the `flux-system`
  egress and the Alertmanager ingress on :9093 opened and the Slack text reading Flux's `message`. **Lab 17: verified** on Scaleway: a broken Kustomization reached Alertmanager within 60 s with the real labels
  (`alertname=FluxKustomizationArtifactfailed` i.e. `Flux<Kind><Reason>`, `kind`, `name`, `namespace`, `reason`, `reportingcontroller`, `revision`, `severity=error`) and a non-empty `message`;
  `severity=error` is routed by the root route to the default `slack` receiver (the sub-routes are only for `critical` and Watchdog). Flux sends no resolve, so each alert expires after about 6 minutes.
  **Covered** event sources: every GitRepository and every Kustomization of `flux-system` only (a Kustomization in another namespace is not covered), and the HelmReleases of the listed namespaces
  (`capi-operator-system`, `cert-manager`, `foundation-service-mesh`, `foundation-storage`, `kube-system`, `longhorn-system`, `services-identity`, `services-observability`,
  `services-observability-audit`, `services-observability-node`, `capi-clusters`). Not covered: a HelmRelease created in a new namespace must be added to the Alert (a test says so).
  The rejected-signature case was **not exercised** (git verification was off).
- **`FluxObjectSuspended` no longer fires for the HelmRelease `alloy-audit`, suspended by design** (found in lab 17): the audit collector is opt-in, so every default
  install keeps `alloy-audit` suspended and the alert (`for: 24h`) would have fired after a day. The rule now excludes that one HelmRelease; any other suspended object still alerts. A vmalert unit test
  (the by-design object does not alert, another suspended HelmRelease does) fails without the change. Suspending `alloy-audit` after enabling the collector is not reported by this alert.

### Known limitations

- `cluster-verify` counts 15 checks on a default install (lab 16 of 0.3.0 counted 16): lab 17 reported 15 passed, 0 failed; the missing check was not investigated.
- The rejected-signature case of #139 was not exercised.
- #129: a real node-replacing roll was not run; a plain drain is not blocked, a second concurrent drain waits by design.
- `FluxReconciliationStalled` (`critical`) was seen firing 6 to 7 minutes after hard-reboot drills on OVH with every object Ready again; its window and `for` are unchanged in 0.3.1 (not clearly safe to loosen without evidence from a cluster), to be looked at in 0.4.0.
- Unchanged and still open: #134, #133, #124 and infra #42.

---

## [0.3.0] — 2026-10-19

0.3.0 is the pair of OpenAether-infra 0.3.0: a **version upgrade campaign** (every component that was past support, plus the independent low-risk bumps) and a
**security hardening wave**, the latter opt-in or in Audit unless stated. The project follows ANSSI recommendations as far as possible.
**Nothing here is a claim of compliance with any ANSSI document**: each entry names the requirements it addresses and the rung it reached.

**A fresh install lands on the new versions (run on Scaleway, OVH and Outscale, labs 12 to 15). A running cluster must hop one minor at a time where the
table says so** (`docs/patching.md`; the in-place hops were rehearsed on Scaleway only, in lab 15c, and not all of them).

### Version upgrades

| Component | 0.2.0 | 0.3.0 | Running cluster |
|---|---|---|---|
| Longhorn | 1.9.2 | 1.12.1 | hop 1.10.2 -> 1.11.3 -> 1.12.1; **pre-step on the five CRDs with a conversion webhook before 1.10** (lab 15c: the 1.10 hop failed without it, 14 min without attach); no downgrade exists; the HelmRelease no longer remediates |
| cert-manager | 1.15.3 | 1.21.2 | one minor at a time, six Flux hops, 221-255 s each, no probe failure |
| Kyverno | 1.12.1 | 1.19.1 | 1.13 -> 1.15 -> 1.17 -> 1.19 ran in 57-123 s each; **1.19 is the last line serving `kyverno.io/v1`; migrate before 1.20** (#113) |
| CloudNativePG operator | 1.23.1 | 1.30.1 | every hop rolls the Clusters; with the new `primaryUpdateMethod: switchover` the write gap is about 10 s instead of 195 s; take the 0.3.0 `cnpg` Kustomization no later than the 1.27 hop (CRD substitute opt-out) |
| PostgreSQL (Grafana DB image) | 16.3 | 16.15 | a minor roll of that Cluster |
| External Secrets Operator | 1.2.0 | 2.13.0 | 1.3.2 then 2.13.0 ran without failure |
| Loki chart (Loki) | 6.25.0 (3.3.2) | 18.16.2 (3.7.8), chart now from `grafana-community` | procedure below; the 6.25 -> 6.55 hop timed out in lab 15c |
| Grafana chart (Grafana) | 8.6.4 | 14.0.1 (13.2.4), from `grafana-community` | **not rehearsed in place**; take a CNPG backup first |
| Alloy chart (Alloy) | 0.11.0 (1.6.1) | 1.13.1 (1.20.1) | **not rehearsed in place** |
| Zitadel chart (Zitadel) | 10.0.2 | 10.4.0 (4.19.2) | 4.14 -> 4.15 -> 4.17 -> 4.19.2 ran in 219-250 s each; migrations are forward-only: CNPG backup first |
| victoria-metrics-operator | 0.65.1 | 0.68.1 | one rolling restart of the VM pods |
| Flux on the children | 2.8.8 | 2.9.6 | not rehearsed |
| cluster-api-operator / ORC | 0.27.0 / 2.4.0 | 0.29.0 / 2.6.0 | the ORC version the pinned CAPO needs |
| Cilium on the children | 1.19.2 | 1.19.9 | a patch; one minor behind the management cluster on purpose |
| Gateway API CRDs | 1.1.0 | 1.2.1 | **not 1.5.x** (Istio 1.24 does not become Ready with them); CRDs are not rolled back; check `storedVersions` first |
| restic | 0.18.0 | 0.19.1 | exit code 3 now fails the Job; `prune` repacks more |
| local-path-provisioner | 0.0.31 | 0.0.37 | restarts once; PVs untouched |
| node-exporter chart / kube-state-metrics chart / metrics-server chart | 4.39.0 / 5.25.1 / 3.12.2 | 4.60.0 / 8.6.0 / 3.14.0 | each restarts once |
| Kubernetes / Talos (infra) | 1.37.1 / 1.14.2 | unchanged | |
| Istio, OpenBao, MinIO | 1.24.2, 2.7.1, RELEASE.2024-04-18 | unchanged | Istio and MinIO waived to 2026-12-31 (`docs/eol-calendar.md`) |

### Upgrade notes by component (from 0.2.0)

- **Longhorn.** Follow `docs/patching.md` ("Longhorn in-place hops") in order: the CRD conversion pre-step, then 1.10.2, 1.11.3, 1.12.1, engines upgrade automatically afterwards.
  The chart NetworkPolicies are off and the brick's CiliumNetworkPolicy opens the new ports (9503/9600, 10000-30000, NFS 2049). The RWX StorageClasses
  now carry `softerr,timeo=100,retrans=3` (a graceful reboot of a node with a share-manager and a client no longer hangs for more than 12 minutes, apps #124);
  a volume created earlier keeps its old mount options until it is recreated.
- **Loki chart 6.x -> 18.** A fresh install takes 18 directly. In place: the StatefulSet selector and volume claim template are unchanged. The HelmRelease has a 15 minute
  timeout and **no automatic upgrade rollback**: on a stalled hop follow "Loki chart hop" in `docs/patching.md` (inspect the pod that is not Ready, delete all Loki pods at
  once if only the memberlist ring waits, `flux reconcile helmrelease loki --reset --force`). Logs are not lost: Alloy retries and the chunks are in the object store.
- **Kyverno.** The five Pod policies get a per-namespace Enforce through the **rule-level `failureAction` / `failureActionOverrides` spelling**; the old spec-level
  `validationFailureActionOverrides` is ignored on 1.19.1 and denies nothing (lab 15a, apps 669494b). Enforce stays off by default.
- **CloudNativePG.** Both Clusters use `primaryUpdateMethod: switchover`. `barmanObjectStore` stays until the Barman Cloud plugin replaces it (removed in CNPG 1.31, #114).
- **Disk.** The infra default root volume is 50 GB; a 20 GB node exhausts EPHEMERAL after the larger images of 0.3.0 (DiskPressure, evictions). Grow the disks before the hops (infra CHANGELOG).
- **OpenBao** metrics move to a second listener on 8202 (headless Service only); the listeners accept TLS 1.3 only.
- **Grafana and Zitadel** database migrations are forward-only. Take a CNPG Backup first.

### BREAKING and behaviour changes

- `Longhorn` HelmRelease: `retries: 0`, no remediation (a person fixes forward). `Loki` likewise.
- Kyverno is on 1.19.1: the cleanup CronJobs no longer exist; `PolicyException` stays off; images come from `reg.kyverno.io`.
- node-exporter runs in its own privileged namespace `services-observability-node`; four namespaces can drop to the baseline Pod Security level (opt-in).
- Zitadel no longer installs with its public admin password: a generated 32-character password is used.
- The kubelet and apiserver scrapes verify TLS (the kubelet side is an opt-in component that needs infra's `kubelet_serving_cert_rotation`).
- Kyverno reports sensitive capabilities, init containers and read-only rootfs in Audit (new violations to review, none blocking).
- The Gateway API CRDs v1alpha2 `GRPCRoute`/`ReferenceGrant` versions disappear.

### Known limitations

- **Unsigned history:** no commit on the history verifies yet, so the Flux Git verification component cannot be turned on (#86; infra #332, #333). The maintainer creates a dedicated signing key later.
- **CNPG `barmanObjectStore` is removed in CNPG 1.31** (#114) and **Kyverno `kyverno.io/v1` ClusterPolicy is removed in Kyverno 1.20** (#113): stay on 1.30.x and 1.19.x until migrated.
- **In-place hops not rehearsed:** Loki chart 6.25 -> 18 (the lab hop stopped at 6.55 and timed out), Grafana 8.6 -> 14, Alloy, Gateway API CRDs, Flux on the children,
  Cilium and Talos patches, Istio (#121). A fresh install of these versions was run on three clouds.
- **Scaleway first install with the app load balancer allow-list needs two applies** (infra #361).
- **OVH S3 has no bucket policies through the S3 API** (infra #379); **Outscale did not pass the stability criteria with the full stack in lab 16** (3 CP + 3 W of 3 vCPU / 6 GB: an IO and memory
  cascade after one backup and restore and one drained reboot, not cured by a live `io1 3000`; the 20 vCPU quota allows no larger shape, infra #376) and OOS refused dedicated EIM keys (infra #377).
- **Losing a worker.** The shipped 2-worker examples could not be rolled (172% of CPU requested, infra #378; the infra examples now ship 3, not re-run); only the CPU gate is checked. Replicas with a required
  spread stay short while a worker is out. `CnpgWalArchiveFailing` can stay pending about 25 minutes after a drained reboot (#138), and Flux has no notification Alert or Provider (#139): both 0.4.0.
- **MinIO (dev profile) is archived upstream;** the waiver runs to 2026-12-31 (#104). Istio 1.24 is waived to the same date.
- **Kubernetes 1.37 is outside the range cert-manager 1.21, CNPG 1.30, Flux 2.9 and others test upstream** (#105, #120); the labs ran on it.
- **Loki retention deletion of expired chunks was never exercised** (#127); Alloy keeps log batches in memory only (#80, #119); **Zitadel -> Grafana login with MFA** was not run (#126).
- OpenBao replicas and volumes can land on one worker (#129); the Zitadel setup Job killed half way leaves a migration lock (#133); the Longhorn install has no retries (#134).
- The alert thresholds are estimates; most rules have run against synthetic series only.

### Validated on

The same labs as the infra CHANGELOG (11 to 15c, 2026-10-16 to 2026-10-18; Scaleway full DAG in 12, 13, 14 and 15c, OVH complete in 15a, Outscale full in 15b),
each torn down and checked clean. At the end of a fresh install on the defaults: 38 Kustomizations and 16 HelmReleases Ready, `cluster-verify` 16/16, `cluster-security` 33/36 to 34/36
(the rest skipped, not failed), strict plan "No changes". Lab 15c ran 40 upgrade hops and drills from apps 0.2.0 on Scaleway: 20896 HTTP samples through the load balancer,
135 failed, one 73 s episode (a drain evicting the single Gateway pod); CNPG primary kill 24 s of write gap, planned switchover 12 s; Loki outage of 240 s lost no line.
Offline: Kyverno CLI 1.19.1 (90 + 8 fixtures), Alloy 1.20.1, Zitadel 4.19.2 with PostgreSQL, Loki `-verify-config`, Grafana 13.2.4, CRD schema validation, vmalert dry runs.

**Lab 16 (2026-10-18, final validation on the head of this branch with a signed throwaway Flux branch, each cloud torn down and checked clean):** Scaleway and OVH, a fresh install from the shipped
examples: 38 Kustomizations and 16 HelmReleases Ready 23.5 min (Scaleway) and 29 min (OVH) after the start, idle alerts Watchdog only (61 rules healthy), Longhorn RWO, LUKS2 and RWX backups restored with matching
checksums, CNPG backup and restore into a new cluster (150 tables = origin), the five Kyverno Enforce policies and the ephemeral-container denials, OpenBao TLS 1.3 only with re-key, ESO 15/15, Zitadel setup, Loki
write and read-back, a drained worker reboot and a control-plane reboot. Outscale (3 CP + 3 W of 3 vCPU / 6 GB): everything passed for the first hour, then the cascade above; its control-plane reboot, alert drill and
final acceptance were not run. A provoked alert reached Alertmanager (the webhook-receiver leg was skipped on purpose). Found and filed: #137 (fixed here), #138, #139.

**NOT run:** the etcd snapshot restore; OpenBao raft snapshot restore; Istio hops; Alloy, Grafana and Gateway API CRD in-place hops; Flux (children) and Cilium/Talos patch hops;
Zitadel -> Grafana interactive login; the alert drills (no alert was provoked); Loki retention expiry; the Flux Git verification (no signing key); an unattended run to completion.

---

### Detail of the 0.3.0 entries

ANSSI hardening work, released with 0.3.0. **Nothing here is a claim of compliance with any ANSSI document**: each entry names the requirements it addresses and the rung it reached; the ones that only a cluster can show are listed in the lab checklist of the maintainers.

### Security

- **Review fixes (mirrors the infra review). Static / mocked, not run on a cluster.**
  - `check-commit-signatures.sh` skips merge commits: on a PR run HEAD is GitHub's unsigned synthetic merge commit, so the documented `--strict`
    switch-on would have failed every PR. Its test now has a merge-commit case and expired-key/signature cases (status X/Y, throwaway key signed in the past).
  - `docs/signed-commits.md` and the `git-verify` component comment no longer say Flux checks OpenPGP only: the Flux CRD vendored in infra documents
    SSH keys (`.sshpub`) too. OpenPGP is this repository's choice, and SSH keys are NOT wired.

- **Critic follow-ups: apps areas the audit had not read** (no ANSSI requirement id was assigned to them by the audit; they sit near DP-02, DP-03/F06, LOG-13 and SSDLC-CICD-AIP-01; mocked/static, not run on a cluster):
  - CAPI provider egress: `capi-system` no longer allows the Outscale endpoints and `capi-outscale-system` no longer allows Scaleway and OVH
    (each namespace runs the providers of its own cloud). Rollback: restore the removed `toFQDNs` lines.
  - `cert-manager-vault-token` Role: `serviceaccounts/token` `create` is limited to the `cert-manager` ServiceAccount (`resourceNames`), not every
    ServiceAccount of the namespace.
  - `openbao-snapshot` CronJob: the Job revokes its own token right after the snapshot (it read a copy of every secret); a failed revoke only warns.
  - Alloy (logs): `discovery.kubernetes` is limited to the pods of the node (`NODE_NAME` from the downward API). The DaemonSet had every instance
    tail every pod through the API server. The config parses with Alloy 1.6.1 (`alloy fmt`); that logs still arrive from every node is a lab check.
  - `apps/vendored-dumps.sha256` + `scripts/dev/check-vendored-lock.sh` (CI): the six vendored upstream dumps are hash-locked, so a change to one
    moves the lock in the same commit.
  - Does NOT: prove a dump equals its upstream release, narrow the ClusterRoles that the vendored External Secrets dump grants, split the
    `openbao-snapshot` pod so the restic container has no ServiceAccount token, or restrict who may use the `openbao-ca` ClusterIssuer.

- **Flux Git source verification, opt-in** (ANSSI SSDLC-CICD-AIP-03, SSDLC-CONT-REC06; apps half of F04, F10; mocked/static, not run on a cluster):
  - New component `apps/flux/components/git-verify`: adds `spec.verify {mode: HEAD, secretRef: git-signers}` to the `GitRepository`, only when a cluster's overlay lists it. Default unchanged (`scripts/dev/test-git-verify.py`, in CI).
  - `scripts/dev/check-commit-signatures.sh` in the `commits` job: a WARNING for unsigned commits of a PR (the history has none signed), `--strict` to fail; own test with a throwaway key. `CODEOWNERS` names the Flux, RBAC and workflow paths. `docs/signed-commits.md`: GPG as the standard, signer table (to fill), enable order, break-glass.
  - Does NOT: change any GitHub ruleset, publish a key, create the `git-signers` Secret or the bootstrap `verify` (OpenAether-infra, `flux_git_verify`), verify tags, or prove source-controller accepts it on a cluster.

- **Named operator identities** (ANSSI PA022 R38, R40, NSA12 AUTH-02; apps half of F04, F13, F20):
  - New brick `platform/rbac` (Flux `platform-rbac`, in the baseline of every generated profile): `ClusterRoleBinding`
    `oa-admins` (group `oa:admins` to `cluster-admin`) and `oa-readers` (group `oa:readers` to `view`). Inert until a credential
    carries one of these groups; nothing existing changes. Rollback: remove the Kustomization.
  - **Not done here**: the CSR-based kubeconfig tool, the RBAC review script, the break-glass rules and the apiserver OIDC
    configuration belong to `OpenAether-infra`; OIDC groups, the OpenBao OIDC/JWT auth method and the Zitadel MFA login policy
    need an issuer reachable outside the cluster and are **not** attempted. A client certificate stays non-revocable: the control is
    its short lifetime, a group binding cannot single out one holder.
  - **Rung: static** (`scripts/dev/test-rbac-brick.py` in CI), mocked/static, not run on a cluster.
- **In-cluster transport and metadata egress** (ANSSI HYG-M21, PG078-R11, NT-35, NSA12 NET-07, NET-09, HYG-M22; apps half of
  F14, F12, DP-11, F20, F05):
  - Zitadel (User and Admin) and Grafana connect to CNPG with `sslmode=require`: the credentials stop crossing the pod network
    in clear. It encrypts but does **not authenticate the server**; `verify-full` needs the CNPG CA copied into the consuming
    namespaces with a rotation-safe mechanism and is **not done**. Rollback: `disable`.
  - No TLS version floor is set in istiod's `meshConfig`: `tlsDefaults.minProtocolVersion` (added then removed in this release
    cycle) is ignored by Istio, which reads only `ecdhCurves` and `cipherSuites` there and leaves ISTIO_MUTUAL to `meshMTLS`.
    Envoy's own TLS 1.2 floor applies, unchanged. A TLS 1.3 floor and cipher lists are **not** set (client versions not
    measured); the Gateway floor would be a per-listener `tls.options`, not done.
  - New CCNP `deny-cloud-metadata-egress`: every pod is denied 169.254.169.254 and 169.254.42.42 (deny-only, no default-deny
    side effect, Talos hostDNS untouched).
  - The cert-manager egress rule no longer names the Let's Encrypt hosts: no issuer in the repo is ACME. Anyone with an ACME
    ClusterIssuer must add its directory FQDN back.
  - **Not done / already done**: Loki `insecure` was fixed in 0.2.0; the kubelet and metrics-server CA path is the opt-in component of
    B23; Zitadel's own TLS (gateway to Zitadel stays h2c behind WireGuard and the CNPs), the gateway key algorithm, node-level egress
    (infra) are not touched.
  - **Rung: static** (`scripts/dev/test-in-cluster-transport.py` in CI), mocked/static, not run on a cluster.
- **Container hardening and resource bounds** (ANSSI FT082 R10, R11, R12, NSA12 POD-06, BP028 R64, PA012 R10, R14; apps half of
  K8S/F09, K8S/F10, LOG-13, LOG-14):
  - Alloy and its config-reloader, Grafana and the CAPI operator run with a **read-only root filesystem**; what each writes
    goes to an emptyDir (Alloy's storage path, Grafana's and the operator's `/tmp`). The CAPI operator also gets a pod and a
    container `securityContext` (uid 65532, drop ALL, seccomp RuntimeDefault). Alloy's `loki.write` retries 30 times with
    at most 1 minute between tries instead of giving up after about 5 minutes (Alloy v1.6 has no write-ahead log).
  - **local-path-provisioner**: the provisioner has a restricted `securityContext`, its helper image is `busybox:1.37.0`
    instead of the bare `busybox`. It does NOT change `mkdir -m 0777` to 0770: the volumes are used by non-root pods with
    other uids and the hostPath volumes ignore `fsGroup`, so 0770 would stop them from writing; that needs a different
    storage design (Longhorn, or a Talos user volume), not a mode.
  - **ServiceAccount token dropped** from the Zitadel server and login pods and the MinIO pod (Helm post-renderer: neither
    chart has a value for it). Grafana keeps it (its dashboards sidecar reads ConfigMaps through the API); the Zitadel setup
    Job keeps it (it runs kubectl).
  - **Bounds**: `sizeLimit` on the OpenBao `tmp` and openbao-init emptyDirs (64Mi); memory and CPU limits on the three ESO
    Deployments, CPU limits on the four Kyverno controllers (their memory limits were already in the vendored file).
    **The ESO and Kyverno values are generous guesses, not measured** (no cluster here); the lab step tightens them.
  - **Rung: static** (`scripts/dev/test-container-bounds.py` in CI, `helm template` of the Alloy, Grafana, CAPI operator,
    Zitadel and MinIO charts at the pinned versions, `kubectl kustomize`, and the new `loki.write` block loaded by a real
    Alloy v1.6.1); **not run on a cluster**. Whether each workload really starts and works with a read-only root filesystem is
    unproven until then. It does NOT add LimitRange or ResourceQuota (deferred), `ephemeral-storage` requests, or the Talos
    `podPidsLimit` (infra), and does not bump any chart (Renovate PRs); Alloy to Loki stays cleartext HTTP, covered by
    WireGuard and the CiliumNetworkPolicy, not by TLS.

- **Four namespaces can drop from the privileged to the baseline Pod Security level, opt-in** (ANSSI FT082 R4, R6, NSA12 POD-05;
  apps half of K8S/F08, OSN/F16, SCC/F09): `services-observability` (node-exporter has left it), `services-gateway` (the Envoy proxy
  istiod generates), `foundation-service-mesh` (HelmReleases and the waypoint Gateway; ztunnel and istiod run in `istio-system`) and
  `foundation-storage` (MinIO). **Off by default**: `enforce` stays `privileged` as released, `warn`/`audit` are restricted (they only
  log); the new component `apps/flux/components/psa-baseline` (tested by `scripts/dev/test-psa-restricted.py`) lowers them. It is the
  one change of the series that can stop a pod at admission, and the required server-side dry run (`LAB-CHECKLIST`) was NOT run:
  do that dry run first. Baseline is only checked on pods created or rescheduled after it. To go back, remove the component.
  **Upgrade note**: a cluster owner who wants it adds `components: [../components/psa-baseline]` to the cluster overlay; it patches
  `spec.patches` of three Flux Kustomizations (`namespaces`, `istio`, `storage`), which replaces a list, so merge by hand if the
  overlay already patches them or also uses `psa-restricted`. It does NOT lower `istio-system`, `longhorn-system`,
  `kube-system` or the new node-exporter namespace, nor raise `foundation-networking` (Cilium hostPort), `zitadel`, `kyverno`,
  `cnpg-system`, `orc-system` or `flux-system` (their warn logs have to be read first).

- **node-exporter moves to its own privileged namespace, `services-observability-node`** (ANSSI FT082 R4, R6, NSA12 POD-05;
  apps half of K8S/F08, OSN/F16, SCC/F09). It is the only observability workload that needs hostPath, hostNetwork and hostPID,
  and Pod Security has no per-pod level, so the whole `services-observability` namespace was privileged for it. The HelmRelease,
  the `node-exporter` VMServiceScrape and the Kyverno exemptions (`restrict-privileged` by pod name, `disallow-root-user` by
  namespace as before) follow; `services-observability` itself is NOT lowered here (previous entry, opt-in). The CiliumNetworkPolicies and Istio
  policies need no change: node-exporter is hostNetwork, out of the mesh, and reached through vmagent's host egress on :9100.
  `restrict-privileged` also stops sparing `foundation-service-mesh` wholesale (ztunnel and the Istio CNI agent by name).
  **Rung: static (`scripts/dev/test-node-exporter-namespace.py` in CI, render, Kyverno CLI fixtures); not run on a cluster.**
  **Upgrade note**: Flux uninstalls the old release and installs the new one, so node_* series stop for one or two scrape
  intervals and the `node` DaemonSet briefly has both pods wanting :9100; series gain `namespace="services-observability-node"`
  (no shipped rule or dashboard selects on it). It does NOT touch the Talos or Cilium host policies.

- **Kyverno: sensitive capabilities, init containers and read-only rootfs are reported, all in Audit** (ANSSI FT082 R1, R8, R15
  in part, NSA12 POD-01, BP028 R64; apps half of K8S/F04). New `restrict-capabilities` (names `capabilities.add` values such as
  SYS_ADMIN, NET_ADMIN, NET_RAW, SYS_PTRACE, SYS_MODULE on containers, initContainers and, through `Pod/ephemeralcontainers`,
  ephemeral containers; NET_BIND_SERVICE is left alone; ztunnel and the Istio CNI agent are exempt by pod name, the
  single-purpose system namespaces by namespace), plus the informational `require-ro-rootfs` and `require-limits` (memory only).
  `restrict-privileged` now also reads initContainers; `require-security-context` is split into three rules so that
  runAsNonRoot and seccomp are satisfied at the pod OR the container level (the two fields exist at both), while
  allowPrivilegeEscalation and `drop: [ALL]` stay container-level, initContainers included. The alert `KyvernoPolicyViolation`
  skips the two informational policies. The header of `apps/base/kyverno/kustomization.yaml` named v1.18.2; the vendored file is
  v1.12.1 (same sha256), so the `validationFailureAction` syntax is the right one. **Rung: Kyverno CLI 1.12.1 on fixture pods
  (`scripts/dev/test-kyverno-policies.sh`, 80 expectations, in CI), static render; not run on a cluster**: the report counts on a
  fresh install and after a node reboot, the ephemeral-container match and `names` against real pod names are for the lab. It does
  NOT flip any policy to Enforce, narrow the exclusions of `disallow-root-user` (the one Enforce policy that spares whole
  namespaces), cover the Gateway-generated pods' needs, or add `verifyImages` (the registry allow-list was already per organisation).

- **OpenBao PKI: ECDSA keys, a server-only role, revocation information and auto-tidy** (ANSSI IGC-BP-03, NT-35 R8, R27-R29, R33,
  IGC-BP-06; TLS-07, TLS-08, TLS-09, DP-08, apps half). `configure.sh` generates an ECDSA P-384 intermediate CSR and writes the
  `openaether` role as ECDSA P-256, serverAuth only, `DigitalSignature` only, no wildcard, bare domain, IP SAN or required CN; it
  also sets `pki/config/urls` (AIA, CRL, OCSP on the in-cluster service), `pki/config/crl` (automatic rebuild, delta, 72 h) and
  `pki/config/auto-tidy`. The gateway Certificate becomes ECDSA P-256 with `rotationPolicy: Always`, the four explicit hostnames
  (no wildcard, no commonName) and `renewBefore` of one third. `OA_PKI_KEY_TYPE=rsa` is the documented RSA-3072 + PSS variant;
  `OA_PKI_DEFAULT_FOLLOWS_LATEST` (off) lets a renewed intermediate become the default issuer, which a plain import does not.
  Rung: mocked/static plus the real-OpenBao harness (`scripts/dev/test-openbao-bootstrap.sh`: EC leaf, RSA/wildcard/bare/IP/outside
  names refused, URLs in the leaf, revoked serial on the CRL, idempotent re-run, both key types, a throwaway EC root signing the
  intermediate); not run on a cluster. It does NOT: convert an existing RSA-2048 intermediate (regenerate it, runbook section 5), make
  any client check revocation (none does; Envoy does not hard-fail), replace the Istio mesh root (deferred to the Istio upgrade),
  replace the `openbao-ca-bundle` leaf Certificate (needs trust-manager), or alert on CRL age (OpenBao exposes no such metric, and
  its 1 m metric retention would drop the tidy ones). **Upgrade note**: a new HTTPRoute hostname must be added to
  `certificate-openaether-tls.yaml`; the gateway key is re-issued as ECDSA at the first sync.
- **OpenBao's listeners accept TLS 1.3 only, and its server certificate re-keys at every renewal and is reloaded without a restart**
  (ANSSI NT-35 R10, R11 and R25, IGC BP-08; TLS-02, TLS-10). `tls_min_version = tls13` and `tls_key_exchange_preferences`
  (`X25519MLKEM768, CurveP384, CurveP256, X25519`, a key that OpenBao 2.7.1 does honour: P-521 is refused) on the API listener;
  `OA_TLS_MIN_VERSION=tls12` on the container is the documented way back and an unknown value stops the entrypoint.
  `openbao-server-tls` gets `rotationPolicy: Always` and `renewBefore: 2920h` (a third of its life, was 720h). OpenBao reads the pair at
  start and on SIGHUP only, so a `cert-reload` sidecar (same image, pod `shareProcessNamespace`) hashes the two files every 30 s and
  sends SIGHUP. **Real OpenBao in Docker (`scripts/dev/test-openbao-bootstrap.sh`): TLS 1.2 refused, 1.3 accepted, a new pair served on
  both listeners with the server staying unsealed and the audit device still writing.** Not run on a cluster: that cert-manager,
  ESO, the `bao` CLI in the Jobs, kubelet's probes and the Gateway's Envoy all speak 1.3, and a real renewal reaching a running pod
  through the Secret volume. The StatefulSet pod template changes, so the three pods roll once. It does NOT re-key `openbao-ca`
  (10 years; re-keying it means re-issuing every leaf) nor the Gateway certificate (a later batch).

- **Every route on the shared Gateway is registered with what authenticates its callers, and an opt-in component takes the OpenBao
  and Grafana routes off it** (ANSSI BP-028 R51 and R79 in part; the Zitadel first-admin half is the
  Zitadel entry of this section). The app load balancer has no source ACL, so each HTTPRoute on `openaether-gateway` answers anyone who sets its Host
  header. `docs/public-exposure.yaml` lists the five routes and their guard; `scripts/check-gateway-routes.py` (fixture-tested,
  in CI) fails on an unregistered route, a missing authentication, a route served by default that states none, or flags that
  disagree with the manifests. `apps/flux/components/private-admin-routes` (default off, a cluster adds it to its own overlay)
  deletes the `openbao` and `grafana` routes through Flux `spec.patches`; `scripts/dev/test-private-admin-routes.py` renders it
  and applies those patches to the real bricks (exactly two routes go). **Mocked/static: Flux's merge of `spec.patches` into a
  path, and the admin paths after the routes are gone (OpenBao through `bao_admin`, Grafana through a port-forward without its
  Zitadel login), were not run on a cluster.** It does NOT filter the app load balancer (an `app_lb_allowed_cidrs` ACL is the
  infra half), create a second Gateway for public applications (reviewed as high risk: every policy names `openaether-gateway`),
  or change what a deployed cluster serves unless its overlay opts in.
- **The OpenBao seal-key escrow is proven by a hash, needs two custodians, and a missing or stale attestation raises an alert**
  (ANSSI F-BKP-05 / GH-M37-5, DP-02 / B2 recovery rule, PA-022 R45 in part). Day 0 now escrows the key with at least two
  recipients or as a k-of-n split, and ends with a step that reconstructs the key from the escrow, compares its sha256 with the live
  Secret's and only then writes the ConfigMap `openbao-escrow-ack` (date, hash, number of holders). A new CronJob
  `openbao-escrow-check` (foundation-vault, daily, `get` on three ConfigMaps and never on the Secret) fails on a production
  cluster that has no attestation a week after initialisation, a malformed one, fewer than two holders, or one older than 400
  days; the VMRule `OpenBaoEscrowNotAttested` (warning) fires when it has not succeeded for 36 h. A development cluster passes.
  `scripts/dev/test-escrow-check.sh` (36 assertions, run on the host and under the CronJob's image, wired into CI) caught a real
  flaw in a first draft: an unreachable API read as a development cluster and passed. **Mocked/static: the CronJob, the alert and
  the ConfigMap flow were not run on a cluster, `kube_cronjob_status_last_successful_time` was not checked against the deployed
  kube-state-metrics, and the `ssss`/`gfsplit` split was not tried.** It does NOT detect an escrow of an old key by itself
  (the job never reads the key: the rotation runbook now deletes the ConfigMap and the `escrowed-at` annotation that
  OpenAether-infra's check 4.7 reads, and the Day 0 step sets that annotation), run the restore drill, or move the key out of the cluster (the
  `transit` seal stays a recorded deviation). The `get` on `openbao-seal-key` in the init Role stays: `init.sh` needs it on every
  re-run to refuse inventing a second key. The infra half (an `escrow` task, the cluster-verify check, the runbook twins) is not in
  this repository.
- **A key inventory, the replacement procedure of every application key, the root-CA custody options and the backup policy are written
  down** (ANSSI B2 impact/crypto-period/erasure rules, TLS-12, TLS-20, DP-06, DP-07, F-BKP-07, F-BKP-16). `docs/key-inventory.md`
  lists each key the applications repository creates (purpose, location, algorithm, period, impact of loss and of leak, replacement,
  archive) and the recorded deviations (software seal key and AES-GCM wrap, listener CA, Istio CA, root custody); the offline-root
  runbook is now English, offers a token/HSM or offline media (a password manager stays as a recorded deviation), signs with
  SHA-384, and covers compromise and erasure (`shred` is dropped as unreliable on SSDs); the backup README gains a per-dataset
  policy table (schedule, RPO, retention, destination, key holder, restore test, destruction), the order of a full recovery
  (OpenBao before the Zitadel database, because the masterkey lives in OpenBao) and is translated; `SECURITY.md` points to the
  incident procedure. The Longhorn comment that called a passphrase change harmless now says not to rotate while encrypted volumes
  exist. **Docs only: the OpenSSL commands of the runbook were run on a scratch directory (ECDSA P-384 root, SHA-384 signature of a
  CSR, verification); nothing else was run, and no replacement procedure was run on a cluster.** The token/HSM commands, the
  root-compromise path and the transit-seal migration are untried. It does NOT change any manifest, add a key period the code
  enforces, or claim compliance. Grafana's `secret_key` is unset (upstream's public default): listed as a gap, not changed.
  The links to `OpenAether-infra/docs/{key-management,incident-response,disaster-recovery}.md` point at documents the infra half of
  this work writes: they do not exist on the infra branch yet.
- **Zitadel no longer installs with its public admin password, and its authentication policy is written down.** A new instance used to
  get `zitadel-admin` / `Password1!` (upstream's default, reproduced: it logs in as IAM_OWNER, and Grafana's admin role hangs off that
  account) and Zitadel's open defaults: 8-character passwords, no lockout, no MFA, self-registration open, 12 h tokens. Now `configure.sh`
  creates `secret/zitadel/admin` (create only; 28 random characters and `Aa1!` so Zitadel's policy accepts it), External Secrets merges
  it into `zitadel-secret-config`, and `DefaultInstance` sets 15 characters, lockout at 5 (password and OTP), MFA forced, no
  self-registration, no external IdP, 12 h check lifetimes and 1 h tokens. Password hashing moves to argon2id (ANSSI PA-079 R29 asks
  for a memory-hard function; the audit's PBKDF2 reading was R29-, the fallback) with the `bcrypt` verifier, applied to running
  instances at the next rollout. Logs are JSON and the HTTP access log goes to stdout. **What an existing cluster needs, said
  plainly:** `FirstInstance` and `DefaultInstance` are read once, at instance creation, so on a running Zitadel they change nothing:
  `zitadel-admin` may still have `Password1!` (`docs/zitadel-hardening.md` has the check), and
  `scripts/zitadel-apply-policies.py` (dry run by default, same values as the HelmRelease) applies the policies through the Admin
  API. The OpenBao value is the initial password, not the current one. Grafana: sessions 2 h idle / 12 h (were 7 / 30 days),
  request log as JSON; the SSO-only mode (local form hidden, strict role mapping with a `grafana-viewer` role) is the overlay
  `apps/base/observability-sso-only`, **off**. Addresses ANSSI PA-079 R10, R12, R21, R23, R29, PG-078, PA-012 R27, BP-028 R51 in
  part. Rung: **a real Zitadel v4.14.0 against PostgreSQL in containers** (`scripts/dev/test-zitadel-config.sh`, 31 checks, 1 min:
  the default admin logs in before and not after, the generated password is accepted, the policies land on a new instance and not
  on an old one, the apply script and the values agree, lockout at 5 and the PAT unlock, argon2id with and without the verifier
  (without it nobody logs in), JSON and access logs, no token in them) and a real Grafana 11.3.1 on the rendered ini; the
  bootstrap test (127 checks) covers the generated password. Not run on a cluster, with OpenBao, External Secrets or Istio.
  **Not done, on purpose:** the Vault and Grafana routes stay on the shared Gateway (removing them takes the Grafana UI away
  from operators; the load-balancer ACL is infrastructure), no password expiry (Zitadel's enforcement unchecked), no breached-password
  check, no per-audience Loki tenant, no export of authentication events to Loki (the access log says a login page was hit, not by
  whom), and the chart's `iam-admin` key and PAT stay valid to 2029 (they are the break-glass). Lab check: a fresh cluster's setup
  Job completes with the generated password, and the `zitadel-secret-config` ExternalSecret on an existing cluster before the key
  is seeded.

- **An inventory of what is deployed, a scan of it, and the image pins checked.** `scripts/dev/gen-sbom.py` writes a CycloneDX 1.5 file from
  the rendered DAG: the 15 Helm charts, 14 images from the manifests and, with `--helm`, about 38 images with those the charts render
  (`valuesFrom` targets get a placeholder). The new `SBOM` workflow runs weekly and on each release, attaches it as
  `openaether-apps-<tag>.cdx.json` and scans every image with Trivy (CRITICAL with a fix upstream fails; accepted findings go in
